
### Paper [1]
Title: Tuning La0.7Sr0.3MnO3 magnetic anisotropy via interfacial coupling in homometallic trilayers
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37736
Authors: Nunzia Coppola, Giovanni Vinai, Alksandr Yu Petrov, Chiara Sacco, Hafiz Sami Ur Rehman, Sofia Scarnicci, Lucia Capogna, Cinzia Di Giorgio, Carmela Aruta, Luigi Maritato, Alice Galdi
Abstract: In this work we explore the complex magnetic behaviour of SrMnO3/La0.7Sr0.3MnO3/SrMnO3 trilayers combining magneto-optic Kerr effect (MOKE) and magneto-resistance measurements. The interplay of structural and orbital reconstruction and the competition of ferromagnetism/antiferromagnetism at the interfaces is responsible of a variety of complex magnetic properties, highlighting the use of heterostructures as an effective strategy to tune and control magnetic behaviour. We demonstrate the possibility to tune the magnetic properties of a ferromagnetic La0.7Sr0.3MnO3 (LSMO) layer, embedded in two homometallic antiferromagnetic layers (SrMnO3, SMO), by properly varying the role of interfaces using two different LSMO thickness values. Spin reorientation of the LSMO magnetic easy axis away from its room temperature orientation was observed below 125 K, due to the predominance of exchange interaction at the interface over the substrate-induced magnetic anisotropy, in agreement with established models for homometallic manganite heterostructures. The competition of super-exchange and exchange interactions at the interface results in a highly frustrated interfacial spin glass state, resulting in 20-fold enhancement of the coercive field and exchange bias. The modification of the magnetocrystalline anisotropy driven by the interfacial effects leads to a change in the anisotropic magneto-resistance response of the system, which dominates the low temperature behaviour.

### Paper [2]
Title: A $B_{1g}$ phonon unlocks $d$-wave superconductivity in the repulsive Hubbard model
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37746
Authors: Wen O. Wang, Tianshu Li, Xiaoyue Ma, Rong Zhang, Lei Zhang, Shruti Agarwal, Benjamin Cohen-Stead, Emily Z. Zhang, Brian Moritz, Steven Johnston, Zhi-Xun Shen, Thomas P. Devereaux
Abstract: Using numerically exact finite-temperature quantum Monte Carlo, we demonstrate that a $B_{1g}$ phonon coupled to the doped $t$-$t'$ repulsive Hubbard model produces genuine $d$-wave superconductivity at a substantial transition temperature, in excess of either interaction alone. The observed trends suggest that stronger Hubbard repulsion may cooperate with the phonon to further raise the pairing scale. The enhancement persists at stronger repulsion within the accessible window, limited by the fermion sign problem, and follows a common trend across phonon frequencies $\Omega$ when organized by a retardation scale $2\pi T/\Omega$. We also find a well-defined superconducting dome in doping, echoing a characteristic feature of correlated superconductors. Our results identify the $B_{1g}$ phonon as a plausible microscopic ingredient for robust $d$-wave superconductivity in the Hubbard model and suggest a concrete, physically motivated route toward materials with higher superconducting transition temperatures.

### Paper [3]
Title: Approaching Landauer's limit in a single superconducting vortex memory bit
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37753
Authors: Nofar Fridman, Itai Keren, Avia Noah, Yishay Zur, Martin E. Huber, Nadav Katz, Roland Willa, Hadar Steinberg, Yonathan Anahory
Abstract: This work investigates the energy required to manipulate a memory bit using a superconducting vortex confined in a nanoscale double-well pinning potential. In the experiments, various double-well systems subjected to sinusoidal forces is explored, and the effects of both driving force and frequency is analyzed. Results reveal characteristic stochastic resonance behavior for weak drives and adiabatic manipulation at strong, low-frequency drives. Although our experimental method requires a steady sinusoidal drive incompatible with Landauer's erasure protocol, our numerical analysis demonstrates that the work required for such bit manipulation (erasure) is within an order of magnitude of Landauer's limit ($k_B T \ln 2$) at 4.2 K. These findings open possibilities for creating highly efficient memory elements that could be integrated with cryogenic quantum circuits or function as topological quantum computing qubits; thereby contributing to memory devices with lower energy consumption and mitigating the escalating global energy demand in the computing industry.

### Paper [4]
Title: Meta-Altermagnetism and Enhanced Spin-Splitting
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37754
Authors: Bogeng Wen, Hae-Young Kee
Abstract: Altermagnets are collinear magnets with zero net magnetization and spin-split electronic bands. Here, we explore whether altermagnetism can exhibit a discontinuous jump, a phenomenon we refer to as meta-altermagnetism. We show that meta-altermagnetism can emerge in 2D systems when magnetic-order-induced band reconstruction drives spin-split van Hove singularities (vHSs) across the Fermi level. Using mean-field theory for an extended $t$-$J$ model on a two-sublattice square lattice including anisotropic hopping integral, we find that a first-order meta-altermagnetic transition produces an intermediate altermagnetic regime with reduced staggered magnetization but strongly enhanced magnetic octupole and time-reversal-odd spin-splitter conductivity. We further show that the first-order transition line terminates at a quantum critical endpoint, giving rise to a rich phase diagram as the chemical potential and anisotropic hopping strength are varied. Our results identify the spin split vHSs as a route to enhancing spin-splitter effect for spintronic applications.

### Paper [5]
Title: MoireKP: An Automated Workflow for Symmetry-Constrained Moir\'e Continuum Models
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37760
Authors: Yan Zhang, Hanqi Pi, Wangqian Miao, Zhong Fang, Hongming Weng, Xi Dai, B. Andrei Bernevig, Quansheng Wu
Abstract: First-principles Hamiltonians contain the electronic information needed to study moir\'e materials, but their size makes subsequent calculations costly. We present MoireKP, an automated workflow for constructing compact continuum models from localized-orbital ab initio Hamiltonians. The workflow constructs a valley-resolved truncated atomic-plane-wave (TAPW) Hamiltonian, selects a low-energy basis, and projects the crystal-symmetry operations into this basis. These representations determine the symmetry-allowed Hamiltonian terms, whose coefficients are obtained by projecting the low-energy effective Hamiltonian onto the corresponding operator basis. The model dimension, localized reference states, and harmonic cutoff can be selected automatically or specified by the user. This Hamiltonian-based construction retains wave-function information within the selected low-energy subspace. We apply the workflow to AA-stacked twisted bilayer MoTe$_2$, AB-stacked twisted bilayer ZrS$_2$, and A--AB twisted trilayer MoTe$_2$, covering K, M, and $\Gamma$ valleys. All models reproduce TAPW band energies within 3.0 meV in the target windows along high-symmetry $k$ paths.

### Paper [6]
Title: Field theory of competing phases in the interacting Kitaev chain
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37761
Authors: Pradip Kattel, Thierry Giamarchi
Abstract: We study the interacting Kitaev chain with nearest-neighbor interactions and finite chemical potential using a combination of bosonization and density-matrix renormalization-group calculations. Focusing away from special exactly solvable lattice limits, we develop a continuum field-theoretic description of the generic finite-density interacting regime, where doping nonperturbatively modifies the competition between superconducting pairing and commensurate charge ordering. We show that the phase diagram consists of an intermediate incommensurate Luttinger liquid between the topological superconducting and commensurate charge-density-wave Mott phases, as well as a trivial band-insulating regime at large chemical potential. The commensurate--incommensurate and incommensurate--topological-superconducting phase boundaries are governed by Pokrovsky--Talapov and Berezinskii--Kosterlitz--Thouless criticality, respectively. We analyze the interacting topological superconducting regime and its boundary excitations. At the semiclassical level, and explicitly in the free-fermion boundary theory, the topological phase supports exponentially localized Majorana edge modes at interfaces between topological and trivial regions. We also construct a dual continuum fermionic representation in which this topological bulk sector is described instead by conventional symmetry breaking. This gives a continuum version of the SPT--SSB correspondence between different fermionic representations. Overall, our results provide an analytical description of one-dimensional interacting topological superconductors, from the competition between topology and commensurability and the resulting quantum criticality to the dual symmetry-breaking description of the topological phase.

### Paper [7]
Title: Long-Range Toric Code: Finite-Temperature Topological Order in Two Dimensions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37765
Authors: Jacopo Gliozzi, Taylor L. Hughes, Kyung-Su Kim
Abstract: We introduce two-dimensional toric code models with additional long-range loop interactions. Our models preserve the topologically ordered ground states of the ordinary toric code, while confining anyons at zero temperature. For sufficiently long-ranged interactions, we argue that this confinement persists at finite temperatures, strongly constraining anyon mobility and giving rise to finite-temperature topological order. This topological phase acts as a self-correcting quantum memory and exhibits long-range entanglement. For shorter-ranged interactions, signatures of topological order survive only up to an exponentially large length scale at finite temperature, yielding a partially self-correcting quantum memory.

### Paper [8]
Title: Spin Space Symmetry Without Spin Space Groups
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37770
Authors: Jeffrey G. Rau
Abstract: Spin space groups provide a complete symmetry classification of magnetic structures in the non-relativistic limit and have become an increasingly useful language for organizing magnetic phases and their physical properties. Each spin space group with a given parent space group is specified, up to spin-only symmetries, by a real representation of that space group acting in spin space. We argue that working directly with this representation not only avoids the construction and identification of the group, but also organizes and simplifies the resulting symmetry constraints on allowed responses. Determining the presence or absence of magnetic compensation, piezomagnetism, spin and charge transport, the Edelstein effect, and momentum-space spin textures becomes an ordinary problem in the representation theory of space groups. The resulting conditions reduce to overlaps between the three exterior powers of the defining representation and multipole representations of the point group. Several physically distinct responses then reduce to the same condition on the defining representation. For example, a $p$-wave spin texture and the time-even Edelstein effect are allowed under the same condition, as are piezomagnetism and a $d$-wave spin texture in compensated order. The wave-vector content of the exterior powers gives a further hierarchy of selection rules: depending on the relevant power, a uniform response requires one, two, or three ordering wave vectors to sum to a reciprocal lattice vector. These rules can exclude many responses before point-group symmetry is considered. We illustrate the approach with examples drawn from a wide variety of materials hosting collinear, coplanar and non-coplanar magnetic order. Finally, we discuss extensions of these results to magnetic space groups, connections to Landau theories of collinear order, and the characterization of odd-parity magnets.

### Paper [9]
Title: Evidence for a charged vortex lattice in an exciton superfluid
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37781
Authors: Ryan L. Lee, Jungwoo Lee, Viliam Va\v{n}o, Bo Zou, Cheng-Li Chiu, Kenji Watanabe, Takashi Taniguchi, Allan MacDonald, Ali Yazdani
Abstract: Lattices of quantized vortices are a defining property of rotating superfluids and of type-II superconductors in magnetic fields. Realizing an analogous vortex lattice in an exciton superfluid-formed from bound electron-hole pairs-has remained challenging because excitons are electrically neutral. Building on previous theoretical studies of the low-energy excitations in an exciton superfluid, recent theory has predicted that in a strong magnetic field, a weakly charged exciton superfluid can stabilize a vortex-antivortex lattice arising from the mutual repulsion of fractionally charged vortex cores. Here we report evidence for this predicted vortex lattice in a bilayer exciton condensate by imaging its charged vortices with the scanning tunnelling microscope. The condensate forms between Landau levels of two graphene layers separated by a thin hexagonal boron nitride barrier in a perpendicular magnetic field. We find that the vortex lattice periodicity scales with the total excess charge and is insensitive to its distribution between the two layers, in quantitative agreement with theoretical predictions. Our observations provide evidence for phase coherence in an exciton superfluid system.

### Paper [10]
Title: Parity-Resolved Many-Body Kinetics of a Topological Qubit under Nonequilibrium Phonon Pulses
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37793
Authors: Han Huang
Abstract: Phonon bursts from ionizing radiation cause correlated errors in superconducting quantum processors. We study prescribed nonequilibrium phonon pulses in a Majorana qubit formed by two $2\,\mu\mathrm{m}$ proximitized nanowires. Combining the lattice Lutchyn--Oreg model, microscopic electron--phonon coupling, and parity-resolved many-body dynamics, we track excitation, relaxation, and logical coherence. Without quasiparticle escape, recombination restores the initial Majorana occupations in about 130 ns, while phonons exchanged during excitation and recovery can retain logical-state information, leaving permanent coherence loss. For mirror-symmetric wires, the fractional coherence loss per wire is 0.75--1.0 times the excitation probability averaged over the two logical states, even at zero Majorana splitting; breaking mirror symmetry can suppress dephasing at small splitting. Factorized Boltzmann kinetics neglects quasiparticle correlations and overestimates residual parity flips. At equal deposited energy density, phonon spectra yield different excitation probabilities and coherence losses. Comparisons with Kitaev chains matched to the wire's gap and Majorana decay length reveal that low electron density suppresses excitation by energetic phonons, whereas hard-wall boundaries in a high-density control increase excitation efficiency by a factor of $5\times10^4$ relative to the wire for pulses centered at 1 meV. Magnetic-field and chemical-potential tuning yield different phonon sensitivities at the same bulk gap through changes in spin and electron--hole structure. Including hybrid states' semiconductor weights reduces near-threshold excitation rates fourfold when the tunnel coupling equals the parent superconducting gap. Our framework connects phonon spectra to leakage, poisoning, and coherence loss to guide Majorana-device design.

### Paper [11]
Title: Geometry-controlled active transport in smectic textures
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37812
Authors: Yhony M. Arce, William G. C. Oropesa, Alef R. Siqueira, Hartmut L\"owen, Andr\'e P. Vieira, Danilo B. Liarte
Abstract: Self-propelled particles moving through structured anisotropic media such as smectic liquid crystals exhibit transport properties that differ qualitatively from those in isotropic environments. Here we introduce a minimal model of an active Brownian particle whose propulsion axis is coupled to the layer-normal field of a smectic liquid crystal. Using numerical simulations complemented by approximate analytical calculations, we investigate the dynamics of an active particle in three representative two-dimensional smectic microstructures: a uniform planar texture, an isolated toroidal focal conic domain, and a periodic array of toroidal focal conic domains. For the planar and isolated focal-conic geometries, we characterize the crossover from short-time superdiffusion to long-time normal diffusion. In periodic focal-conic arrays, however, transport is controlled by two distinct persistence mechanisms whose relative importance depends on the P\'eclet number ($\mathrm{Pe}$) and the coupling strength $\varepsilon$ between the particle orientation and the smectic layer-normal field. At small $\varepsilon$, long-time diffusion is governed by the intrinsic persistence time of the active Brownian dynamics. At large $\varepsilon$, repeated scattering by smectic defects generates an emergent persistence time that dominates the transport. These results demonstrate that the competition between the intrinsic persistence of active motion and the mean free time between encounters with smectic defects provides a simple physical mechanism for the emergence of nontrivial diffusive behavior in structured anisotropic media.

### Paper [12]
Title: G\ae{}niceN: a minimal model to relax magnetization states self-consistently in general artificial spin ices
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37889
Authors: H. Braun, E. Freeman, G. Alatteili, E. Iacocca
Abstract: Artificial spin ices are geometric arrangement of nanomagnets that exhibit reconfigurable magnetization states and GHz dynamics. Their investigation in the GHz regime is challenging because of the existence of multiple modes and the impact of magnetization deviations at the edges of the nanomagnets. A minimal model to capture such dynamics require three macrospins per nanomagnet, an approach that was implemented for generic artificial spin ices in the tool Gaenice. Here, we use the Gaenice framework to realize a relaxation method to stabilize magnetization states self-consistently, called GaeniceN. We implement the dissipative component of the Landau-Lifshitz equation in an iterative approach to efficiently converge to the relaxed state. As an example, we find our approach to correctly predict the transition between phases in a pyramid ASI subject to a low-magnitude external field by computing the system's average energy. Because they are rooted on the same framework, GaeniceN produces the initial condition needed for Gaenice to compute dynamics such as ferromagnetic resonance or band structures. Combined, these tools will be able to aid in the design of artificial spin ices with applications in magnonics.

### Paper [13]
Title: Planar energetics do not determine twin persistence in nanocrystalline aluminium
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37925
Authors: Yue Wu, Yuan Tian, Haoyu Wan, Deng Pan, Mingwei Chen
Abstract: Planar energetics are the standard descriptors of twinnability in face-centred cubic metals and the usual validation targets for interatomic potentials. Here we show that they do not determine whether deformation twins persist in nanocrystalline aluminium. Two machine-learned potentials reproducing the same zero-load slip curves and closely spaced layered energy surfaces predict, from a common separated-fault precursor under identical loading, retained twin coverage of 64\% and 14\%. The divergence traces to forces within distorted partial cores acting along the trailing translation that would remove an outer fault. Planar energetics do set the margin: an outer fault more than halves the incremental barrier for middle-plane shear, yet twin completion and fault removal remain separated by only 10--21 mJ m\textsuperscript{-2}. Whether an assembled twin persists is decided instead by the orientation of local traction relative to the full-slip bisector and by the arrival order of partial-slip fronts from the grain-boundary network.

### Paper [14]
Title: Anomalous Hall Conductivity as an Intrinsic Property of a Tight-Binding Graph
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37939
Authors: E. S. Andriyakhina, G. Lemut, P. W. Brouwer
Abstract: In a ballistic system, the anomalous dc Hall response generically depends on microscopic details, including the geometric embedding of the orbitals and the precise implementation of the measurement. Here, we consider the question under what circumstances the anomalous Hall conductivity can be regarded as an intrinsic property of a system. We show that the full dc Hall response $\sigma_{xy}^{\rm H}$ becomes embedding-independent in the presence of relaxation by impurity scattering, although the individual (intrinsic) band and (extrinsic) side-jump and anomalous-distribution contributions to the Hall conductivity separately remain embedding-dependent. We further derive a symmetry criterion for when the band term itself becomes embedding-independent, but argue that this alone neither identifies it with the full Hall conductivity $\sigma_{xy}^{\rm H}$, nor makes it a reliable approximation for $\sigma_{xy}^{\rm H}$. More broadly, our results establish embedding dependence as a diagnostic of whether a response coefficient represents a stationary intrinsic property of a material or retains sensitivity to measurement protocol.

### Paper [15]
Title: Few-Electron Partitioning in the Fractional Quantum Hall Regime
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37950
Authors: T. Jonckheere, J. Rech, B. Gr\'emaud
Abstract: Motivated by few-electron partitioning experiments [Shaju et al., Nature 642, 928 (2025)], we study the partition statistics of $N$-electron states at a fractional quantum Hall point contact. At the solvable point $g=1/2$, we obtain nonperturbative current and noise formulas for arbitrary drives, while for Lorentzian pulses (Levitons) the full counting statistics reduces to an $N\times N$ determinant. We show that the charge is transferred solely in units of $e$, and that symmetrized cumulants for $N=2,3,4$ reveal strong antibunching and higher-order correlations, observable in a $(\nu_1,\nu_2)=(1,1/3)$ junction.

### Paper [16]
Title: Analytical framework for interpreting thermoreflectance measurements of nanoline gratings under finite spot illumination
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37957
Authors: Abhishek Pathak (Department of Mechanical Engineering, Carnegie Mellon University), Seungwon Park (Department of Mechanical Engineering, Carnegie Mellon University), Shubham Deshmukh (Department of Mechanical Engineering, Carnegie Mellon University), Alan J. H. McGaughey (Department of Mechanical Engineering, Carnegie Mellon University), Jonathan A. Malen (Department of Mechanical Engineering, Carnegie Mellon University)
Abstract: A frequency-domain thermoreflectance (FDTR)-based technique is presented to measure the effective thermal properties of a material stack whose surface is patterned with metallic nanoline gratings that are heated and probed by lasers. An analytical heat diffusion model is derived for predicting the FDTR phase response that incorporates a finite laser spot radius with a Gaussian intensity profile for the pump and probe lasers, as well as the dimensions of the nanoline grating. The model is validated by comparing with measurements of Au nanoline gratings, with widths ranging from 100 to 1000 nm, fabricated on a-SiO$_2$ and $c$-sapphire substrates. Due to the short phonon mean free paths (MFPs) in these substrates, thermal transport remains in the diffusive regime for the whole range of nanoline widths. The model and the measurement show excellent agreement across different laser spot radii and nanoline widths, with a bulk thermal conductivity value of $c$-sapphire and the thermal boundary conductances at the Au-Cr/a-SiO$_2$ and Au-Cr/$c$-sapphire interfaces comparable to our prior measurements and the literature. The validated model will serve as a robust baseline for extending this method to measure effective thermal conductivity as a function of the heater size. This approach is particularly relevant for materials where non-diffusive thermal transport regimes enable probing phonon MFP dependent contributions to the thermal conductivity.

### Paper [17]
Title: Electronic-morphological decoupling in mixed-dimensional halide perovskite heterostructures
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37964
Authors: Mahmoud Samadpour, Alireza Fathi Beiraghvandi, Kazem Meraji, Tristan Sachsenweger Ballantyne, Fuxiang Ji, Mahdi Mohammadi, Nasim Kabir, Wolfgang Tress
Abstract: Surface morphology is widely considered an indicator of thin-film quality, with smoother films generally expected to exhibit more uniform optoelectronic properties. Here, we demonstrate that substantial surface evolution does not necessarily lead to increased electronic heterogeneity in mixed-dimensional perovskite heterostructures. We investigate how low-dimensional Ruddlesden-Popper capping layers derived from the sterically hindered 5-tert-butyl-phenanthridinium cation affect the spatial distributions of photoluminescence (PL) characteristics and quasi-Fermi level splitting (QFLS) in 3D perovskites. By combining statistical atomic force microscopy (AFM) analysis with confocal, spatially resolved PL spectroscopy, we correlate surface topography with microscale optoelectronic properties.
  Although capping increases surface roughness and height asymmetry, it does not increase electronic inhomogeneity. Instead, the heterostructures exhibit enhanced PL, reduced non-radiative recombination, and improved spatial uniformity of radiative QFLS compared with pristine 3D films. Under accelerated aging, this contrast becomes more pronounced: pristine films develop substantial spatial fluctuations in emission and QFLS, whereas capped heterostructures largely preserve their optoelectronic homogeneity. These findings indicate that the low-dimensional capping layer suppresses aging-induced optoelectronic disorder, demonstrating that electronic uniformity can be maintained despite morphological irregularity. This decoupling provides a design principle for developing durable, high-performance perovskite photovoltaics.

### Paper [18]
Title: Finite-step statistical mechanics of scale-invariant learning: spectral equations of state and curvature transport
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38012
Authors: Alexander Itin
Abstract: Stationary learning admits an ideal-gas description under restrictive noise assumptions. In neural networks whose gradient noise departs from these assumptions, however, an entropy-scaling relation resembling a Maxwell relation remains approximately valid. We ask which thermodynamic-looking relations follow from scale symmetry rather than equilibrium, and how departures from the ideal-gas description can be quantified, for discrete stochastic gradient dynamics with memory. An exact rescaling of trajectories explains the entropy relation without assuming thermal equilibrium. Within a local harmonic approximation, self-adjusting weight norms yield a spectral equation of state whose virial coefficients are set by moments of the curvature spectrum, and coupled parameter groups can sustain stationary probability currents. Our central result beyond this approximation is an exact curvature-transport identity. Together with a finite-step loss balance, it decomposes corrections to the harmonic fluctuation relation into measurable curvature changes, finite-step remainders, gradient-memory correlations and finite-window drift terms. A related displacement-weighted curvature change provides a diagnostic of the arrow of time; its mean and second moment give a lower bound on the relative entropy between forward and reversed state pairs. We also calculate the activation-boundary contribution to curvature for rectified teacher-student networks with Gaussian inputs. Tests in sphere models, teacher-student networks and convolutional networks on CIFAR-10 and CIFAR-100 support the transport description and identify limits of the harmonic and cubic-remainder approximations. These results separate symmetry-based relations from equilibrium assumptions and provide quantitative diagnostics of nonideal fluctuations in learning dynamics.

### Paper [19]
Title: From a Lifshitz Transition to Spin-Density-Wave Order: Sr$_2$RuO$_4$ under Uniaxial Strain
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38025
Authors: Evgeny A. Stepanov, Sophie Beck, Maria Chatzieleftheriou, Antoine Georges
Abstract: In recent years, the effects of uniaxial strain on Sr$_2$RuO$_4$ have been extensively investigated, revealing a pronounced enhancement of the superconducting critical temperature accompanied by experimental indications of an emergent magnetically ordered state. In this Letter, we study the impact of strain on the spectral and magnetic properties of this material within an advanced many-body theoretical framework. We find that strain induces an anisotropic Lifshitz transition, which enhances the electron scattering rate along the elongation axis while simultaneously promoting coherent Fermi-liquid behavior in the perpendicular direction, in close agreement with experimental observations. Taking into account this anisotropic behavior allows us to capture the magnetic instability leading to a finite-temperature ordered state at large strain values. Our analysis further reveals that the magnetic response is dominated by two competing wave vectors associated with distinct nesting vectors: one within the $\gamma$ sheet and the other between the $\alpha$ and $\beta$ sheets of the Fermi surface. Both wave vectors can stabilize magnetic order, with their competition depending sensitively on the coherence of the respective Fermi surface sheets.

### Paper [20]
Title: Coherent control and high purity single-photon emission from telecom quantum dots on metamorphic InGaAs
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38060
Authors: Beatrice Costa, Bianca Scaparra, Hubert Riedl, Gregor Koblm\"uller, Eugenio Zallo, Jonathan J. Finley, Lukas Hanschke, Kai M\"uller
Abstract: Semiconductor quantum dots emitting in the telecommunication O- and C-bands are promising candidates as single-photon sources for fiber-based quantum communication. In this work, we show how coherent quasi-resonant and phonon-assisted excitation help reveal the intrinsic optical properties of InAs quantum dots embedded in relaxed InGaAs matrices on top of metamorphic InGaAs layers for achieving emission in the telecom bands. By systematically comparing excitation schemes, we separate excitation-induced effects from intrinsic emitter properties. This allows us to observe linewidths as narrow as 21 $\mu$eV, the disappearance of slow decay channels, and single-photon purity reaching second-order autocorrelation values in the order of $10^{-2}$. Photoluminescence excitation reveals higher excited states, enabling coherent control of trion transitions and the observation of Rabi-like oscillations in both telecom bands. These results confirm the promising potential of these quantum dots for applications in quantum technologies.

### Paper [21]
Title: Thermal laser evaporation of superconducting Nb films
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38070
Authors: Yifei Yan, David S. Catherall, Finley B. Donachie, Dorsa Najafipanah, Austin J. Minnich
Abstract: Thermal laser evaporation (TLE) is an emerging physical vapor deposition (PVD) technique that uses continuous-wave lasers to evaporate even the most refractory elements. The deposition and characterization of superconducting refractory elements are of interest to assess the quality of films deposited by TLE. Here, we report the deposition of niobium (Nb) on c-plane sapphire using a home-built TLE system equipped with a 1070 nm continuous-wave fiber laser. A deposition rate of 0.19 \r{A}/s was achieved with 533 W of laser power focused to a diameter of approximately 500 $\mu$m on a Nb target. A film with a thickness of $22.47 \pm 0.05$ nm and a root-mean-square (RMS) roughness of $0.23 \pm 0.02$ nm exhibited a room-temperature resistivity of $21.60 \pm 0.05$ $\mu\Omega\mathrm{cm}$ and a residual-resistivity ratio (RRR) of 5.71. The superconducting transition temperature, $T_\mathrm{c}$, of the film was measured as 9.24 K, only 10 mK below the bulk value, with a transition width of 29 mK. Among films deposited without substrate heating, the electrical and superconducting properties of the present films meet or exceed those deposited by other techniques. This work contributes to the emerging consensus regarding TLE as a versatile PVD method for refractory elements and compounds.

### Paper [22]
Title: Emergent higher-order optical nonlinearity in self-assembled polar supertextures
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38138
Authors: Daniel A. Bustamante Lopez, Deepankar Sri Gyan, Xiaojiang Li, Vladimir A. Stoica, Sujit Das, Peter Meisenheimer, Akash Surampalli, Mohit Tanwani, Xianghan Xu, Aiden Ross, Long-Qing Chen, Sang-Wook Cheong, Lane W. Martin, Ramamoorthy Ramesh, Venkatraman Gopalan, Wanzheng Hu
Abstract: Giant optical nonlinearities enable advanced materials control and bridge exotic light-matter phenomena to next-generation photonics. However, materials exhibiting strong higher-order optical nonlinearity beyond the second order are typically rare. Here, we report the discovery of a giant and ultrafast second-harmonic generation (SHG) in self-assembled polar skyrmions under mid-infrared excitations. The transient SHG intensity exhibits a distinctive power dependence originating from higher-order nonlinearity. More generally, we show that large higher-order nonlinearity emerges across diverse domain structures in polar superlattices. Phase-field simulations demonstrate a strong and periodic local permittivity variation within each polar texture at equilibrium, and further confirm the fast polarization oscillation in polar skyrmions with mid-infrared excitations. This intense local permittivity modulation can satisfy epsilon-near-zero conditions, unlocking dramatically enhanced nonlinear optical responses without photonic engineering. Consequently, the self-assembled polar topologies with strong inherent optical nonlinearity could establish a platform for quantum information processing and high-speed optical communications.

### Paper [23]
Title: Incoherent dark excitons extend Floquet-like physics across time and momentum
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38156
Authors: Xing Zhu, Jacques Hawecker, Enrico Perfetto, Vivek Pareek, David R. Bacon, Harley Suchiang, Nanami Tomoda, Amrita Mitra, Takumi Fukuda, Sebastien Roux, Ouri Karni, Jack Featherstone, Kenji Watanabe, Takashi Taniguchi, Michael K. L. Man, Julien Mad\'eo, Felipe H. da Jornada, Gianluca Stefanucci, Keshav M. Dani
Abstract: Floquet phenomena - where a coherent, time-periodic drive hybridizes the conduction band with energy-replicated copies of the valence band - have been demonstrated with intense optical fields and, more recently, other bosonic excitations. However, within mean-field approximations and the current understanding of Floquet phenomena, these effects survive only for the coherence time of the drive, limiting practical use and current demonstrations to the sub-picosecond regime. Here, consistent with recent theoretical predictions beyond the mean-field picture, using time- and angle-resolved photoemission spectroscopy in WSe$_2$/MoS$_2$ heterostructures, we show that incoherent, dense populations of dark interlayer excitons hybridize the conduction band with energy-replicated copies of the valence band, mimicking Floquet-like phenomena - now persisting for nearly a nanosecond. With increasing interlayer exciton density, the valence band and its replica evolve from an inverted-parabolic to a Mexican-hat dispersion, a signature of the predicted non-zero static coupling that hybridizes the bands and scales linearly with the exciton density. By tuning twist angle, we further show that dark excitons with net momentum create replicas that hybridize with bands offset not just in energy but also in momentum, providing another powerful degree of freedom beyond conventional Floquet engineering. These results establish a robust, long-lived route to engineering electronic states, beyond a coherent, zero-momentum drive.

### Paper [24]
Title: Level ordering in the linear $E \otimes e$ dynamic Jahn-Teller model
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38190
Authors: Naoya Iwahara
Abstract: The ground level of the linear $E\otimes e$ dynamic Jahn-Teller model has long been believed to be the doublet with vibronic angular momentum $j = \pm\tfrac12$. We prove that, for arbitrary coupling strength, the $n$th level $E_{nj}$ in the subspace of fixed $j$ strictly increases with $|j|$ for every $n$, $E_{n,1/2} < E_{n,3/2} < E_{n,5/2} < \cdots$; in particular, the ground level is always the $j = \pm\tfrac12$ doublet. The proof uses only a unitary transformation that lowers $j$ by one, combined with the variational principle. We also give an explicit lower bound on the gap $E_{0,j+1} - E_{0j}$.

### Paper [25]
Title: Crystoria: A Large-Scale Crystal Dataset Linking Thermodynamic and Dynamical Stability for Materials AI
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38195
Authors: Ke-Xin Pang, Ze-Feng Gao, Yan Gao, Zhong-Yi Lu
Abstract: Large-scale crystal databases have transformed materials discovery by making millions of structures and thermodynamic properties accessible to materials informatics. Yet dynamical stability remains sparsely characterized at a comparable scale because its evaluation is substantially more computationally demanding than conventional structure and energy calculations. This mismatch creates a critical gap between the rapidly expanding crystal-structure space and the physical information available for materials research and machine learning. Here we introduce Crystoria, a large-scale crystal dataset designed to bring dynamical stability to the scale of modern materials data. We construct Crystoria by systematically curating low-energy and metastable inorganic crystals, standardizing their structural identities, and combining machine-learning-accelerated structural relaxation with phonon-based dynamical screening. The resulting dataset contains 2,649,640 crystal structures, spanning 89 elements, 219 of the 230 space groups, and primitive cells containing 1-48 atoms. Among them, 1,793,353 structures (67.68%) are classified as dynamically stable, revealing substantial variation in dynamical stability across chemical and crystallographic space. By bringing dynamical stability to the scale of millions of crystal structures, Crystoria provides a physically grounded foundation for materials artificial intelligence (AI) and enables systematic exploration of the dynamical crystal landscape.

### Paper [26]
Title: Influences of $N_2$ flow rate on the superconducting properties and dimensionality of epitaxial TiN thin films
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38250
Authors: Shilong Chen, Yuchuan Liu, Yihui Sun, Xiaoni Wang, Wei Peng, Yu Wu, Gang Mu, Zhirong Lin
Abstract: TiN thin films have received great attention in the field of superconducting quantum computing due to their low loss characteristics derived from stable chemical and thermal properties, as well as excellent electrical conductivity. In this study, TiN thin films with a thickness of 35 nm were epitaxially grown on MgO substrates using the DC reactive magnetron sputtering technique. The crystal structure, film thickness, and surface morphology of the thin films were characterized. The films exhibit a very flat surface with a root mean square roughness of only a few tenths of a nanometer. The influences of nitrogen ($N_2$) flow rate on the surface morphology, superconducting transition temperature ($T_c$), residual resistivity ratio (RRR), normal-state resistivity ($\rho_n$), upper critical field, and superconducting dimensionality were investigated systematically. The experimental results show that the TiN film deposited with a $N_2$ flow rate of 1.5 sccm exhibits optimal performance, including the highest $T_c$ (4.93 K), the largest RRR, the lowest $\rho_n$, and the lowest slope of the out-of-film upper critical field near Tc. Moreover, it is found that the background vacuum inside the growth chamber has a significant impact on the superconducting properties of TiN films, and the degradation of vacuum degree can lead to the decrease of $T_c$. The upper critical field reveals a significant anisotropic feature. Importantly, the in-film upper critical field data demonstrates that the optimization of sample quality leads to deviation from two-dimensional superconducting behavior, which provides a new perspective on the influencing factors of superconducting dimensionality beyond the thickness of films. The present results provide useful references for a comprehensive understanding of the relationship between growth parameters and the superconducting properties of thin films.

### Paper [27]
Title: Charge Filling-Dependent Valley Polarization and its Dynamics Driven by Correlated Interlayer Exciton States in WSe2/WS2 Heterobilayer
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38251
Authors: Zhiwei Li, Haonan Wang, Yubei Xiang, Wataru Idehara, Hailin Liu, Shinichiro Matano, Kenji Watanabe, Takashi Taniguchi, Kazunari Matsuda
Abstract: Moir\'e heterostructures of transition metal dichalcogenides provide an emerging platform for studying the strongly correlated electronic and excitonic states. The valley degree of freedom and its dynamics in the strongly correlated electronic and excitonic states in the moir\'e system are crucial for fundamental understanding and applications of valleytronics. Although the strongly correlated states in moir\'e systems have been extensively investigated, their impact on valley degrees of freedom and its dynamics remains unexplored. Here, we study the charge filling-dependent valley relaxation dynamics of interlayer excitons (IXs) in WSe2/WS2 moir\'e heterobilayer system. The evolution of moir\'e IXs dynamics across the Mott transition is elucidated by combining time-resolved optical spectroscopy with Monte Carlo simulation. Our results show that the radiative decay and recombination rate of IXs are enhanced at the integer filling factors n (=-1). Moreover, the valley relaxation rate of IXs at n=$\pm$1 is significantly decreased. The resulting reduction in wavefunction overlap and phonon scattering process suppresses the nonradiative recombination and intervalley scattering of IXs. These anomalous behaviors are attributed to the modification of IXs recombination pathways and enhance excitonic coherence due to the localization of introduced charge carriers at integer filling of moir\'e site. Our results explore exciton and valley dynamics as sensitive probes of strongly correlated states in moir\'e systems and highlight possible ways to control excitonic coherence and valleytronic properties in two-dimensional quantum materials.

### Paper [28]
Title: Finite-Temperature Magnetism and Longitudinal Spin Transport in Compensated Half-Metal Ferrimagnets with Near-Zero Magnetization
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38302
Authors: Jiahao Shentu, Jie Li, Jianting Dong, Meng Zhu, Chen Wang, Qing Zhang, Yizhuo Song, Wenqi Sun, Jia Zhang
Abstract: Fully compensated ferrimagnets, characterized by nearly vanishing magnetic moments at zero temperature, perfect spin-polarized electronic structures, and ultrafast spin dynamics, represent a highly promising materials platform for next-generation spintronic devices. By minimizing stray magnetic fields and reducing switching currents, FCFs enable a viable path toward high-speed, low-power spintronic devices. In this work, we present a systematic investigation of the electronic structure, finite-temperature magnetism, and spin-dependent transport properties of the Heusler-type fully compensated half-metallic ferrimagnet Mn3Al in magnetic tunnel junctions. We demonstrate that Mn3Al is a half-metallic FCF with 100% spin polarization and fully compensated magnetization at 0 K. Combining density functional theory with Monte Carlo simulations, we characterize its finite-temperature magnetic properties and show that Mn3Al exhibits a net magnetization below 11.6 emu/cc at 300 K, confirming the robustness of its nearly compensated ferrimagnetic order at room temperature. Furthermore, we predict that the Mn3Al/MgO/Mn3Al MTJ yields a giant tunneling magnetoresistance effect at 0 K, which remains as high as 232% at 300 K. Our results establish Mn3Al and related half-metallic FCFs as excellent candidates for magnetoresistance-based spintronic applications.

### Paper [29]
Title: The Flexoelectric Twist: Persistence of Heavy Fermion Picture in Small-Angle Twisted Bilayer Graphene
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38305
Authors: Qirong Yao, Ka Cheuk Fan, Tianyu Qiao, Hao Shi, Xi Dai, Berthold J\"ack
Abstract: Twisted bilayer graphene (TBG) near the first magic angle ($\theta\approx1.05^{\circ}$) hosts flat bands that provide a fertile ground for exploring strongly correlated and topological phases. At smaller twist angles ($\theta<1^{\circ}$), attice relaxation profoundly alters the moir\'e landscape. The extent to which moir\'e lattice reconstruction at different length scales affects the electronic structure through electro-mechanical coupling remains unresolved. Here, using scanning tunneling microscopy on small-angle TBG devices ($\theta\approx0.3-0.7^{\circ}$), we observe a pronounced $dI/dV$ peak at the AA site of the moir\'e lattice on top of a spectrum that is asymmetric about the charge neutrality point, lattice reconstruction over moir\'e lattice length scales, as well as a dilation of the atomic lattice at the AA sites. We demonstrate at a phenomenological level that the inclusion of moir\'e lattice reconstruction supports compact localized heavy fermion $f-$ states localized at AA sites. The additional inclusion of atomic lattice dilation enerates a strong flexo-electric response that explicitly breaks particle-hole symmetry, pushing compact, localized $f$-states at the AA sites down in energy. Consequently, these heavy fermion states energetically coexist with itinerant $c$-electrons at the AB/BA sites. Our spatially resolved spectroscopic measurements quantitatively visualize this coexistence, confirming the persistence of the heavy fermion model in the small-angle regime $\theta<1^{\circ}$. Our findings provide microscopic insights into how flexo-electricity affects the low-energy electronic structure of small-angle TBG. More broadly, our study establishes small-angle TBG as a new class of moir\'e materials to explore the interacting Kondo lattice problem with high tunability of the $f-$occupation and $c-$density through twist-angle and electrostatic back gates, respectively.

### Paper [30]
Title: First-Principles Identification of Higher-Order Configurational Energy Terms and Their Magnetic-State Dependence in Lithium-Rich Oxyfluorides: From QUBO to HUBO
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38321
Authors: Joonho Bae
Abstract: In this work, we use spin-polarized density functional theory (DFT) to identify substantial third-order, nonadditive configurational energy contributions in lithium-rich oxyfluorides that quadratic unconstrained binary optimization (QUBO) models cannot represent. We test the extension from QUBO to higher-order unconstrained binary optimization (HUBO) in two disordered-rocksalt cathode materials, Li$_2$MnO$_2$F (LMOF) and Li$_2$Mn$_{0.5}$Ti$_{0.5}$O$_2$F (LMTiOF), using composition-preserving DFT energy maps. Using fixed-geometry energy maps, we show that cubic HUBO captures nonadditive contributions absent from QUBO models and substantially reduces fitting errors in both materials. Direct DFT energy differences confirm third-order nonadditivity, while comparisons of magnetic initializations reveal a strong dependence of the LMTiOF contribution on the converged electronic state. These results support the inclusion of higher-order terms when modeling configurational energetics and highlight the need to account for magnetic-state dependence in constructing DFT energy maps. The extracted terms describe effective couplings among composition-preserving exchange variables, rather than uniquely identified microscopic three-atom interactions.

### Paper [31]
Title: Time evolution in the multi-component dynamical Keldysh models
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38338
Authors: M. N. Kiselev
Abstract: We construct a time evolution operator for a dynamical quantum models with a multi-component random Gaussian field characterized by a long correlation time. We analyze three paradigmatic cases: non-Abelian $SU(2)$ symmetry class for the three-component Gaussian noise and Abelian symmetry classes $U(1)$ and $Z_2$ for the two- and one-component noises correspondingly. Starting with a limiting case of the infinite correlation time (Keldysh model) we shown that statistics of the two-component random field with a non-zero mean is described by the Rice distribution. We derived and solved the system of coupled linear differential equations for the exact diagonal and off-diagonal single-particle Green's functions. We showed how the finite time correlation in Keldysh models are accounted by a continuous fractions method.

### Paper [32]
Title: Structural Determination vs Stochastic Events: Relaxation Dynamics in a Supercooled Liquid
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38375
Authors: Shivam Mahajan, Peter Harrowell, Massimo Pica Ciamarra
Abstract: Structural relaxation in supercooled liquids is influenced by the initial configuration, yet its microscopic evolution exhibits substantial variability. Here, we investigate the interplay between structural constraints and dynamical stochasticity by examining fluctuations of relaxation dynamics across and within isoconfigurational ensembles. We show that fluctuations among trajectories originating from the same configuration constitute a substantial fraction of the total dynamical fluctuations over a broad range of temperatures. At the collective level, the distribution of relaxation times is well described by a stochastic model involving a sequence of effective relaxation events, whose number decreases upon cooling. At the microscopic level, dynamical fluctuations exhibit spatial correlations that grow upon cooling, revealing a spatial organization of relaxation even when the initial configuration is fixed. Remarkably, the characteristic length associated with these fluctuations closely follows the length scale inferred from the stochastic first-passage model, establishing a connection between the spatial organization of dynamical fluctuations and the temporal statistics of collective relaxation. Together, these results suggest that structural relaxation is governed by an interplay between configuration-dependent constraints and stochastic dynamical events: the initial structure influences when and where relaxation occurs, without uniquely determining its microscopic realization.

### Paper [33]
Title: Strain-overlap and surface-confinement engineering in AlScN/YIG magnetoacoustic devices
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38399
Authors: Kevin K\"unstle, Kaya Gauch, Agn\.e \v{Z}ukauskait\.e, Stephan Barth, Mathias Weiler
Abstract: We establish AlScN/YIG/GGG as an integrated piezoelectric-magnetic heterostructure for electrically generating surface acoustic waves (SAWs) and coupling them to spin waves in monocrystalline YIG. Combining electrical transmission measurements of interdigital-transducer delay lines with finite-element simulations, we identify the excited modes as Rayleigh-type SAWs whose surface confinement varies strongly with AlScN thickness. The resulting changes in the strain field within YIG directly modify the magnetoelastic coupling. Using the simulated complex strain components and an effective-driving-field model, we calculate the relative SAW attenuation for several AlScN thicknesses and wavelengths. The calculations qualitatively reproduce the measurements: at all investigated wavelengths, thinner AlScN layers yield stronger relative attenuation. Experimentally, the attenuation approaches 70dB/mm for a 54.5nm-thick AlScN layer at a wavelength of 1.2$\mu$m. However, the strongest relative attenuation does not maximize the detectable signal. Instead, the absolute field-induced transmission change peaks at an AlScN thickness of approximately 100nm, with the precise optimum depending on the acoustic wavelength. These results identify the AlScN thickness as a key design parameter for balancing magnetoelastic attenuation and pure SAW transmission in AlScN/YIG devices.

### Paper [34]
Title: Direct real-space imaging of sign-reversal superconducting state by scanning Josephson tunneling
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38421
Authors: Tianheng Wei, Yanzhao Liu, Wei Ren, Zhen Liang, Ziqiang Wang, Jian Wang
Abstract: Phase-sensitive experiments based on Josephson tunneling have been considered as a pivotal method to determine the pairing symmetry in high-temperature (high-Tc) cuprate superconductors. However, in iron-based superconductors, traditional phase-sensitive experiments face setbacks because of the complexity of pairing symmetry in multiband systems. Here, using scanning Josephson tunneling microscopy (SJTM), we report the half-unit-cell periodic Josephson tunneling modulations in monolayer high-Tc Fe(Te,Se) film epitaxially grown on SrTiO3(001). The Josephson tunneling almost vanishes along the lines connecting next-nearest-neighbor Fe atoms under an s-wave superconducting tip. Combined with single-particle tunneling spectra showing the full superconducting gap, our experimental results are consistent with the sign-reversal nodeless d-wave pairing symmetry. Our findings provide sub-unit-cell scale superconducting phase information for understanding the mechanism of high-Tc superconductivity. We anticipate our work as a starting point for atomically resolved Josephson interference experiments using SJTM in various unconventional superconductors.

### Paper [35]
Title: Layer-Parity Control of the Superconducting Diode Effect in Monolayer NbSe$_2$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38449
Authors: Yusuf Wicaksono, Toshikaze Kariyado
Abstract: The superconducting diode effect is often associated with finite-momentum pairing, although model studies indicate that rectification reflects the asymmetry of the free energy about its minimum rather than the equilibrium pair momentum itself. Using a Wannier-based, self-consistent finite-$\mathbf Q$ Bogoliubov-de Gennes approach with a Fermi-surface-adaptive quadrature that converges the critical currents, we show that the layer parity and orientation of magnetic proximity select the diode response of monolayer NbSe$_2$. For in-plane exchange, the equilibrium pair momentum $\mathbf Q_{\rm eq}$ and the diode efficiency are both linear in the layer-odd exchange contrast: parallel proximity on the two Se sublayers is reciprocal, and antiparallel proximity doubles the response. An out-of-plane component separates the two observables. It leaves $\mathbf Q_{\rm eq}$ nearly unchanged but, through pair breaking, suppresses the efficiency by more than an order of magnitude and, at low temperature, reverses it. Purely out-of-plane exchange, for which $C_3$ symmetry forbids a helical term, keeps $\mathbf Q_{\rm eq}=0$ yet produces a trigonal diode effect, controlled by the layer-even exchange and larger than the in-plane one above $0.65$~meV at low temperature. Magnetic layer parity thus selects between a helical and a trigonal diode channel. The trigonal channel is an intrinsic, symmetry-allowed contribution to the field-free rectification reported in NbSe$_2$/CrBr$_3$ and predicts that two-sided out-of-plane proximity cancels it when antiparallel and, at the same exchange per layer, enhances it about fourfold when parallel, in the computed range.

### Paper [36]
Title: Coherent Electronic Lensing in Proximitized Graphene
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38476
Authors: Ivan Villani, Samuele Fracassi, Niccol\`o Traverso Ziani, Maura Sassetti, Matteo Carrega, Vaidotas Mi\v{s}eikis, Camilla Coletti, Fabio Beltram, Kenji Watanabe, Takashi Taniguchi, Sergio Pezzini, Stefan Heun
Abstract: Spatially resolved control of coherent electron transport offers a route to understanding and engineering interference phenomena in hybrid superconducting devices. Here, we use Scanning Gate Microscopy (SGM) to investigate Fabry-P\'erot (FP) interference in the n-p-n cavity of a ballistic graphene Josephson junction. Spatial maps of the superconducting switching current and normal-state conductance reveal circular interference fringes arising from two distinct effects of the SGM tip potential. A global shift of the Fermi wave vector modulates the FP resonances of the cavity, while a local perturbation produces additional oscillations that persist at fixed Fermi wave vector as the tip bias is varied. An analytical model identifies the origin of this local response with coherent electron lensing, in which the tip modifies the phase accumulated along interfering trajectories. The measured fringe patterns in the switching current indicate that this mechanism affects superconducting transport, consistent with tip-induced shifts of the resonance conditions for Andreev bound states. These results establish SGM as a tunable local lens for controlling interference in superconducting ballistic graphene devices.

### Paper [37]
Title: Strain-Induced BCC-to-FCC Transformation in Tungsten Uncovered by Molecular Dynamics Simulations with Machine Learning Potentials
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38477
Authors: Qiu-Hong Hu, YueYue Wang, Xinyi Leng, Hezhu Shao, Luru Dai
Abstract: Polymorphic transformations occur in a minority of metals. Tungsten is not one of them. This observation does not rule out its intrinsic phase transition possibility, but may be due to inaccessible thermodynamic and mechanical conditions in conventional experiments. Herein, we investigate the body centered cubic (BCC) to face centered cubic (FCC) transformation in tungsten via a rigorously validated machine learning potential (MLP) molecular dynamics (MD) approach. Our simulations demonstrate that strain induced BCC to FCC transformation indeed occurs in tungsten. The FCC phase exceeds 20 at.% at low temperature and in the presence of dislocations. In the perfect crystal moderate temperatures favor phase transformation kinetics. Structural analysis confirms the transformation following the Bain and Nishiyama Wassermann orientation relationship. These results establish the viability of strain induced polymorphic transformation in tungsten and provide a critical guideline for the experimental production of duplex tungsten with enhanced ductility addressing a longstanding challenge in tungsten material engineering for high-temperature structural applications.

### Paper [38]
Title: Two-Dimensional Magnetic Materials from Ferromagnetism to Altermagnetism: Fabrication, Symmetry, Evidence, and Device Readiness
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38491
Authors: Guojian Zhao, Ziang Meng, Li Liu, Zhiyuan Duan, Jingyu Li, Sixu Jiang, Xiaoyang Tan, Shiheng Bu, Chen Liang, Xiaoning Wang, Peixin Qin, Zhiqi Liu
Abstract: Atomically thin magnets are governed by composition, thickness, stacking, strain, defects, substrates, ferroic order, and interfaces. This Review organizes two-dimensional (2D) magnetism using net magnetization and momentum-space spin splitting or spin-polarization patterns as primary coordinates, supplemented by sublattice structure and the symmetry relations between opposite-spin sublattices. Ferromagnets generally carry a net moment; ferrimagnets can be fully compensated. Conventional antiferromagnets may retain spin-degenerate bands, while altermagnets permit nonrelativistic alternating spin splitting under dimension-specific symmetry constraints. M1-M4 reporting classes introduced here distinguish layer-resolved atomically thin forms, non-layer-resolved thin flakes or films, bulk benchmarks, and theoretical candidates or design routes; the evidence category and ordering-temperature provenance are reported independently. Current experimental support for altermagnetism comes mainly from M2 thin-film and M3 bulk benchmarks, leaving a fully verified atomically thin M1 altermagnetic phase as an open experimental goal. Most proposed monolayer and few-layer systems therefore fall within M4, and contested cases clarify the measurements needed for phase assignment. Device readiness is compared qualitatively using operating temperature, readout, switching, domain control, stability, scalability, interface sensitivity, and integration difficulty. Reliable phase assignment and device assessment require complementary measurements from the same material form under matched conditions, ideally on the same specimen or in situ.

### Paper [39]
Title: Far Tails of the Positional Distribution in random walk: A Saddle-Point Approach
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38492
Authors: Chen Zhou, YuDa Cheng, Yangji Li, Jing Xu, Wanli Wang
Abstract: Rare events and large-deviation theory have attracted considerable interest in the study of random walks. Here, we investigate the rare events of the positional distribution of continuous-time random walks under ordinary and equilibrium initial conditions using the saddle point method. We show that the far-tail behavior is sensitive to the expansion of the waiting-time distribution near its minimum value. For a general power-series expansion near the minimum waiting time, the positional distribution exhibits an approximately exponential decay in its far tails. In contrast, when the waiting-time distribution has a cutoff at its minimum value, the positional distribution exhibits an unexpected Gaussian decay, in contrast to the far-tail behavior of the renewal-count distribution. We further find that the most likely number of renewals required to reach a given position, as well as the associated rate functions, is insensitive to the initial conditions, whereas the far tails of the positional distribution retain a dependence on the initial conditions. Our results provide new insights into the rare-event statistics and large deviations of random walks.

### Paper [40]
Title: Ordered patches cap the stability of disordered frameworks
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38501
Authors: Joohan Nam
Abstract: Structural disorder can stabilize frameworks against pressure-induced failure, yet the ordered regions it contains limit this gain. We show that, in rigid-jointed frames under hydrostatic pressure, an ensemble containing arbitrarily large patches of a crystal at positive density, whose centres carry a scaled crystal prestress, cannot stay linearly stable once those patches are stressed beyond the crystal's threshold for localizable instabilities. A homogeneous loss of positive definiteness while all Bloch modes, including the acoustic limit, remain stable cannot localize, leaving a window for stabilization. Extending an acoustic criterion to prestress, we show for all point groups that such a window requires a strain that keeps the crystal's symmetry. Unless the patches are shielded from stress, disorder cannot protect a crystal against a failure that breaks its symmetry, and protects a cubic crystal at most against volume collapse. In two-dimensional Truchet frames, planted ordered domains bring the threshold within 1% of the bound, and correlations short of long-range order localize failure on ordered clusters. Across 876 slender-strut nets, 798 fail first by a localizable instability; the other 78 have a median window of 7.6%. Stability gained through disorder is capped by the localizable instabilities of the local order it permits.

### Paper [41]
Title: Extraordinary stiffening and reciprocal control in a DNA condensate/actin composite
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38504
Authors: Sam Wilken, Austin Hopkins, Kyu Hwan Choi, John Berezney, Matthew E. Helgeson, M. Cristina Marchetti, Sho Takatori, Zvonimir Dogic, Omar A. Saleh
Abstract: Soft composites, materials consisting of inclusions within a polymeric matrix, offer a broad design space with diverse mechanical properties. Composite stiffness is normally set by the volume fraction of filler, and classical theory predicts at most ~25% stiffening when inclusions occupy 10% of the volume. Here we introduce a composite of phase-separated DNA droplets that occupy <10% of an entangled actin network, yet stiffen it 25-fold, reversibly. Microscopy reveals that actin penetrates the DNA droplets, leading network elasticity to determine their size; thus the system exhibits reciprocal control between matrix and droplet properties. We attribute the extraordinary stiffening to entrainment of actin by the droplets, which enlarges the effective inclusions until their contact network dominates the mechanics. This shows that liquid-liquid phase separation can control not only local compositions, but also mechanics, achieving exceptional stiffening at modest filler fractions through local geometries that constrain the matrix beyond the inclusions themselves.

### Paper [42]
Title: Reciprocal transverse Seebeck and Peltier effects in MoSi2
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38506
Authors: Aina Sakamoto, Shoya Ohsumi, Shogo Yoshida, Ryuji Okazaki, Yoshiki J. Sato
Abstract: Transverse thermoelectric conversion, in which charge and heat currents are interconverted in orthogonal directions, has recently attracted increasing attention as an alternative to conventional longitudinal thermoelectric conversion. Although transverse Seebeck effect has been investigated in various materials, its reciprocal process, transverse Peltier effect, remains much less explored, and direct comparison of the two effects in the same homogeneous material is still limited. Here, we experimentally investigate both transverse Seebeck and Peltier effects in a single crystal of MoSi2, an axis-dependent conduction polarity material in which the sign of the Seebeck coefficient varies depending on the crystallographic axis. Multi-terminal voltage measurement determines the direction of the induced internal electric field and establishes the transverse Seebeck response. We also directly visualize the transverse temperature modulation consistent with the corresponding transverse Peltier effect by using lock-in thermography under an applied ac current. These results establish reciprocal transverse thermoelectric conversion in a homogeneous nonmagnetic single crystal without engineered interfaces.

### Paper [43]
Title: Tan's Contact for Trapped Spin-Orbit-Coupled Fermi Gases at Finite Temperature
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38512
Authors: Tianhao Wu, Yuanbang Wei, Rui Tian, Dongdong Pu, Beizhi Chen, Qihang Ying, Hongyan Shi, Yue Zhang, Shuai Li, Bo Liu
Abstract: Tan's contact, bridging microscopic features of many-body quantum systems to two-body contact interactions, is pivotal in the description of quantum gases. However, past studies of Tan's contact of the spin-orbit coupled Fermi gases have been exclusively discussed at zero temperature. The thermal effects on that, which cannot be neglected in most experimental conditions, has not been explored so far. Here, through a path integral Monte Carlo (PIMC) study, we show that thermal effects fundamentally alter the scaling relation between spin-orbit coupling (SOC) and Tan's contact. The linear scaling of Tan's contact with respect to the strength of SOC found at zero temperature is drastically undermined by the thermal effects. And a new mechanism based on the competition between the mean occupancy and the energy-reweighted occupancy of the opposite spin fermion pairs within the interaction range has been unveiled, which radically changes the above linear scaling to a non-monotonic dependence. Our work should be useful for future experiments to explore the preternatural thermal effects on Tan's contact of the strongly interacting spin-orbit-coupled Fermi gas.

### Paper [44]
Title: Giant piezomagnetic response driven by spin reorientation in the topological Kagome magnet TbMn6Sn6
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38528
Authors: Hanyang Qian, Wen Sun, Juntao Huo, Guowei Li
Abstract: Achieving a large strain response to a small magnetic-field variation remains a challenge in magnetomechanical materials, particularly in topological systems where the coupling between spin reconstruction and lattice deformation remains unclear. Here, we report an anomalous magnetostrictive response associated with field-induced spin reorientation in the topological Kagome magnet TbMn6Sn6. Below its spin-reorientation temperature of 310 K, an in-plane magnetic field induces an abrupt increase in magnetostriction, which exceeds 120 ppm at 240 K. This transition produces a giant differential piezomagnetic coefficient of approximately 5000 ppm T-1, far exceeding the values reported for other topological magnetic materials and commercial FeGa alloys, and placing TbMn6Sn6 among the most field-sensitive magnetostrictive materials. The field position of this peak agrees with the anomalies in magnetization and magnetotransport, demonstrating that the large differential response is tied to field-induced spin reorientation. Thermal expansion also changes across the spin-reorientation boundary. Raman measurements reveal corresponding anomalies in phonon energy and linewidth, indicating that the reorientation modifies the lattice dynamics. The combined results support a mechanism in which competing magnetic anisotropies of the Tb and Mn sublattices, coupled through Tb-Mn exchange, create magnetic configurations with different equilibrium strains. This finding establishes spin reorientation as a route to giant piezomagnetic responses in topological magnets and provides a basis for magnetomechanical sensing and switching near room temperature.

### Paper [45]
Title: Swimming versus Screening: How Activity Reshapes the Ionic Cloud of a Charged Particle
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38594
Authors: Tanmoy Chakraborty, Tomer Markovich, Ram M. Adar
Abstract: A major question in active matter is how interactions between particles are modified by activity. Here, we address this question for Coulomb interactions by considering a charged swimmer immersed in an electrolyte. A static charge is screened by an ionic cloud that decays over the Debye length $\lambda_D$. We show that the persistent motion of the swimmer can qualitatively modify the steady-state ionic charge-density profile. At low activity, the swimmer appears effectively quasistatic to the ions, and conventional Debye-H\"uckel screening is recovered. At higher activity, the ballistic-to-diffusive dynamics of the swimmer are reflected in distinct spatial regimes. A power-law decay is followed by a stretched-exponential decay at intermediate distances. At sufficiently large distances, exponential screening is restored, but with an increased screening length, $\lambda>\lambda_D$, revealing activity-induced underscreening. Thus, active motion modifies both the spatial form and the range of electrostatic screening. Our results motivate the study of active electrolytes composed of positively and negatively charged swimmers.

### Paper [46]
Title: Nonreciprocal Floquet tunneling dynamics of spin-orbit-coupled bosonic atoms in a non-Hermitian double-well potential
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38597
Authors: Limei Ju, Yingying Zhang, Jilei Liang, Shoukang Chang, Zunlue Zhu, Lu Qin, Wuming Liu, Xingdong Zhao
Abstract: The stable tunneling dynamics in non-Hermitian quantum systems is one of the fundamental issues in the study of quantum transport. Nonreciprocal coupling, as another crucial manifestation of non-Hermiticity, exerts significant regulatory effects on the stability of quantum devices and on spin tunneling dynamics. Here we place spin-orbit-coupled bosonic atoms in a double-well potential with gain and loss, and investigate the effect of nonreciprocal coupling on the system dynamics. Through an analytical analysis of the Floquet states and the quasi-energy spectrum under periodic driving, we obtain the PT-symmetry phase diagram of the system. We further explore the synergistic regulation mechanism of several factors---including the strength of nonreciprocal coupling, the non-Hermitian strength arising from gain-loss, and the periodic driving---on stable spin-flip tunneling. The results show that precise manipulation of nonreciprocity and gain-loss can substantially enlarge the stable parameter region. In nonreciprocal systems with balanced gain and loss, the continuity or discreteness of the stable parameter region is determined by the parity of the driving-frequency ratio $\Omega/\omega$, and the spin-flip tunneling can be precisely controlled through the parameters $\lambda$ and $2\varepsilon/\omega$. For the unbalanced gain-loss scenario, the equilibrium conditions for achieving stable spin-flip tunneling under different parities of $\Omega/\omega$ are further provided. These findings offer a new theoretical approach for realizing robust spin-flip tunneling, provide guiding significance for the study of quantum-state transport in non-Hermitian ultracold atomic systems, and also serve as a reference for the experimental design of novel spin-based quantum devices.

### Paper [47]
Title: High-Performance Nuclear Demagnetization Refrigerator Reaching 50 $\mu$K
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38622
Authors: Zhengchao Liu, Junyang Song, Bingbing Tong, Hiroki Ikegami
Abstract: Research in the deep sub-mK regime around 0.1 mK may open new opportunities in condensed matter physics in light of recent rapid progress in quantum technologies and quantum materials. Here, we report the design and performance of a nuclear demagnetization system that provides efficient access to the deep sub-mK regime for a wide range of experiments, using a standard wet dilution refrigerator with a medium-sized copper nuclear stage. We show that the temperature measured by a Pt NMR thermometer reaches 50 $\mu$K and that the refrigerator can maintain 100 $\mu$K for more than ten days. Remarkably, even with only 12 hours of precooling, the refrigerator cools into the deep sub-mK regime and can maintain 100 $\mu$K for more than three days, providing sufficient time for sample measurements. Numerical analyses indicate that the copper nuclear-spin system is cooled to about 18 $\mu$K, whereas a local heat leak to the thermometer limits the thermometer temperature to about 50 $\mu$K. These features establish the system as a practical platform for efficient experiments in the deep sub-mK regime.

### Paper [48]
Title: Topology of Wannierizable Insulators
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38626
Authors: Ken Shiozaki
Abstract: We study the freedom in choosing Wannier centers and the associated symmetry representations in noninteracting Wannierizable insulators. We compare Wyckoff deformations of atomic configurations with symmetry-preserving adiabatic deformations of occupied states, both with and without adding common occupied atomic orbitals. Explicit examples show that stable Wyckoff equivalence and adiabatic equivalence at fixed filling do not imply each other. In spinless $Fm'm'm'$, an Euler class distinguishes stably Wyckoff-equivalent configurations. In spinless $P\overline1'$, adiabatically equivalent configurations remain distinct under stable Wyckoff deformations. We also classify when stable Wyckoff and stable adiabatic equivalence coincide. They agree in two or fewer dimensions and in three-dimensional spinful systems. For three-dimensional spinless systems, Chern insulator pumps give additional equivalences in 134 magnetic space groups. These groups preserve spacetime orientation and have a point whose site-symmetry group contains $222$, $\overline1'$, or $\overline4'$.

### Paper [49]
Title: Uniaxial strain tuning of functional properties in antiferroelectric PbZrO3 membrane capacitors
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38627
Authors: Umair Saeed, Huazhang Zhang, David Pesquera, Fernando G\'omez-Ortiz, Jessica Padilla- Pantoja, Jose Santiso, Philippe Ghosez, Gustau Catalan
Abstract: Lead zirconate (PbZrO3, PZO) is a prototypical antiferroelectric (AFE) system promising for applications such as energy storage and neuromorphic computing. However, in conventional thin-film architectures, its functional potential is often limited by substrate clamping. Although emerging freestanding membrane techniques offer an approach to release this clamping and enable dynamic strain engineering, a systematic understanding of how external strain modulates the switching between AFE and ferroelectric (FE) states is missing. In this study, we investigate the uniaxial strain effects on PZO membrane capacitors transferred onto flexible substrates via a gold-bonding method. We demonstrate that in-plane dynamic strain modulates the AFE-FE switching and functional properties. Corroborated by atomistic simulations, we reveal that the strain-dependent evolution arises from the superposition of the intrinsic strain dependence of specific domain orientation variants and extrinsic strain-induced redistribution of the relative population of different domain variants. These findings provide a predictive understanding of uniaxial strain tuning of AFE-FE switching in PZO, and pave the way for exploiting the functional potential of freestanding antiferroelectric membranes.

### Paper [50]
Title: Amorphous selenium-alloyed TeOx thin-film solar cells
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38643
Authors: Zhaoheng Ling, Linqu Luo, Chrysa Aivalioti, Temur Maksudov, Mengxi Zhang, Theodoulos Kalogerakis, Lazaros Panagiotidis, Elefterios Lidorikis, Spyros Doukas, Nikolaos Kalfagiannis, Epameinondas Tziortzios, Leonidas Tsetseris, Huihui Zhu, Ao Liu, Yong-Young Noh, Martin Heeney, Shadi Fatayer, Thomas D. Anthopoulos
Abstract: Tellurium dioxide (TeO$_2$) is a wide-bandgap oxide with attractive processing versatility and emerging p-type semiconducting behaviour, making it a promising material system for optoelectronic and photovoltaic applications. However, TeO$_2$-derived thin films prepared by scalable manufacturing processes, such as vacuum sublimation, can contain residual metallic Te$^0$ species that introduce trap states, limit hole transport, and promote carrier recombination. Here, we demonstrate a selenium alloying strategy to control the defect chemistry of vacuum-sublimed TeO$_x$ films. TeO$_2$:Se powder mixtures (90:10~wt\%) were thermally evaporated and subsequently annealed in air at 225~$^\circ$C for up to 9~h, yielding amorphous TeO$_x$:Se films with an optical bandgap of 0.98~eV under optimised conditions. Structural, chemical, optical, and electrical analyses show that prolonged annealing promotes Se redistribution, suppresses residual Te$^0$ to approximately 0.4\%, reduces the film's surface roughness, and shifts the valence-band edge. To evaluate the potential of TeO$_x$:Se for photovoltaic applications, we fabricated thin-film solar cells with an ITO/ZnO/TeO$_x$:Se/MoO$_x$/Ag architecture. Devices based on an 80~nm TeO$_x$:Se active layer annealed for 9~h exhibit optimal performance, with broadband photocurrent generation from 300 to 1000~nm, yielding an open-circuit voltage of 0.50~V, a short-circuit current density of 8.50~mA~cm$^{-2}$, a fill factor of 0.44, and a maximum power conversion efficiency of approximately 1.87\%.
