These results establish selenium-alloyed TeO$_x$ as a viable oxide-derived absorber for thin-film photovoltaics and highlight chemical-state control as an effective route for improving photovoltaic performance.

### Paper [51]
Title: Information-Theoretic Reliability--Safety Bounds and Finite-Alignment Dynamics in AI Knowledge Distillation
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38651
Authors: Miao-Miao Yi, Zhen-Yu Cai, Y -M. Du, C. P. Sun
Abstract: The reliability and safety of artificial intelligence (AI), together with their transfer and evolution through knowledge distillation, have attracted growing attention. We establish an information-theoretic bound on the reliability--safety trade-off and characterize the dynamics of this trade-off under knowledge distillation. We first prove a universal trade-off inequality that is independent of the specific model form. Its boundary is determined solely by a single parameter Kh, which quantifies the hidden layer's ability to distinguish benign from hazardous inputs. We then develop a canonical Boltzmann-machine model of AI response behavior and derive two exact relations between reliability and safety. The trade-off curve is determined by the hazard discrimination capacity K, while the overall answer tendency controls the operating point along the curve. These relations agree well with existing experimental data. In knowledge distillation, we find that teacher responses induce a renormalization of the student model's trade-off parameters. We further use a renormalization-group approach to study multigenerational distillation, deriving exact recursion relations and the associated renormalization-group-like flows for two representative direct intergenerational distillation protocols at arbitrary teacher--student alignment strength. These flows reveal fixed-point structures, long-term dynamics, and critical behavior, yielding testable predictions for the critical transition between capability loss and stable transmission.

### Paper [52]
Title: Finite-time minimum-work protocols for underdamped traps driven by active forces
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38653
Authors: Kangcan Yao, Ruicheng Bao, Zhonghuai Hou
Abstract: Active systems provide non-equilibrium energy that can, in principle, be harvested using control protocols, making the optimization and reliability of work extraction a fundamental problem in microscopic thermodynamics. Yet how inertia and activity jointly modify the optimal work extraction protocol, the maximum extractable work its fluctuations, and implementability of protocols remains unclear. Here, we design minimum-work protocols, which maximize work extraction whenever extraction is possible, for an underdamped active particle in a translated harmonic trap. Remarkably, both the minimum mean work and its fluctuations are independent of mass, although inertia modifies the protocol. The results are then applied to an underdamped active Ornstein-Uhlenbeck particle (AOUP), designing an explicit work-extraction criterion and revealing extracted work's dependence on rotational noise and initial conditions. We further show that inertia strongly affects the difficulty of implementing the optimal protocol, producing a transition-like change between underdamped and overdamped optimal protocols. These findings uncover work extraction mechanisms at the microscale and guide the design of reliable energy-harvesting microscopic devices and robots.

### Paper [53]
Title: The effects of hydrodynamic interactions on the swimming velocity and stability of a swarm of microswimmers
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38689
Authors: Andr\'{e}s C\'{o}rdoba, Jay D. Schieber
Abstract: The dynamics and stability of a swarm of microswimmers is examined here using a thermodynamically compliant microswimmer model. The theory presented here is a mean-field model in which the swarm is considered to be a uniform solution of swimmers that are moving, on average, in the same direction. The hydrodynamic interaction between swimmers is modeled accurately assuming a crystalline arrangement to the swarm. A swimmer in the swarm can swim up to twelve times faster than when alone in an infinite sea, when the volume fraction of swimmers in the swarm is about 0.14. Moreover we have also studied the stability of the swarm around a uniform crystalline arrangement by calculating the hydrodynamic torques generated by the swarm as a function of the volume fraction of swimmers. The predictions presented here agree with recent multiparticle simulations that have shown that hydrodynamic torques have a stabilizing effect in swarms of pullers while swarms of pushers are generally destabilized by hydrodynamic interactions. The thermodynamically admissible coupling between the swimmer's motion and fuel consumption allows us to study the full dynamics instead of artificially constrained steady-states only. By accounting for fuel consumption and high order hydrodynamic interactions we are able to examine the swarm's stability as function of fuel concentration and the volume fraction of swimmers. We find that at high concentrations of fuel, swarms of pullers are stabilized by hydrodynamic torques for volume fractions of swimmers as low as 0.02 but at lower volume fractions Brownian forces make the swarm unstable.

### Paper [54]
Title: Symmetries and Propagation of Surface Plasmon Polaritons in Axion Electrodynamics
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38692
Authors: Marco Peluso
Abstract: Surface plasmon polaritons at the interface between a conductor governed by axion electrodynamics and an ordinary dielectric are studied using a general phenomenological model, in which the macroscopic response is described by an axial vector $ \mathbf{G} $, responsible for the anomalous Hall effect, and a pseudoscalar $ W $, accounting for the chiral magnetic effect. We extend the analysis of the chiral magnetic effect in the planar geometry, address its role in the cylindrical one and, in both geometries, discuss the case in which the two effects coexist. We find an upper bound on $ W $, a nonreciprocity requiring both effects and the absence of freely propagating modes, depending on the orientation of $ \mathbf{G} $, for the planar interface, and, for the cylindrical one, a novel coupling between the linear and orbital angular momenta of the modes, which already arises from the chiral magnetic effect alone and persists when the anomalous Hall effect is also present. The exact eigenmodes of the axion medium are derived, and the symmetries and propagation features of the surface modes are made explicit by analytical and asymptotic evaluation of the dispersion relations.

### Paper [55]
Title: Gravitational anomaly spectroscopy of a Kitaev spin liquid
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38704
Authors: Kumar Ghosh
Abstract: The chiral central charge $c$ measures the gravitational anomaly of a two-dimensional topological phase. It fixes the heat current carried along the edge. So far, it has been accessed through thermal transport, where phonons complicate the signal, or through wave-function probes that need a pure ground state. Here we show that $c$ is a spectroscopic quantity of the edge. A weak modulation of the edge couplings that travel along the edge is absorbed according to the Virasoro algebra. Normalized by the response to a static change of the same couplings, the absorbed weights obey $D_n=2n\langle L_0\rangle+\frac{c}{12}(n^3-n)$, so that $\mathcal{C}\equiv2D_2-4D_1=c$ in any state of the ideal edge theory, thermal or not. The central charge thus appears in transition weights, although it cancels from excitation gaps. The ratio of line weights with and without a vison in the bulk, $\Lambda=4h_\sigma/c$, needs no calibration and fixes the conformal spin $h_\sigma$ of the non-Abelian Ising anyon. For the Kitaev honeycomb spin liquid we find $\mathcal{C}=0.49999$ and $h_\sigma=0.06250$ on 7168 spins. Calibrating with the shift of the measured line itself gives $c$ within about $1\%$ on 96 spins and within $1\%$ under $20\%$ random coupling errors. Interacting edge Majoranas in a fixed flux sector, treated with DMRG, keep $\mathcal{C}$ within $1.5\%$ while the edge velocity changes by $\pm13\%$. An exact simulation of a quantum-processor protocol, including the native Floquet circuit, returns $c$ within $3.5\%$ on 96 to 600 spins.

### Paper [56]
Title: Large connectivity expansion of Ising Spin Glasses on random graphs
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38724
Authors: Luca Maria Del Bono, Flavio Nicoletti, Federico Ricci-Tersenghi
Abstract: We compute a high-connectivity analytical expansion for Ising spin glasses on sparse random graphs. We expand the cavity equations around the fully-connected mean-field limit, focusing on the critical behavior of the system: our approach allows us to compute the de Almeida-Thouless line of mean-field finitely-connected systems in the temperature $T$--magnetic field $H$ plane, by means of closed analytical equations. The exact cavity solution of such systems can only be obtained by solving numerically iterative equations, so our approach brings a notable computational advantage. On the theoretical side, we are able to identify three sources of corrections to the fully-connected mean-field solution: i) the non-Gaussianity of the cavity fields, ii) the statistical correlation between the cavity fields and their fluctuations, and iii) the corrections to the cavity filter functions. These corrections are treated using two different expansions: a non-uniform expansion, where we expand all three contributions around the mean-field solution, and a uniform expansion, where we only expand the statistics but leave the cavity filters untouched. The former allows for a direct comparison with the Sherrington-Kirkpatrick model but fails at low temperatures, as a consequence of the de Almeida-Thouless line diverging in the zero-temperature limit. On the other hand, the uniform expansion describes with good accuracy the critical behavior of the system. We also extend these results to the $T = 0$ limit.

### Paper [57]
Title: Mixed-State Topological Transition and Emergent Fractionalized Supersymmetry in the Non-Abelian Kitaev Spin Liquid
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38741
Authors: Zhou-Quan Wan, Hong Yao, Shao-Kai Jian
Abstract: We study a mixed-state topological transition in the non-Abelian Kitaev spin liquid driven by a bond-dephasing channel. In the vectorized picture, bond dephasing maps onto interactions between two time-reversed fractionalized Majorana systems coupled to gapped $\mathbb Z_2$ gauge fields, enabling sign-problem-free Monte Carlo simulations. We show that increasing dephasing condenses an inter-copy fermion pair, converting ${\rm Ising}\boxtimes\overline{\rm{Ising}}$ to toric-code order in the Choi state and yielding intrinsically mixed-state topological order upon unfolding. More interestingly, at a temporal interface between topological and trivial Kitaev states, a Chern-number mismatch enforces a gapless Majorana mode. Dephasing then drives a mixed-state topological transition at which the gapless mode develops emergent $\mathcal N=1$ supersymmetry. Our Monte Carlo simulations yield $\nu=1.27(3)$ and $\eta_b\simeq\eta_f\simeq0.40$, consistent with the exact supersymmetric values $5/4$ and $2/5$. The supersymmetric partners are fractionalized and appear physically as distinct gauge-invariant spin strings, providing concrete observables for future experiments.

### Paper [58]
Title: A Luminescent Organic Triradical as an Optically Addressable Qudit
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38762
Authors: Kueyoung E. Kim, Avik Bhanja, Alistair Inglis, William K. Myers, Sam L. Bayliss, Richard H. Friend, Petri Murto
Abstract: Luminescent molecular trityl radicals provide options for optically-addressable electron spins and are promising for quantum sensing. Here, we present a luminescent triradical comprising three trityl groups covalently attached to a central phenyl ring. We find two distinct photoluminescence bands at 633 nm and 690 nm with different temporal evolutions, which we attribute to excited quartet and doublet states respectively. The ground state quartet is 1 meV (11.5K) lower in energy than the doublet, resulting in near-equal population of doublets and quartets at room temperature. We demonstrate optically initialized spin polarization, coherent control of the ground state quartet, and optical readout. Potential for optical quantum sensing is shown via optically detected magnetic resonance at room temperature and magneto-photoluminescence up to 3% at 5 mT. This material is a candidate optically addressable molecular qudit, providing opportunities to leverage larger state spaces than qubit systems.

### Paper [59]
Title: Multiscale evaluation of the elastic response of polycrystalline NiAl using molecular statics, EBSD-based finite element and analytical homogenization methods
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38783
Authors: Szymon Nosewicz, Marcin Ma\'zdziarz, Pawe{\l} Ho{\l}obut, Witold Chromi\'nski, Katarzyna Kowalczyk-Gajewska, Grzegorz Jurczak
Abstract: The presented work proposes a multiscale framework for the numerical and analytical determination of the elastic constants of the intermetallic nickel aluminide (NiAl) at the microscopic scale. Molecular dynamics (MD) calculations have been employed to evaluate the stiffness tensors of a single NiAl monocrystalline body with cubic Pm$\bar{3}$m symmetry. The atomistic results were transferred to the upper scale as an input to micromechanical models. Here, finite element modeling (FEM) and analytical homogenization methods were used to simulate the elastic behavior of polycrystalline NiAl, with special attention to grain anisotropy and its morphology. The microstructural features of the heterogeneous body were evaluated experimentally using electron backscatter diffraction (EBSD) and subsequently used to generate a 3D polycrystalline geometric model that preserves realistic distributions of grain orientations and grain sizes. The reconstructed NiAl polycrystal was subjected to uniaxial tensile test simulations using FEM to evaluate the effective Young's modulus ($E$) and Poisson's ratio ($\nu$). The numerical results were confronted with the experimental values of $E$ and~$\nu$. With these model assumptions, a very good agreement between numerical and experimental results was achieved. Finally, the experimental results were compared with classical analytical estimates. Among the analytical approaches, the Hashin-Shtrikman upper bound and self-consistent approximation reproduced the experimental Young's modulus with minor deviations.

### Paper [60]
Title: Transverse momentum transport and collective-mode localization in dipolar tube arrays
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38801
Authors: Camilla Polvara, Nishchhal Verma, Sarang Gopalakrishnan, Vadim Oganesyan
Abstract: We study transverse transport of longitudinal momentum in an array of one-dimensional Bose gases coupled by dipolar interactions without particle tunneling. Within zero-temperature Bogoliubov theory, we characterize this transport using the inter-tube momentum correlator and its squared magnitude, termed amplitude and intensity. In a uniform array, pairs of Bogoliubov quasiparticles propagate ballistically across the tubes, while their dephasing produces a $t^{-3}$ decay of the long-wavelength amplitude. Tube-to-tube density imbalance acts as quenched disorder and localizes the transverse collective modes. At fixed longitudinal momentum, this arrests the growth of the intensity profile, whose time-averaged spatial decay measures the localization length. Equal-time correlations are much less sensitive to disorder and primarily reflect the interaction range: in the uniform array they decay with tube separation as $r^{-7}$ for dipolar coupling, compared with exponential decay for nearest-neighbor coupling. Finally, sufficiently strong inter-tube repulsion destabilizes the longitudinally uniform state toward density modulations staggered between neighboring tubes, at a threshold independent of density imbalance.

### Paper [61]
Title: Spectrum of the steady-state distribution function of disordered Lindbladian fermions at large channel number
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38805
Authors: Maksim Parfenov, Igor Burmistrov
Abstract: Free fermions coupled to particle gain and loss relax to a Gaussian steady state, in which every natural orbital has its own occupation. We find the distribution of these occupations $\varrho_{\rm st}$ for a random Hamiltonian coupled to many random gain and loss channels. Each orbital is filled and emptied at its own rates, and the Hamiltonian, moving particles across the levels during their lifetime, averages the differences between the orbitals. We show that this averaging is incomplete: the spread of the occupations decreases only as the inverse square root of the number of channels per level. At large system size the distribution depends on the grain Hamiltonian only through its density of states $\nu(E)$. The spread is bounded by the baths alone: even Hamiltonians with unbounded, heavy-tailed spectra cannot widen it beyond this bound. The spread suppresses the particle-number fluctuations below their binomial value.

### Paper [62]
Title: Autonomous Quantum Measurement with Agentic AI
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38850
Authors: Eunbi Rha, Abhijatmedhi Chotrattanapituk, Mouyang Cheng, Chung-tao Chou, Shenyong Ye, Ashley S. Dale, Long Ju, Luqiao Liu, Mingda Li
Abstract: Quantum materials exhibit diverse electronic and functional phenomena, but their characterization requires extensive experimental expertise, iterative decision-making and accumulated laboratory knowledge that is difficult to document and transfer. Here we introduce Quantum Agent (QUANTA), an agentic artificial intelligence (AI) framework for autonomous quantum-materials characterization. Using a widely adopted Physical Property Measurement System (PPMS) as an experimental platform, QUANTA combines retrieval augmentation using scientific literature, experimental protocols and prior measurements with specialized agents for experimental planning, troubleshooting, and data interpretation. A separate deterministic control layer validates instrument actions. Guided by incoming data, QUANTA evaluates measurement quality, revises experimental plans and selects subsequent measurements. We demonstrate these capabilities through transport measurements of the topological Weyl semimetal TaP, as well as investigations of nonlinear responses in magnetic material Co2MnGa. These results establish QUANTA as a generalizable framework for evidence-driven, safeguarded and closed-loop quantum-materials characterization.

### Paper [63]
Title: Effect of the surface on the energy levels in PbS and PbSe nanocrystals
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38874
Authors: O. Lundgren, A. V. Rodina, Al. L. Efros
Abstract: The structure of the electron and hole quantum confinement levels in spherical PbS and PbSe nanocrystals (NCs) is studied within the framework of a four-band effective-mass model. This level structure was initially calculated within the Kang--Wise model, which employs the standard boundary condition of vanishing carrier wave functions at the NC surface. This model, however, does not allow one to account for the effect of the NC surface on the level structure. Here, the effect of the NC surface is modeled using general boundary conditions (GBCs) imposed on the envelope wave functions at the surface. We show that the surface properties modify the size dependence of the electron and hole confinement levels and lead to an additional spin--orbit splitting of the $1P_{h,e}$ levels. Using the GBCs, we are able to quantitatively describe the size dependence of the lowest optical transitions in bare PbS and PbSe NCs and explain the differences between the absorption spectra of bare and Cl-passivated PbS NCs

### Paper [64]
Title: Learning Between the Lines: Interaction Interpolation in a Multi Element Machine Learning Interatomic Potential
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38876
Authors: Ademola Soyemi, Kipton Barros, Timothy C. Germann, Benjamin T. Nebgen, Brenden W. Hamilton
Abstract: The continuous development of machine learning inter-atomic potentials (MLIAPs)has greatly accelerated materials research in recent years. However, the need to con- struct custom MLIAPs for each unique problem remains a bottleneck which raises the question of the transferability of MLIAPs. While foundation MLIAPs can achieve transferability due to the broad coverage of chemical space represented in their training sets, their size and computational cost limits their routine use. We address this gap by developing a lightweight general-purpose MLIAP for metal alloys spanning twelve elements (Al, Sc, Ti, V, Cu, Zn, Zr, Nb, Mo, Hf, Ta, W). Here, we utilize a step-wise active learning (AL) approach to develop a compact yet diverse dataset and model that generalizes well by interpolating from previously seen interactions. The resulting potentials transfer effectively to unseen, higher body-order alloys systems, requiring only unary and binary training data to capture ternary and quaternary systems. The model also transfers well to new elements via an alchemical extrapolation within the network, requiring only unary data for the new element and its alloys. These findings suggest that MLIAPs trained on targeted, diversity-driven datasets provide transferability at a relatively low computational cost, offering a practical path toward general-purpose, resource-efficient MLIAPs for materials modeling.

### Paper [65]
Title: Thermodynamic cost of locally maintaining a steady state
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38925
Authors: Sarang Gopalakrishnan
Abstract: The Gibbs states of local Hamiltonians have short-range conditional correlations. To maintain a steady state with long-range conditional correlations, one might expect that a stochastic process must ``pay'' either in nonlocal moves or entropy production. Surprisingly, we find strictly local Gaussian processes that obey detailed balance and have unique steady states with arbitrarily long-range conditional correlations. We show that such processes must still produce entropy, if implemented using a local quadratic Hamiltonian coupled locally to equilibrium, rapidly relaxing baths. We lower-bound this entropy production in terms of the steady state's conditional mutual information (CMI); if the CMI decays exponentially with distance, the associated length-scale is called the Markov length $\xi$. The entropy production per site per relaxation time obeys $\hat\sigma \ge c \xi^2$ for large $\xi$, and with $c$ an implementation-dependent constant. We also bound the tradeoff between coupling range and entropy production. We contrast these Gaussian processes with jump processes on discrete state spaces, for which, under certain irreducibility assumptions, steady states with long-range CMI are only possible if detailed balance is violated. We conjecture (and present evidence) that rapidly mixing local jump processes cannot stabilize steady states with long-range CMI.

### Paper [66]
Title: Collective sliding relaxor ferroelectricity across thousand-layer van der Waals superstructures
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38928
Authors: Nianze Shang, Cuiju Yu, Yucai Lin, Juan C. Arias-Mu\~noz, Jiajie Qi, Quanlin Guo, MD Gius Uddin, Youqiang Huang, Tuomas Pajunp\"a\"a, Christopher P. Edwards, Rodolfo E. Canet-Albiach, Faisal Ahmed, Andreas C. Liapis, Yaoqiang Zhou, Zhibin Zhang, Hao Hong, Qing Dai, Andrea Al\`u, Jos\'e L. Lado, Kaihui Liu, Zhipei Sun
Abstract: Relaxor ferroelectrics are among the most important electromechanical materials owing to their exceptionally large electrostrictive responses. However, conventional relaxor behavior is generally associated with chemical complexity and structural disorder, where local compositional fluctuations generate polar nanoregions, making the microscopic origin of this behavior difficult to control and engineer. Here we report sliding relaxor ferroelectricity (S-RFE), a previously unknown relaxor state in which engineered stacking-phase heterogeneity enables cooperative interlayer sliding to generate collective relaxor behavior. Through stacking-phase engineering in rhombohedral boron nitride, we create sliding domains that act as structural analogues of polar nanoregions and cooperatively align under an electric field through interlayer sliding. This collective state produces reversible electromechanical deformation of up to approximately $-1.6\%$ with negligible hysteresis in approximately 300-nm-thick crystals, corresponding to approximately 1000 atomic layers. The resulting electrostriction coefficient ($Q_{33}$ of approximately $1200\,\mathrm{m}^{4}\mathrm{C}^{-2}$) exceeds those of most crystalline electrostrictors by orders of magnitude. Unlike conventional relaxors, whose electromechanical response is often suppressed by dipolar freezing at low temperatures, S-RFE remains active down to 1.6 K and maintains giant electromechanical functionality. Our results establish sliding relaxor ferroelectricity as a new route to relaxor behavior and reveal how atomic-scale interlayer motion can be amplified into collective mesoscale functionality across approximately one thousand layers, providing a materials-by-design platform for electromechanical, photonic, and quantum technologies based on van der Waals materials.

### Paper [67]
Title: Development and application of a machine learning potential for diamond surfaces and their sliding interfaces with silicon nitride and silica: effect of passivation, orientation and load
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38937
Authors: Enrico Pedretti, Maria Clelia Righi
Abstract: Friction and wear of diamond are controlled by its surface chemistry, which determines whether covalent bonds form across the sliding interface. First-principles simulations have explored the role of surface orientation and passivation, but are limited to a few hundred atoms and tens of picoseconds. To bridge this gap, we develop a reactive machine learning interatomic potential for the C/H/N/O/Si chemical space, covering diamond, silicon nitride, silicon oxide, their surfaces, interfaces, and environmental molecules. The potential is trained on a large dataset of diverse structures and is tested on multiple physical properties, including ab initio reaction barriers on diamond surfaces, and structural/mechanical properties of amorphous Si$_3$N$_4$ and SiO$_2$. We then simulate sliding of diamond-Si$_3$N$_4$ interfaces with four surface orientations, clean and H/OH-passivated terminations, and normal loads from 0.1 to 23 GPa. We identify two different regimes: one where sliding is localized at the interface, and shear stress is proportional to the interfacial bond density, and another where shear is distributed inside the softer material, and adhesion due to full interfacial bonding dominates. These results show how passivation, surface orientation and load together determine interfacial bonding and friction, with implications for the environmental sensitivity of diamond wear. More broadly, the potential enables reactive simulations of diamond, silicon nitride and silica with near-ab initio accuracy at length and time scales beyond first-principles methods, providing a useful tool for future studies of wear at plowing asperities, chemical-mechanical polishing, and properties of amorphous ceramic films.

### Paper [68]
Title: Comment on "Is there a density-gradient expansion for the electronic exchange energy?"
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38943
Authors: Mario Benites, Angel Rosado, Efstratios Manousakis
Abstract: Perdew et al. [arXiv:2610.07201 (2026)] dispute our finding [Phys. Rev. B 113, 115136 (2026)] that the separate exchange ($b_x$) and correlation ($b_c$) contributions to the $q^2$ coefficient of the long-wavelength proper polarization function depend on the infrared regularization of the Coulomb interaction, although their sum $b_{xc}=b_x+b_c$ does not. We question their claim that the conventional exchange coefficient is uniquely determined. In Qian's [J. Math. Phys. 56, 111901 (2015)] derivation, which Perdew et al. cite in support of their claim of a unique exchange-gradient coefficient, an integral of the form $\int_{-a}^{b}dx/x$ crosses a singularity and does not exist as an ordinary improper integral. Its finite Cauchy principal value (which is a particular way of regularizing the integral) is obtained only by excluding equal intervals on the two sides of the singularity; unequal exclusions give different answers. Independently, Sham's calculation with the screened interaction $e^{-\lambda_0 r}/r$ gives an exchange-gradient coefficient different from the bare-Coulomb calculations of Svendsen--von Barth [Int. J. Quantum Chem. 56, 351 (1995)] and Engel--Vosko [Phys. Rev. B 42, 4940 (1990)]. The discrepancy remains when $\lambda_0>0$ is so small that $1/\lambda_0$ is comparable to the size of the observable universe, making the two interactions experimentally indistinguishable on laboratory scales. Why should the exactly unscreened result be preferred, even if its calculation is accepted as valid? Our calculation shows that the ambiguity of the separate contributions cancels in the combined, regulator-independent coefficient $b_{xc}$.

### Paper [69]
Title: Separating the Density Wave Phase from Zero-Resistance Superconductivity in La$_4$Ni$_3$O$_{10}$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38959
Authors: P. Puphal, A. Suthar, B. Wehinger, V. Sundaramurthy, T. Priessnitz, M. Knauft, P. Sosa-Lizama, Y. -M. Wu, M. Hepting, J. Nuss, M. Isobe, P. A. van Aken, Y. E. Suyolcu, B. Keimer, H. Takagi, P. Reiss
Abstract: Identifying the key ingredients for superconductivity under pressure in the Ruddlesden-Popper nickelates remains a formidable challenge, due to a complex interplay between structural variations, oxygen content and location, electronically ordered phases, and the need for high pressures to reach the superconducting phase. To disentangle these complex inter-dependencies, here we study a series of clean single crystals of trilayer La$_4$Ni$_3$O$_{10}$. We discover that enhanced biaxial, compressive pressures induce a zero-resistance state at a 60\% lower pressure compared to previous reports. At the same time, we completely separate the low-pressure density wave phase from superconductivity, effectively ruling out that soft, quantum critical fluctuations of the density wave order parameter act as the pairing glue in this system. Our results establish biaxial, in-plane pressures as a powerful tool for promoting superconductivity in nickelates, unifying the required pressure scales for superconductivity in different Ruddlesden-Popper structures, and providing a connection to the recently discovered thin film samples.

### Paper [70]
Title: Coexistence of superconductivity and magnetic order in BaFe$_2$S$_3$: evidence for orbital-selective delocalization
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38966
Authors: Y. Oubaid, N. S. Dhami, P. Fertey, C. V. Colin, C. Ritter, A. Forget, D. Bounoua, J. -P. Rueff, M. Verseils, P. Foury-Leylekian, V. Bal\'edent
Abstract: Understanding how superconductivity emerges from correlated electronic states is a central challenge in condensed matter physics. The quasi-one-dimensional spin-ladder compound \bfs\ serves as a unique platform to study this interplay, as it lacks the two-dimensional square lattice characteristic of most iron-based superconductors. Here, we combine high-pressure x-ray and neutron diffraction, x-ray emission spectroscopy, and electrical transport to map its structural, magnetic, and electronic evolution into the superconducting regime. We \revised{observe} a striking pressure-induced decoupling: while the long-range stripe-type antiferromagnetic order remains remarkably robust under pressure up to the onset of superconductivity, the local iron magnetic moment is suppressed by nearly 80\%. This dichotomy \revised{points to an orbital-selective mechanism}, where specific Fe $3d$ electrons remain localized to sustain magnetism while others delocalize to form the itinerant and superconducting state. \revised{Superconductivity emerges while long-range magnetic order is still present, revealing that the two coexist in \bfs, which we attribute to} the selective response of multiorbital electronic degrees of freedom.

### Paper [71]
Title: Magnetism in a homometallic square-planar Cu$({\mathrm{II})}$$_4$Cu$({\mathrm{II})}$ metallacrown
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38989
Authors: Luca Bischof, Ariyan Tavakoli, Sascha Pietruska, Lena Spillecke, Ina Heckelmann, Changhyun Koo, Luca M. Carrella, Eva Rentschler, Hans Christian Schneider, R\"udiger Klingeler
Abstract: The well-established homometallic metallacrown Cu(II)[12-MC$_\text{Cu(II)N(Shi)}$-4]$^{2-}$, comprising four square-planarly arranged Cu(II) ions surrounding a slightly off-plane central Cu(II) ion, provides an excellent platform for investigating magnetism in exchange-coupled Cu(II) clusters both by experimental and ab initio numerical methods. In the present work, sterically bulky substituents are introduced into the salicylhydroxamate ligand to obtain a modified derivative, Cu$({\mathrm{II})}$[12-MC$_\text{Cu$({\mathrm{II})}$N(L)}$-4]$^{2-}$ (Cu$_4$Cu), thereby effectively suppressing intermolecular magnetic interactions and enabling a detailed investigation of its intrinsic ground-state spin structure and exchange interactions. Static magnetization reveals competing antiferromagnetic exchange interactions of $J_1=-8.1(2)$ THz ($-33.5(8)$ meV) between the central and the peripheral sites and $J_2=-4.40(12)$ THz ($-18.2(5)$ meV) between neighboring sites on the ring of the metallacrown, which are corroborated by ab initio broken symmetry-based density functional theory (DFT). These couplings yield an $S=1/2$ ground state for Cu$_4$Cu at low temperatures, for which X-band and high-frequency/high-field electron paramagnetic resonance (EPR) precisely determine the $g$-factors $g_x = 2.028$, $g_y = 2.056$, and $g_z = 2.222$ close to axial anisotropy and in agreement with our DFT results. The ground-state spin-configuration is characterized by $d_{xy}$-character of the unoccupied states. Furthermore, our magnetization data evidence thermal transitions across a doublet-to-quartet gap of $\Delta_{1/2\rightarrow3/2} = 4.8(4)$ THz which is also indicated by the emergence of an excited-state EPR signal.

### Paper [72]
Title: Spectral theory of large data with power-law latent features
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39013
Authors: Philipp Fleig
Abstract: Power-law eigenvalue spectra of covariance matrices estimated from data appear in diverse domains ranging from neural populations to proteins. We introduce a generative latent feature model for such data, optionally corrupted by measurement noise, and develop its spectral theory. We derive closed leading-order self-consistent equations for the Stieltjes transform of the covariance matrix from which the bulk eigenvalue density is obtained and provide a variational principle for the self-consistent equations. We also compute the positions and fluctuations of isolated top eigenvalues. The theory is validated in known algebraic limits and tested against simulations.

### Paper [73]
Title: Nonreciprocity Propels Nematic Defects
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39037
Authors: Yunpeng Cao, Yu Du, Yao Li
Abstract: Nonreciprocal interactions are a generic source of nonequilibrium dynamics, yet their consequences for nematic topological defects remain unexplored. We introduce a nonreciprocal nematic model on a two-dimensional hexagonal lattice and formulate a continuum theory containing a nematic nonreciprocal torque. In the deterministic limit, simulations and symmetry analysis show that this torque drives +1/2 defects to self-propel along their polar axes. Nonreciprocity modifies the splitting pathway of integer +1 defects. These results identify a symmetry-resolved route from nonreciprocity to nematic defect transport and topological reorganization, distinct from active-stress-driven motion.

### Paper [74]
Title: Response of Hydrogels and Microgels to Nanoparticle Crowding
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39039
Authors: Mahesh Aryal, Alan R. Denton
Abstract: Internal degrees of freedom of cross-linked polymer networks enable compressible hydrogels and microgels to swell or deswell by absorbing or expelling solvent in response to environmental changes. Macromolecules in solution can influence the swelling response by acting as crowding agents, with practical relevance for drug delivery and biosensing. We extend the Flory-Rehner theory of polymer network swelling to incorporate nanoparticle crowders as another implicit species that can perturb a gel through volume exclusion, including the entropic cost of crowder penetration from polymer field theory. Within the free energy landscape of asymmetric ternary mixtures, we numerically solve equilibrium relations equating solvent and crowder chemical potentials inside and outside the gel. Independently, we perform Monte Carlo simulations, including trial moves that allow gels to swell and take up crowders. We investigate how crowder size, solution concentration, cross-link density, and solvent quality influence swelling of polymeric gels, partitioning of crowders between inside and outside, and structural properties of microgel suspensions governed by Hertzian elastic pair interactions. Our results show that nanoparticle crowding promotes deswelling, thereby weakening interparticle correlations, and that, with increasing the concentration of crowders, polymeric gels reach a maximum loading capacity, dependent on crowder size and polymer cross-link density.

### Paper [75]
Title: HERA: Assessing an Autonomous Agent for Quantum Device Measurement
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39074
Authors: Zachary W. Gomez, Nathan Bittner, Jordan T. McCourt, Qingrui Cao, Sandesh S. Kalantre, Benjamin H. Alexander, Zhiren Zheng, Kenji Watanabe, Takashi Taniguchi, Aaron Sharpe
Abstract: Large language model agents have begun to carry out laboratory measurements, but their scientific conclusions are useful only if they hold up under scrutiny. Demonstrating this requires controlled studies because models are stochastic and the context a model is given steers its conclusions. Here we introduce HERA, an autonomous agent designed to carry out cryogenic transport measurements of quantum devices. HERA draws on a knowledge base curated by lab members that holds measurement protocols, software tools, and literature references. We assessed HERA with a blinded trial against an expert on the stacking order of two multilayer graphene devices, an ablation of its knowledge base, and hundreds of offline replays in which models designed the trial's central measurement without running it. HERA's conclusions matched the expert's sealed conclusions on both tested devices. The ablation and the replay studies measured the impact of the knowledge base across different models, showing that with it every model designed better measurements and reasoned more soundly. To test how readily HERA extends to a new problem, we tasked it with finding and characterizing superconductivity in a different material system. This required a few hours of expert time to develop a new experimental protocol, after which HERA did both autonomously. These results show that, with a knowledge base designed for the task, language model agents are capable of sound experimental science.

### Paper [76]
Title: A rhombic spin-1 interpretation of a room-temperature GaN emitter
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39081
Authors: Daniel Hashemi
Abstract: Single defects in GaN show optically detected magnetic resonance at room temperature. In the more abundant class, a minimal $S=3/2$ model leaves a $g\approx 4$ line unexplained. For one defect, this line lies near the sum of the two outer lines, consistent with transitions among three shared levels. A rhombic $S=1$ model reproduces the three line positions with rms errors of 8.1-9.8 MHz. The tested second-order $S=3/2$ fits containing all three transitions and the published minimal model do not reproduce these positions. A ladder fitted to the strong lines remains viable if the $g\approx 4$ line is a separate transition near the sum of the outer two. An unresolved frequency-window shift prevents the existing data from establishing the $S=1$ assignment uniquely. We propose a zero-field sweep that directly tests the isolated $S=1$ assignment.

### Paper [77]
Title: The End of Chaos at the Ideal Glass Transition
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39098
Authors: Zi-Long Wang, Jack F. Douglas, Matteo Baggioli, Zhao-Yan Sun, Wen-Sheng Xu
Abstract: The ideal glass transition has often been associated with the extrapolated vanishing of configurational entropy $S_c$, yet how this thermodynamic criterion is reflected in microscopic dynamics remains unclear. Here, we show that the vanishing of deterministic chaos quantified by the maximum Lyapunov exponent $\lambda_{\mathrm{max}}$ provides such a dynamical signature. Using molecular dynamics simulations across diverse model glass-forming liquids in two and three dimensions, we show that $\lambda_{\mathrm{max}}$ follows an approximate scaling $\lambda_{\mathrm{max}} \propto (T-T_{\lambda})^{1/2}$ and extrapolates to zero at a temperature $T_{\lambda}$ consistent with the Kauzmann temperature $T_{\mathrm{K}}$. Furthermore, we establish the quantitative relation $\lambda_{\mathrm{max}}^2 \propto T S_c$, directly linking chaotic dynamics to configurational entropy. By demonstrating that $\lambda_{\mathrm{max}}^2 \propto \sigma_K^2$, where $\sigma_K^2$ is the variance of local potential energy landscape curvature fluctuations, we connect the suppression of chaos directly to landscape geometry. These results provide a unified thermodynamic, dynamical, and geometric framework for glass formation, identifying the ideal glass transition as the endpoint of chaotic exploration of the energy landscape.

### Paper [78]
Title: Two faces of symmetry match in gear-like coupled rotary motors
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39121
Authors: Sara Iranbakhsh, David A. Sivak
Abstract: Rotary molecular motors commonly couple components with different rotational symmetries, but how this gearing controls energy transduction remains unclear. Here we show that symmetry can produce a fundamental tradeoff in coupled stochastic barrierless rotary motors: the gear ratio that maximizes output power under tight coupling is also the ratio that requires the strongest coupling to remain locked. Consequently, weakening the coupling can invert the effect of gearing, transforming a power maximum into a local minimum. At strong driving, both the power-optimal and most slip-susceptible ratios approach symmetry match. Numerical solutions of the Fokker-Planck dynamics for rotors with finite energy barriers reproduce these predictions and reveal how the behavior changes when driving strength is fixed per rotation or per subunit. Motivated by the bacterial flagellar motor, these results identify coupling strength as a key determinant of whether matched rotational symmetries enhance or suppress energy transduction, providing a general design principle for coupled molecular motors.

### Paper [79]
Title: The Constructive Cavity Method for the Ising Perceptron
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39141
Authors: Simone Franchini, Francesco Concetti, Giampiero Bardella
Abstract: We apply the Constructive Cavity Method (CCM) to the Ising perceptron. The process starts from positive soft channels admitting an auxiliary representation $$ U(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}} e^{tx}\rho(\mathrm{d}t),\qquad \rho\ge0, $$ with an auxiliary measure having a finite exponential quadratic moment. This rewrites the perceptron as a dense, bipartite Gaussian model with an Ising layer and a continuous auxiliary layer. The CCM blanket geometry then gives two constructive variance profiles, $Q_\ell$ on the Ising side and $\Gamma_\ell$ on the auxiliary side. The bipartite shell scale is $$ Q_{\ell+1}\Gamma_{\ell+1}-Q_\ell\Gamma_\ell, $$ and Gaussian core comparison removes the core contribution $\Delta Q_\ell\Delta\Gamma_\ell$ under blanket refinement. The scalar cavity functional has three terms, $$ \mathcal{A}_{\mathrm{perc}}(\nu)=\mathbb{E}\left[\log\langle2\cosh x\rangle_\nu+\alpha\log\langle U(s)\rangle_\nu-\log\langle e^y\rangle_\nu\right], $$ where $x$ is the new-spin field, $s$ is the new-constraint field, and $y$ is the cavity correction. CCM identifies the constructive cavity variables with the Aizenman--Sims--Starr random overlap structure (ROSt) variables. Parisi's recursive averaging then gives $$ \mathcal{G}_{\mathrm{perc}}^{\mathrm{CCM}}(\zeta,Q,\Gamma)=X_0(0)+\alpha Y_0(0)-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\ell=0}^{R}\zeta_\ell\left(Q_{\ell+1}\Gamma_{\ell+1}-Q_\ell\Gamma_\ell\right). $$ The replica-symmetric specialization gives Gardner's formula for the generalized Ising perceptron. Specializing the constraint terminal to the hard channel gives the Krauth--M\'ezard functional for the binary perceptron.

### Paper [80]
Title: Atomic Charge Chirality from Electric Multipoles
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39150
Authors: Satoru Hayami, Rikuto Oiwa
Abstract: We establish an electric-multipole ($Q_{lm}$) criterion for chirality of local atomic charge distributions. For the 11 crystallographic chiral point groups, we identify representative lowest-rank multipole contents required to distinguish the opposite handednesses. For the axial groups $C_n$ and $D_n$ with $n=2,3,4,6$, chirality arises from the relative orientation of two azimuthal anisotropies with $m=n$, belonging to consecutive ranks $l=n$ and $n+1$. Their coexistence produces a latitude-dependent twist of the $n$-fold charge distribution. The polar component, although allowed in $C_n$, is distinct from this chiral mechanism and is not required for handedness. The cubic groups $T$ and $O$ exhibit distinct realizations, with octahedral chirality first appearing at rank nine. We further show that, within a spinless $s$-$p$-$d$-$f$ atomic basis, the scalar charge density contains multipoles only up to rank six and therefore cannot encode the handedness of $C_6$, $D_6$, or $O$. These results connect electric-multipole content and atomic orbital degrees of freedom to the microscopic encoding of crystallographic handedness.

### Paper [81]
Title: Berry curvature dipole circuit quantum electrodynamics
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39186
Authors: Oles Matsyshyn, Valla Fatemi, Justin C. W. Song
Abstract: Quantum three-wave mixing is an important resource in superconducting quantum circuits. The traditional implementation uses Josephson junction arrays and is activated by magnetic flux. Here we introduce the Berry curvature dipole (BCD) quantum circuit element, which achieves quantum three-wave mixing without breaking time-reversal symmetry. This quantum three-wave mixing results from the BCD nonlinear Hall effect in noncentrosymmetric quantum materials taken to a dissipationless, quantum coherent few-photon regime. We show how nonlinear Hall materials can be integrated to superconducting microwave circuits to accomplish multiphoton vacuum Rabi splittings and dynamical stabilization of bosonic Schr\"odinger cat states. Such BCD circuit quantum electrodynamics are a concrete exemplar of a physical resource for quantum information processing enabled by nonlinear response in quantum materials.

### Paper [82]
Title: Local Josephson response near nonmagnetic impurities and vortices in a two-orbital $s_{\pm}$ superconductor
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39192
Authors: Yuanyuan Zhao
Abstract: We study the local Josephson response near nonmagnetic impurities and vortices in a self-consistent two-orbital $s_{\pm}$ superconductor. For a local $s$-wave tip that couples equally to the two orbitals, anomalous spectral contributions of opposite sign nearly cancel in the clean sample. Nonmagnetic impurities redistribute these contributions and can reverse the local coupling without a corresponding $\pi$ phase shift of the bond pair fields. Negative coupling appears at off-center sites at weaker impurity potentials than at the impurity site. The impurity-induced change is concentrated at lower quasiparticle energies, while states over a broader energy range contribute to the total clean and impurity couplings. In zero field, the coupling also changes sign as the tip gap is varied, and increasing the tunneling strength can reverse the $m=1$ sine component of the current without changing the sample state. At the chosen coherence-peak energy, increasing impurity strength mainly changes the relative intensities of existing quasiparticle-interference features. In a self-consistent two-vortex state, the Josephson response develops a spatially varying phase relative to the condensate. The response and condensate share the winding close to each core, while larger loops enclose additional winding of the Josephson response. The local Josephson sign and phase reflect the anomalous spectrum and the tip--sample coupling.

### Paper [83]
Title: Nodal magnon spin current at an antiferromagnet/altermagnet junction
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39206
Authors: Koki Mizuno
Abstract: We investigate the thermally driven magnon spin current across a junction between a $d$-wave altermagnet (AM) and a ferromagnet (FM) or an antiferromagnet (AFM).
  The current consists of a component independent of the interface angle and an angularly dependent component with the $d$-wave form.
  For a sublattice-symmetric interface, the two junctions behave oppositely under reversal of the altermagnetic domain: in the FM/AM junction the angularly dependent component is odd and cancels on averaging over the domains, whereas in the AFM/AM junction the angularly independent component vanishes by symmetry and the angularly dependent component survives the domain average at a fixed N\'eel vector of the AFM.
  Both follow from a selective tunneling: a pair of spins bonded across the interface transmits magnons when the spins are parallel and not when antiparallel, which makes the AFM/AM junction a magnon valve: the configuration in which the bonded spins are antiparallel blocks the current up to the sublattice admixture of the magnons.
  The anisotropic tunneling is detectable as the sign reversal of the current between the interfaces normal to $[310]$ and $[130]$, and the valve reads the N\'eel vector of the altermagnet relative to that of the antiferromagnet through the magnitude of the current at a fixed interface.

### Paper [84]
Title: Lone-Pair Stereoactivity Overrides Mass Scaling of Thermal Expansion and Heat Conduction in Non-vander Waals Layered Bismuth Oxychalcogenides
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39213
Authors: Linzhen Li, Guangwu Zhang, Ziyan Qian, Wei Jing, Yang Lu, Jiaqi Gu, Kaifeng Huang, Yanguang Zhou, Qiye Zheng
Abstract: Heavier chalcogens are generally expected to soften isostructural semiconductor lattices, raising thermal expansion and lowering thermal conductivity. Layered bismuth oxychalcogenides, Bi2O2X (X = S, Se, Te), are mixed-anion crystals whose covalent slabs and ionic sheets are bound electrostatically rather than by van der Waals forces and whose Bi3+ 6s^2 lone pairs can become stereochemically active. Despite the rapid development of Bi2O2Se for fin and gate-all-around transistors, its fundamental thermal expansion and heat transport mechanisms remain poorly understood. Here, we show that lone-pair stereoactivity, not chalcogen mass, governs both properties. Path-integral molecular dynamics shows that the volumetric thermal expansion coefficient at 300 K decreases from 8.14 x 10^-5 K^-1 in Bi2O2S to 6.72 x 10^-5 and 6.61 x 10^-5 K^-1 in Bi2O2Se and Bi2O2Te, respectively, despite a 13% larger unit cell in the telluride, whereas substituting the S mass into Bi2O2Se changes its value by only 0.6%. Lone-pair-driven Bi off-centering in orthorhombic Bi2O2S makes b-axis expansion 1.63 times a-axis expansion yet leaves in-plane thermal conduction isotropic within 3%. Phonon transport calculations including four-phonon scattering and Wigner coherence predict a thermal conductivity maximum in Bi2O2Se (1.83 and 0.95 W m^-1 K^-1 in the in-plane and cross-plane directions, respectively, at 300 K) because Bi2O2S is limited by anharmonic scattering and Bi2O2Te by slow cross-plane phonons. Thermoreflectance measurements from 300 to 500 K and in situ X-ray diffraction on Bi2O2Se single crystals agree with these predictions within 6.4%. Phonon Boltzmann transport simulations of 12-nm-wide fins, which run hotter than Fourier diffusion predicts, show that Bi2O2Se minimizes the hotspot temperature and the thermal-mismatch strain at the gate dielectric.

### Paper [85]
Title: Colossal magnetoresistance in narrow-gap magnetic semiconductor Eu$_2$CdAs$_2$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39218
Authors: Zhiyu Zhou, Zhi-Cheng Wang, Xiyu Chen, Xin-Yu Zhao, Yaran Duan, Shiran Tao, Guang-Han Cao, Shuai Dong
Abstract: Discovering antiferromagnetic materials with a sizable colossal magnetoresistance (CMR) effect and precisely tuning their responses remain significant challenges in materials science. This work reports the synthesis, phase transitions, and magnetotransport properties of the narrow-gap Zintl arsenide Eu$_2$CdAs$_2$. The compound undergoes antiferromagnetic ordering at $T_\mathrm{N} = 9$ K and exhibits semiconducting behavior with a low-temperature activation energy of 3.2 meV, achieving a large CMR effect of $-10^4\%$ at 9 T. Below $T_\mathrm{N}$, a distinct butterfly-shaped magnetoresistance hysteresis emerges without any macroscopic ferromagnetic order, revealing a transport anomaly likely dictated by AFM domain dynamics and/or the formation and percolation of localized magnetic polarons. Furthermore, a systematic survey of reported Eu-based Zintl phases uncovers a nearly exponential scaling trend between the maximum CMR magnitude and the low-temperature activation energy. This correlation demonstrates that bandgap engineering serves as an effective strategy for tailoring magnetotransport, establishing Eu-containing Zintl phases as a versatile platform for exploring and manipulating CMR effects.

### Paper [86]
Title: Polarization-locked chiral persistent spin textures in two-dimensional lead-halide perovskites
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39250
Authors: Palanichamy Gayathri, Biplab Sanyal, Jonas Fransson
Abstract: Chiral spacer cations crystallize two-dimensional hybrid perovskites in the polar Sohncke group $P2_1$, which places the polar axis in the inorganic layer. We analyze the spin-orbit field that this geometry allows and test it against first-principles spin textures of ten polar chiral lead-halide perovskites, their mirror enantiomers and four halide-substituted analogues. The point group $C_2$ with the twofold axis in the layer forbids the helical Rashba field and allows a linear term $\gamma_1 k_x \sigma_z$, the Rashba interaction of the in-plane polar axis, together with chirality-related Dresselhaus and radial terms. Thirty-four two-band $k \cdot p$ models were fitted to each band pair and ranked by the agreement of their spin directions with the calculation rather than by the band energies, which cannot distinguish them. In all strongly polar compounds, the conduction band is described by the persistent term alone, with $\gamma_1 = 1.09$-$1.98$ eV {\AA} and a spin agreement of at least 0.98, whereas the best Rashba or Dresselhaus model reaches 0.20. The $R$ and $S$ enantiomers of each compound have opposite polarizations, and therefore opposite $\gamma_1$ and opposite persistent spin textures of equal magnitude, as required by the mirror that relates them; this holds in all ten compounds, so the choice of the enantiomer selects the spin direction. The magnitude of $\gamma_1$ correlates with the disparity of the in-plane Pb-X-Pb angles ($r = 0.95$). With electron masses of $0.26$-$0.43\,m_e$ along the spin-splitting directions, the associated persistent spin helices have wavelengths of 3.4-8.2 nm.

### Paper [87]
Title: Dissipation dilution enhances SiV coupling to vdW diamond nanomechanics
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39271
Authors: Guanhao Huang, Amberly Xie, Chang Jin, Evelyn Hu, Marko Lon\v{c}ar
Abstract: Solid-state color centers embedded into mechanical resonators, where orbital states mediate interactions between spins and long-lived phonons, are a promising platform for hybrid quantum systems. The negatively charged silicon vacancy center in diamond (SiV-) is particularly favorable for strain-coupling to nanomechanical resonators because of its large spin-strain susceptibility and compatibility with device integration. Tensioned nanomechanical resonators offer an attractive route due to their ultra-long mechanical coherence times enabled by diluting defect-induced dissipation, though it is unclear if the same dilution effect undermines strain-mediated coupling to material defect centers. Here, we experimentally demonstrate a tensioned diamond nanobeam platform that enables enhanced strain coupling to SiV- orbital states. Through monitoring the optical response of the SiVs to the static and dynamical strain of the mechanical resonator, we observe that dynamical strain localizes near the clamps of these resonators under static strain. This allows us to enhance SiV-mechanical coupling and the mechanical coherence simultaneously, and provide a SiV-strain sensitivity with greatly improved accuracy over previous literature values. Thus, our work opens a pathway towards seconds-long coherence hybrid quantum systems utilizing point defects in diamond nanomechanics for quantum information processing.

### Paper [88]
Title: Th$^{4+}$ ion cluster structures and electric field gradients in $^{229}$Th:CaF$_2$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39288
Authors: Yuxiang Li, Lin Li, Qiaorui Gong, Shanming Li, Chengchun Zhao, Lu Deng, Peixiong Zhang, Yin Hang, Longsheng Ma, Shining Zhu
Abstract: The low-energy nuclear isomeric transition of $^{229}$Th is a promising frequency reference for a solid-state nuclear optical clock. Wide-band-gap fluoride crystals such as CaF$_2$ offer transparent hosts for embedding Th nuclei at high density. Substitution of Th$^{4+}$ for Ca$^{2+}$ requires charge compensation, linking the local defect structure to nuclear quadrupole splitting. We use first-principles calculations to examine neutral Th dimers compensated by four interstitial F ions ($\mathrm{F}_{i}^{-}$). Screening different compensation geometries by their relative formation energies identifies a family of low-energy configurations with closely similar local structures. These configurations share large electric field gradients and small asymmetry parameters, consistent with the experimental high-EFG site 3. Their calculated quadrupole splittings are further compared with the measured C13 spectra. Together, the energetic and spectroscopic results support $\mathrm{F}_{i}^{-}$-compensated Th dimers as candidate microscopic structures for site 3 in Th:CaF$_2$.

### Paper [89]
Title: Ten-loop fixed-dimension renormalization-group functions of the three-dimensional O(N) model from distributed GPU-accelerated parametric integration
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39307
Authors: Paolo Ribeca
Abstract: The renormalization-group functions $\beta$, $\eta$ and $\nu$ of the three-dimensional $O(N)$ model in fixed dimension (massive zero-momentum scheme) were known to eighth order in the coupling. We extend them to tenth order, and $\beta$ to eleventh, for general $N$, which requires the two- and four-point functions to ten loops (1,593,254 graphs at the last order), and re-evaluate all lower orders. Through the Callan-Symanzik equation every coefficient follows from per-order sums that are linear in finite, zero-momentum $\bar R$-subtracted parametric integrals, one per graph, which we sample on GPUs by tropical Monte Carlo with densities adapted to the forest terms. The computation reproduces the known sums from two to seven loops within three standard deviations and agrees at ninth and tenth order, at lower precision, with an independent route through an intermediate scheme. The new coefficients have relative uncertainties of a few $10^{-5}$ or better, and the eighth-order coefficients of $\eta$ and $\nu$ improve by factors of 120 and 4. The time needed to integrate an order to fixed relative precision grows by only 2.4-2.9 per loop from seven loops on, while the construction of the integrands still grows factorially and, at tenth order, costs more than the integration. Resummed, the tenth-order series give $\nu=0.62997(51)$, $\eta=0.0333(18)$ and $\omega=0.803(14)$ for $N=1$, against $0.62997097(12)$, $0.036297612(48)$ and $0.82968(23)$ from the conformal bootstrap; at $N=3$ the deviations grow, so that the uncertainty is now dominated by the resummation rather than by the coefficients. Every result is generated automatically from, or checked against, a public ledger of independent, exactly pooled runs, hosted on GitHub, to which anyone is welcome to contribute computing time.

### Paper [90]
Title: A Zoo of Fermionic Relative R\'enyi Negativities in the Sachdev-Ye-Kitaev Models
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39349
Authors: Linjing Jiang, Pengfei Zhang
Abstract: Quantum entanglement is a fundamental resource that gives rise to correlations with no classical counterpart in strongly correlated fermionic systems. However, its characterization in mixed quantum states remains challenging. Established entanglement diagnostics, such as logarithmic negativity, are computationally demanding in interacting systems, and their evaluation by exact diagonalization is restricted to small system sizes. Meanwhile, conventional R\'enyi proxies, including twisted and untwisted R\'enyi negativities, can be positive even for separable states. In this work, we introduce a family of \textit{relative R\'enyi negativities} that serve as entanglement witnesses. By relating fermionic partial time reversal to the size superoperator, we establish a path-integral representation for these R\'enyi negativities, enabling their evaluation in thermal states of the Sachdev-Ye-Kitaev (SYK) model and its generalizations via a large-$N$ expansion. Applying this method, we investigate entanglement in both the original SYK model and the Maldacena-Qi coupled SYK model. We identify a special class of relative R\'enyi negativities which serve as plausible proxies for logarithmic negativity and uncover first-order entanglement transitions that are invisible to simple observables. These results highlight the utility of relative R\'enyi negativities as probes of mixed-state entanglement in strongly correlated fermionic systems and suggest the existence of a new class of transitions where these negativities serve as free energies.

### Paper [91]
Title: Metastability, collective excitations, and traveling states of one-dimensional spin-orbit-coupled quantum droplets
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39361
Authors: Harsimranjit Kaur,  Ritu,  Rajat, Kuldeep Suthar, Sandeep Gautam
Abstract: We study the static and traveling droplet phases of spin-orbit coupled, one-dimensional Bose-Bose mixtures with a Lee-Huang-Yang correction. For the static droplets, employing a variational analysis for an infinitely extended mixture, we show that the system can feature two ground-state droplet phases: a stripe droplet phase up to a critical Rabi coupling strength, at which it undergoes a first-order transition to a zero-momentum droplet phase. The variational results for the ground-state properties are shown to be in good agreement with numerical solutions for droplets in the thermodynamic limit as well as finite-size droplets in the "flat-top regime". For finite-size droplets, the stripe phase can feature multiple stationary states with different numbers of peaks in their stripe density patterns. The calculation of the excitation spectrum for these stationary states confirms their stability. To study the moving droplets, we investigate the ground-state phases of the system in the droplet's comoving frame. We show that in the comoving frame, droplets acquire a finite longitudinal magnetization. As a consequence, the phase diagram features two distinct phases: a traveling stripe droplet and a plane-wave droplet. The real-time evolution of these droplets in the space-fixed frame, complemented by the Bogoliubov analysis in the comoving frame, illustrates the stability of these traveling droplet phases.

### Paper [92]
Title: Aging and protocol dependence of thermal activation events in structural glasses
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39405
Authors: Yuki Takaha, Atsushi Ikeda
Abstract: In this study, we investigate the waiting time dependence and the protocol dependence of thermally activated events in structural glasses using molecular simulations and potential energy landscape analysis. We find a qualitative crossover in the nature of activation events; from avalanche-like events to local events. We also use the isoconfigurational ensemble analysis and reveal that the events become less diverse and more reversible. We discuss these crossovers from the perspective of the stability and the potential energy landscape of glasses. To investigate the protocol dependence, we compare the two types of glasses whose stability is controlled by waiting time and parent temperature, respectively. Surprisingly, at the same inherent structure energy level, the parent-temperature-controlled glasses are more unstable and maintain their avalanche-like nature. These results demonstrate that the activation properties of structural glasses are not determined solely by their energy levels, highlighting the essential role of state connectivity and the dynamical sampling in the complex energy landscape.

### Paper [93]
Title: Graphene Islands as a Platform for Band-Structure Engineering
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39428
Authors: Yonah Mann, Tobias Holder
Abstract: Multilayer graphenes have emerged as a versatile avenue towards exploring novel electronic phases of matter. Recently, nanoscale multilayer graphene islands have been realized experimentally, where the island is less than 50 nm in diameter and distinct in layer configuration from the contact portion of the same graphene sheets, the latter of which act as leads for transport probes. Here, we theoretically investigate how the proximity between these islands and their chemically identical environment modifies the electronic structure. Within the tight-binding approximation, we find that the proximity effect is enhanced by the closely matched momenta between both parts, creating an unusually strong band renormalization which persists as the island size is increased, and only limited by the coherence length. An effective single-unit-cell Hamiltonian to capture these modifications is derived, expressed in terms of interlayer hopping parameters that scale with the relative island area. We predict a substantially altered low-energy density of states for 3- and 4-layer graphene islands, demonstrating the tunability of the electronic structure by spatial size.

### Paper [94]
Title: Hop rates of H and D on Pt(111) at finite coverage: the memory equation and the collective friction
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39436
Authors: S. Miret-Art\'es
Abstract: Helium scattering measures the coherent intermediate scattering function of an adlayer, the characteristic function of the separation between adparticles at two times. Published dephasing rates of H and D on Pt(111) are analysed with its memory equation and a nearest-neighbour Ising lattice gas. The measured rate belongs to the adlayer. The hop rate of a single adsorbate is extracted from it by dividing out an interaction factor, without a model of the barrier. This factor lies between $0.97$ and $1.17$ at $0.1$~ML, so that the published single-adsorbate analyses hold there. At $0.4$~ML and $80$~K it lies between $1.2$ and $4.7$, depending on how the occupied neighbours modify a tunnelling jump. The memory function is interpreted as a friction, the collective friction, which the adlayer exerts on its own density fluctuations. The lateral coupling, fixed by the coverage series, is the free energy of a neighbouring pair, with a vibrational pair entropy. The small deformation of the measured curves from the Chudley--Elliott form, the tilt, is proposed as an observable of the coupling. The tilts at $0.15$ and $0.2$~ML are consistent with it, although they are hard to extract from the published curves. The memory equation provides a transition-state theory for the adlayer, with the collective friction in the role of the friction of the substrate and without divergence at the crossover. Combined with the standard quantum transition-state theory of a single adsorbate, the extracted hop rates give the friction of the substrate, $124$ and $75$~ps$^{-1}$ for H and D, independent of the coverage.

### Paper [95]
Title: Resisting Drying Stress in Laponite Bentonite Clay Composites, Two Step Yielding and Crack Peel Selection
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39465
Authors: Tapati Dutta, Agnishikha Chakrabarti, Soham Mitra, Deepshikha Das, Indranil Chakraborty
Abstract: Clay films that dry on a rigid substrate crack, peel or stay intact, and which of these happens matters for coatings, barrier layers and ceramic processing. Composites of clay with polymers have been studied widely. Clay composites, whose components differ in size and charge, are much less explored. We show that mixing the nano scale clay Laponite with the micron scale clay Bentonite reduces desiccation damage on both a hydrophobic and a hydrophilic substrate, Laponite films stay essentially intact, Bentonite films fracture extensively, and on glass most intermediate compositions are less damaged than a simple average of the two would suggest. Rheology of the gelled suspensions reveals a two step yielding in every composition, and the cohesive energy derived from it depends by orders of magnitude on which step is used, a point that any rheology cracking correlation must specify. A dimensionless cohesion adhesion index, whose sign we derive from an energy balance, is consistent with compositions that peel on polypropylene and crack on glass. Replicate experiments were performed for robustness of results. The results identify composition as a simple handle on the crack resistance of clay films and open the search for the critical crack-free thickness as a function of clay composition.

### Paper [96]
Title: Predicting unstable planar fault energies in multicomponent alloys from pure elemental components
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39495
Authors: C Tang, J Liu, J Yi, M R Barnett
Abstract: The unstable planar fault energies (UPFEs) govern slip and twinning but are not experimentally measurable. Using EAM potentials complemented by DFT, we investigate their temperature and compositional dependence and their relationship with the shear modulus. For pure \textit{fcc} elements with low stacking fault energies (SFEs), UPFEs are substantially less temperature-sensitive than SFEs. The reported linear relationship between UPFEs and the product of shear modulus and Burgers vector ($\mu b_{\mathrm{p}}$) holds for close-packed elements and solid solutions, but deviates for intermetallics and non-close-packed elements. In the Co-Cr-Fe-Mn-Ni system, binary alloys show composition-dependent deviations, while the correlation strengthens with increasing chemical complexity. Furthermore, $\mu b_{\mathrm{p}}$ of multicomponent alloys linearly correlates with the composition-weighted elemental $\mu b_{\mathrm{p}}$ ($R^2=0.95$), despite a systematic negative deviation. This provides a simple, computationally inexpensive route for estimating $\mu b_{\mathrm{p}}$ and screening UPFEs directly from pure elemental constituents.

### Paper [97]
Title: It\^o versus Stratonovich for shot noise with state-dependent amplitude: why the answer is Marcus
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39511
Authors: Marco Bianucci, Riccardo Mannella
Abstract: For Gaussian white noise the It\^o and Stratonovich prescriptions differ by $O(dt)$ per time step, which accumulates into the familiar spurious drift $\tfrac12 D\,II'$. We show that for renewal shot noise with state-dependent gain, $\dot x=-C(x)-I(x)\xi[t]$ with $\xi[t]$ a train of Dirac spikes of finite random amplitude, the two prescriptions differ by a finite amount at every event, and that where $I$ vanishes the difference can become qualitative. The Marcus kick, the jump-process counterpart of the Stratonovich prescription, is the flow map of $dx/ds=I(x)$: no kick can cross a zero $x^\ast$ of $I$, for any jump and waiting-time statistics. The It\^o kick is only the tangent map of that flow. For $I(x)=1+\beta x$ it crosses $x^\ast=-1/\beta$ if and only if $\xi\beta>1$ and collapses the whole line onto $x^\ast$ at $\xi\beta=1$; below threshold it is itself a Marcus kick with a remapped jump law. Bounded jump laws thus have a threshold, while unbounded ones leak across the barrier for \emph{any} parameters. The discrepancy cannot be absorbed into a renormalised drift beyond lowest order, and only the Marcus kick is generated by a single operator, which makes the master equation of Bianucci, Bologna and Mannella (2026) intrinsically Marcus. Numerically, the standard Heun kick is a second-order truncation of the Marcus map that preserves the barrier only because its order is even; it shortens every jump, biasing second moments in proportion to the squared jump amplitude and the jump kurtosis, and depleting the region near the barrier. We give the exact-flow integrator that replaces it.

### Paper [98]
Title: Tunable periodic cascade of delocalization transitions in non-Hermitian IAAF model
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39514
Authors: Yi Xue, Yunhua Wang
Abstract: Cascade of delocalization transitions provides a route to multi-step control of delocalization, yet achieving a programmable periodic cascade remains a challenge. Here, we demonstrate a tunable periodic cascade of delocalization transitions in a non-Hermitian interpolating Aubry-Andr\'{e}-Fibonacci (IAAF) model by introducing an imaginary phase shift into the quasiperiodic potential. Such a periodic cascade of delocalization transitions arises from the competition between kinetic-energy-facilitated delocalization and gain-loss-regulated localization. In numerical static localization characterizations, successive regular stripe patterns for localized and extended states emerge in phase diagrams. We also derive an analytical expression for the cascade period and find that it relies solely on the imaginary phase shift. This provides a direct way of continuously tuning the cascade period via a single control parameter. Complementary static and dynamical diagnostics consistently confirm this periodic cascade of delocalization transitions in the non-Hermitian IAAF system. Our findings identify a novel class of delocalization transitions with tunable cascading periods, opening a route toward experimentally programmable control of delocalization.

### Paper [99]
Title: Thermalization-Controlled Superconducting Diode-Like Transport in Disordered Molybdenum and NbN Thin Films
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39523
Authors: Akhilesh Kumar, Shivani Soni, Gomathi Natarajan, Ramanathaswamy Pandian, Thameemul Ansari M, Pooja Gupta, Sanjay Rai, Edward Prabu Amaladass
Abstract: Superconducting diode effects are characterized by direction-dependent critical currents in the superconducting state. Here, we investigate diode-like transport in 128-nm-thick Mo thin film deposited on Si_SiO2 substrate by DC magnetron sputtering. The thin film exhibit a superconducting transition temperature TC of 5.8 K. Pronounced current-direction asymmetry and hysteresis are observed in current-voltage characteristics measured in a closed-cycle refrigerator. In contrast, the same thin film exhibits a substantially more symmetric response in a 14T cryogenic system with improved sample thermalization. We attribute this strong dependence on the measurement environment to electrothermal effects arising from current-induced Joule heating and finite thermal relaxation. As the current approaches the superconducting-to-resistive switching condition, the emergence of a resistive component leads to a rapid increase in Joule heating, raising the sample temperature and modifying the switching condition. Conversely, during the resistive-to-superconducting transition, residual heating and finite thermal relaxation reduce the switching current. Since P is equal to I2R it is independent of current polarity, the observed asymmetry is not associated with polarity-dependent Joule heating but with the interplay of thermal relaxation, sweep history, and thermal boundary conditions. The suppression of the asymmetry under improved thermalization, together with similar behavior observed in NbN reference films, indicates that the apparent diode-like response is strongly influenced by electrothermal effects. These results highlight the need to distinguish thermal switching and hysteresis from intrinsic nonreciprocal superconductivity when identifying superconducting diode effects in transport measurements.

### Paper [100]
Title: Multiple Current Enhancement Regimes and Interaction Induced Re-entrant Transport in a Quasiperiodic Hubbard Ring with Antiferromagnetic Order
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39529
Authors: Souvik Roy, Ranjini Bhattacharya
Abstract: We investigate persistent charge and spin currents in a one-dimensional quantum ring threaded by an Aharonov-Bohm (AB) flux, considering generalized Aubry-Andr\'e-Harper (AAH) modulation with staggered onsite energies and antiferromagnetic (AFM) ordering in both the noninteracting and interacting regimes, with the latter treated using a self-consistent Hartree-Fock mean field theory (MFT). At half filling, two distinct current-enhancement peaks emerge as the modulation strength increases, with quasiperiodic hopping modulation playing a crucial role in establishing the two-peak structure within a restricted parameter regime. Spin-resolved bond-current profiles reveal a switch in the dominant spin contribution between the two peaks, while the inverse participation ratio (IPR) and flux-dependent spin-resolved ground-state energies provide complementary insights into the spatial characteristics of the eigenstates and their spin-dependent flux sensitivity. At quarter filling, the spin current exhibits a single enhancement peak, in contrast to the two-peak response observed at half filling. The Hubbard interaction affects the two half-filled peaks differently: it suppresses the primary peak, whereas the secondary peak exhibits a nonmonotonic, re-entrant response, with the current recovering at finite interaction strength. The interacting results are further characterized through the Drude weight, and comparisons with exact diagonalization (ED) and density-matrix renormalization group (DMRG) calculations for a finite half-filled system support the reliability of the MFT description. Our findings elucidate how quasiperiodic modulation, AFM order, band filling, and electronic correlations collectively govern the magnitude and spin-resolved character of persistent currents, giving rise to multiple enhancement regimes and interaction-induced re-entrant behavior.

### Paper [101]
Title: Edge-Plasmon-Induced Magnetization in Higher Chern Insulators
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39555
