Authors: Prathyush P. Poduval,  Rofii, Muhammad S. Ukhtary, Thomas L. Schmidt, Eddwi H. Hasdeo
Abstract: Higher Chern insulators host multiple dissipationless chiral edge states, one per unit of Chern number. We study the collective plasmon modes of these edge states at a domain wall between Chern numbers $\mathcal{C}$ and $-\mathcal{C}$, using a semiclassical model with Coulomb interactions. The plasmon dispersion is linear at long wavelengths, with a velocity that increases linearly with $\mathcal{C}$. As in conventional two-dimensional metals, the edge plasmon generates an elliptically polarized electric field that decays into the bulk and drives electrons in circular orbits, producing a static orbital magnetization (inverse Faraday effect). For topological edge plasmons, the magnetization has a nontrivial spatial profile set by two competing length scales: the finite extent of the edge states and the plasmon wavelength. At fixed edge-charge amplitude, it grows strongly with $\mathcal{C}$. At the domain wall, it approaches a finite plateau at low frequency and decays as $1/\omega^2$ at high frequency. Its temperature dependence is nonmonotonic, with a maximum at an intermediate temperature. These results show how the Chern number controls plasmon-induced magnetization, in contrast to conventional edge plasmons.

### Paper [102]
Title: Diffusion models for the $O(3)$ nonlinear sigma model
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39568
Authors: Daniel Schubring
Abstract: A score-based diffusion model is trained on the two-dimensional classical Heisenberg model, the lattice representation of the $O(3)$ nonlinear sigma model. Neural networks are trained on three different coarse-grainings of the same training set to implement a multiscale sampling scheme that modestly improves both the accuracy and computational cost of direct sampling on the finest lattice. Stein (or Schwinger-Dyson) diagnostics for the two-point correlation function are introduced to assess the quality of the learned score without relying on reverse diffusion sampling. These diagnostics are also incorporated into an additional training loss. We discuss several technical considerations relevant to implementing diffusion models on $S^2$. These include a consideration of lifting the network input to $S^3$, as in the $CP^1$ representation, and developing a boundary-layer approximation to the short-time spherical heat kernel that extends its usefulness to larger diffusion times.

### Paper [103]
Title: Hidden Phase Transition in Single-Polymer Stretching under Elongational Flow
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39575
Authors: Aiqing Zhu, Fushuai Wang, Eleonore Vissol-Gaudin, Qianxiao Li, Beatrice W. Soh
Abstract: Using machine learning, we identify a second transition in polymer chain stretching driven by planar elongational flow, beyond the well-known coil-stretch transition. We achieve this by developing a conditional extension of Universal and Identifiable Dissipative Dynamics (UIDD) that jointly learns reduced stochastic dynamics from microscopic trajectories across flow strengths and yields the corresponding stationary nonequilibrium free energy landscapes. As the flow strength increases, the evolving landscape exhibits the expected coil-stretch transition, and then a previously unrecognized dynamical transition. In the latter, the stretched states persist, but the distribution along a folding coordinate becomes bimodal when conditioned on small end-to-end separation. Large-deviation analysis of a simplified analytical chain model reproduces both transitions and shows that they are phase transitions in the long-chain limit. Single-molecule DNA experiments provide qualitative support for the dynamical transition through the predicted reversal of the folding response.

### Paper [104]
Title: Spin-Polarized Weyl Fermions and Unconventional Spin Transport
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39604
Authors: Chenhao Liang, Haohao Sheng, Sheng Zhang, Zhong Fang, Hongming Weng, Zhijun Wang
Abstract: Large and electrically controllable spin splitting in nonmagnetic materials is highly desirable for condensed-matter physics and spintronics, enabling strongly spin-polarized quasiparticles and unconventional spin transport as well as their electrical control. Here, we establish a general framework for critical spin-polarized Weyl fermions in two-dimensional (2D) nonmagnetic systems. We predict the $\alpha$-Bi-like Group-V puckered-layer family of 2D ferroelectrics as a material platform combining critical spin-Weyl fermions, giant spin splitting, and electrically switchable nonlinear and spin transport. Our calculations reveal exchange-scale spin splitting induced by spin-orbit coupling and a $p$-wave spin texture in the BiAs monolayer. Moreover, BiAs hosts four critical spin-Weyl points, each formed within a single-spin channel. The nearly quantized $\langle S_z\rangle$ is protected by an emergent $S_z$-conservation symmetry in the low-energy limit. The spin-Weyl cones also give rise to unconventional spin-transport phenomena, including a purely out-of-plane Edelstein response and a pronounced spin Nernst effect. Ferroelectric reversal further enables electrical switching of the $p$-wave spin texture and inversion-odd responses, while the tunability of these properties through composition, strain, and magnetic field is also systematically demonstrated across the entire family. These results establish both a general nonmagnetic framework and a promising material platform for exploring critical spin-Weyl physics, nonlinear Hall phenomena, and unconventional spin transport.

### Paper [105]
Title: Fractal dimensions of paths on three-dimensional random-walk traces
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39612
Authors: Qiyuan Shi, Xinyi Li, Youjin Deng
Abstract: We study four paths that join the starting site to the final position of a simple random walk of \(N=L^2\) steps on the infinite cubic lattice. The paths are selected on the walk trace---the graph of visited sites and traversed bonds---by different rules: the undirected shortest path \(S_1\), the directed shortest path \(S_2\), the time-respecting shortest path \(S_3\), and the depth \(D_e\) of the final site in a depth-first-search tree. Monte Carlo simulations for \(L\) up to 32768 give mean lengths scaling as \(L^{d_O}\), with \(d_{S_1}=1.48005(9)\), \(d_{S_2}=1.49868(5)\), \(d_{S_3}=1.51205(5)\), and \(d_{D_e}=1.7099(7)\), so that \(d_{S_1}<d_{D_e}\), where $d_{\rm LE}=1.62400(5)$ is the known loop-erased-random-walk exponent. Imposing traversal directions increases the shortest-path exponent, and imposing time order increases it further; these constraints therefore change the scaling exponent, not only the amplitude. For every path, the standard deviation scales with the same exponent as the mean, so it is scale-invariant, unlike the visited-site mass; the rescaled length distributions collapse onto a finite-width scaling function. No analytical theory currently predicts these exponents; our results provide precise numerical benchmarks for future studies of path geometry on random-walk traces.

### Paper [106]
Title: Ultrasound-Assisted Facile Nanoconfinement of Luminescent Guests in Zirconium Metal-Organic Frameworks
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39622
Authors: Yiyi Wang, James D. Taylor, Jin-Chong Tan
Abstract: An accelerated sonochemical route for fabricating luminescent guest-MOF (LG@MOF) composites is demonstrated, for which light-emitting molecules are nanoconfined within porous zirconium-based hosts using probe ultrasonication rather than conventional bath sonication. Ultrasonication enables the rapid synthesis of crystalline UiO-66 and UiO-67 nanocrystals within 10 minutes, with material yields of up to 92%, and facile in situ guest encapsulation within 1 hour, hence notably faster than the traditional 24-hour solvothermal route. Using UiO-66 and the larger-pore UiO-67 as porous hosts, tuneable single- and dual-guest fluorescent host-guest systems incorporating Rhodamine B (RB) and Fluorescein (F) were achieved. Photophysical characterisation showed broadband emission, tuneable colours, high quantum yield and improved lifetime, highlighting the synergy between sonochemical conditions, structural defects, and photophysical behaviour. Additionally, the sonochemically prepared LG@UiO particles show well-defined crystal facets and potential as photoactive thin films for solid-state lighting, demonstrating their promise as a tuneable light source for optical sensing and nanophotonics.

### Paper [107]
Title: Origin and photoluminescence of a shallow hydrogen-related donor in silicon
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39623
Authors: Miaomiao Han, Peter Deak, Bing Huang, Adam Gali
Abstract: Paramagnetic color centers in silicon are being investigated recently for use in quantum technologies. While the origin of most of the prominent photoluminescence (PL) lines are well understood, that is not the case for those connected to hydrogen, even though it is almost always present in silicon, e.g., shallow hydrogen-related donors (SHDs), but their atomic structure remains elusive. Recent experiments suggested connection between the SHDs and the so-called X and the B$_i$ (or X$_i$) PL-lines. An SHD, termed the MT2 center, was tentatively assigned to a bistable, negative-U defect, arising due to the interaction of hydrogen with the self-interstitial complex I$_4$. Using high-level density functional theory calculations, we investigate the interaction of hydrogen with self-interstitial complexes In. A shallow effective-mass-theory donor arises when hydrogen attacks a Si-Si bond in I$_n$, inserting itself as a bond-center interstitial (I$_n$H$_{BC}$) in an exothermic reaction. We demonstrate this in the case of a single self-interstitial and of a complex of four. The latter I$_\mathrm{4}$H$_\mathrm{BC}$ complex shows electronic, spin and PL properties remarkably close to MT2. We find that hydrogen in the presence of I$_4$ follows the same behavior as in pristine silicon, i.e., it is a negative-U bistable defect, only detectable under illumination. In the I$_4$H$_{BC}$ complex, H can hop via photo-assisted mechanism between equivalent BC sites of the I$_4$ complex, leading to a motional averaged tetragonal symmetry as experimentally observed for MT2. The zero-phonon line is distinct from the X-line (attributed to the bare I$_4$ complex), and falls well into the range of the X$_i$ lines. Our results identify the I$_4$H complex as a prototype for a class of SHDs, inferring that similar SHDs may arise, with the X$_i$ lines as their PL fingerprints, when H attacks I$_n$ clusters.

### Paper [108]
Title: Fluctuation-dissipation relation in branching particle systems
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39624
Authors: Tom Michoel
Abstract: A fluctuation-dissipation relation (FDR) for branching particle systems is derived that reveals how fluctuations in clonal populations originating from a single ancestor encode both population growth and the dissipative structure of the single-particle dynamics. For particles whose internal states evolve according to a stationary Markov process, the FDR holds in both equilibrium and nonequilibrium stationary states. It yields an upper bound on the spectral gap that is constrained by the magnitude of clonal fluctuations and implies that clonal dynamics is asymptotically dominated by the slowest modes of the single-particle dynamics. This provides a theoretical explanation for the persistence of clonal memory and for experimentally observed clonal cofluctuations between variables with zero covariance in the single-particle stationary state. In nonequilibrium stationary states, the FDR predicts oscillatory clonal fluctuations, suggesting a route to measuring nonequilibrium fluxes in proliferating cell populations without single-cell tracking.

### Paper [109]
Title: Non-Volatile Memory Devices for Neuromorphic Computing
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39625
Authors: Debasis Das
Abstract: This chapter explores the critical role of emerging non-volatile memory (eNVM) devices in advancing neuromorphic computing. Inspired by the human brain's remarkable ability to perform complex tasks like pattern recognition and adaptation, neuromorphic computing offers immense potential for implementing complex neural networks (NNs) in real hardware. Despite ongoing research into the brain's complexities, its fundamental operation relies on neurons (processing units communicating via electrochemical signals) and synapses (junctions between neurons). The modifiable strength of the synapse is key to learning and memory functionality, allowing the brain to adapt and rewire itself. For dense and low-power circuits in neuromorphic computing, these eNVM devices are ideal due to their synaptic-like behavior and non-volatile information storage. Properties such as conductance modulation of these devices emulate synaptic plasticity, which is crucial for learning, and their scalability makes them promising for large-scale neuromorphic architectures. This chapter will delve into the various types of eNVM devices such as resistive RAM (ReRAM), phase-change memory (PCM), spintronics-based memory devices, and their applications in neuromorphic computing, discussing their challenges and opportunities, and highlighting recent advances in their integration within neuromorphic systems.

### Paper [110]
Title: Temporal Correlations and Optimal Feedback Control of an Active Information Engine
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39632
Authors: Laura Hoek, Yael Roichman
Abstract: How much does a feedback controller need to know about the fluctuations it exploits? We address this question using a macroscopic information engine driven by self-propelled bristle-robots. Experimentally measured occupancy time series show that temporal correlations strongly affect vacancy detection. At short measurement intervals, mean first-passage times exceed independent-measurement predictions by more than an order of magnitude. A first-order Markov approximation captures much of this effect. It does not reproduce the full first-passage distribution, revealing higher-order temporal memory. Nevertheless, Markov-selected protocols come within 0.15\% of the maximum experimental power. Neglecting correlations causes power losses of several percent and favors larger compression distances. Introducing a sensing cost shifts the optimal strategy toward sparse sensing. The independent model cannot predict this shift, whereas the Markov model captures it. Cross-validation on separate time segments confirms the near-optimal performance of Markov-selected protocols. These findings demonstrate that near-optimal feedback control in correlated nonequilibrium systems can require a simpler statistical description than predicting their full temporal dynamics.

### Paper [111]
Title: High-Field Heat Capacity of Atacamite Cu$_2$Cl(OH)$_3$ for $\mathbf{H} \parallel b$ axis: From Antiferromagnetic Order to Correlated Disorder
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39669
Authors: L. St\"odter, A. Demuer, T. Kotte, A. U. B. Wolter, B. B\"uchner, K. C. Rule, S. S\"ullow
Abstract: We present the heat capacity of the frustrated sawtooth-chain compound atacamite Cu$_2$Cl(OH)$_3$ in high magnetic fields up to 35 T for $\mathbf{H} \parallel b$ axis. We clearly observe the change from antiferromagnetic long-range order, indicated by a sharp transition anomaly shifted to lower temperatures when increasing field, to a field-induced spin-liquid-like state with the absence of any transition anomaly. Here, in highest magnetic fields above 32 T a broad anomaly is present which is shifted to higher temperatures as the field is increased. The low-temperature heat capacity in the high-field region can be described well by an exponential behavior indicating a thermal activation over a spin gap. The findings are consistent with our scenario for the high-field state in atacamite for $\mathbf{H} \parallel c$ axis, where we proposed the sawtooth chains to decouple into two spin subsystems, basal and apical spin sites, leading to a partially polarized state with $M \sim M_\mathrm{sat}/2$.

### Paper [112]
Title: Exceptional Superconducting Interfaces between Graphene and Atomic Chains in A15-MoRe
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39694
Authors: Hiroto Ogura, Yuto Tsukidate, Shun Kondo, Zhuoqun Li, Natsuki Sasaki, Tsunenobu Sato, Azusa Utsumi, Tomohiro Otsuka, Mitsuhiro Saito, Kazutoshi Inoue, Takashi Taniguchi, Kenji Watanabe, Yuichi Ikuhara, Toshiaki Kato
Abstract: Realizing highly transparent and magnetic-field-resilient superconducting contacts is a critical bottleneck for the advancement of two-dimensional (2D) quantum devices. Molybdenum-rhenium (MoRe) alloys have been empirically favored as they uniquely yield ballistic, highly transparent contacts to graphene that survive in high-magnetic-field regimes; however, the microscopic origin of this anomalous transparency has remained a long-standing puzzle. Here, we resolve this paradox by revealing that pristine graphene/MoRe interfaces are governed by the classical A15 superconducting phase. Combining low-temperature transport, X-ray diffraction, and atomic-resolution electron microscopy, we demonstrate that as-deposited MoRe spontaneously forms the A15 phase in direct contact with graphene, whereas thermal annealing triggers a phase transition to a hexagonal close-packed (hcp) structure that severely degrades the Josephson transparency. Crucially, the one-dimensional (1D) transition-metal atomic chains unique to the A15 structure exhibit a near-perfect lattice match (mismatch < 1%) with graphene edges. First-principles calculations further prove that the d-orbitals of these historically renowned 1D chains possibly hybridize with graphene's Dirac cone near the K point. These findings not only uncover the structural and electronic origins of optimal MoRe contacts but also establish A15-phase superconductors as a geometrically ideal, universal platform for realizing robust quantum electronics based on 2D materials.

### Paper [113]
Title: Observation of light-induced topological state in graphene
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39695
Authors: Sotirios Fragkos, Ann Chantal Goutier, Umberto Dellasette, Dominique Descamps, St\'ephane Petit, Wiebke Bennecke, Philip Hofmann, Marcel Reutzel, Stefan Mathias, Yann Mairesse, Michael Sch\"uler, Samuel Beaulieu
Abstract: Driving quantum materials with light can reshape not only their electronic band structures but also the geometry of their electronic wavefunctions. In graphene, circularly polarized light is predicted to open gaps at the Dirac points and induce a pseudospin texture and Berry curvature characteristic of the Haldane model, realizing a Floquet Chern insulator. Transport and all-optical measurements have provided evidence for a light-induced anomalous Hall response, but cannot resolve the underlying electronic states in both energy and momentum space. Time- and angle-resolved photoemission spectroscopy has revealed Floquet-Bloch sidebands and hybridization gaps at crossings between Floquet bands. However, these experiments used linearly polarized driving, which preserves time-reversal symmetry and thus does not induce a topological phase. Here, we combine circular-dichroic time-resolved extreme-ultraviolet momentum microscopy with ab initio theory to investigate graphene driven by circularly polarized light. We observe a pump-induced photoemission circular dichroism near the Dirac point that reverses sign with pump helicity and across the Dirac energy, in agreement with our calculations. These results link the measured dichroic response to the light-induced pseudospin texture and its associated Berry curvature, providing spectroscopic evidence for the Floquet-engineered topological insulating state in graphene.

### Paper [114]
Title: Pulse Laser Deposited GaFeO3 Thin Film Grown on FTO: Correlated Ferroic Orders and Strong Magnetodielectric Coupling at Room Temperature
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39696
Authors: Ishita Jana, Sourav Sarkar, Soham Saha, Kalyan Mandal
Abstract: Room-temperature single-phase multiferroics exhibiting coupled ferroelectric and magnetic order are promising candidates for next-generation magnetoelectric memory and spintronic technologies. Here, we report the growth of approximately 100.6 nm thick polycrystalline orthorhombic GaFeO3 thin films on fluorine-doped tin oxide-coated glass (FTO) substrates by pulsed laser deposition. Core-level X-ray photoelectron spectroscopy reveals a reduced Fe2+ concentration and suppressed oxygen-vacancy formation, contributing to reduced leakage current and improved ferroelectric characteristics. The films exhibit robust ferrimagnetism and ferroelectricity at room temperature, confirming the coexistence of ferroic orders within a single phase. Piezoresponse Force Microscopy further demonstrates reversible local polarization switching, accompanied by an asymmetric butterfly loop with coercive voltages of -0.31 V and +1.07 V. The films also exhibit a high relative dielectric constant of approximately 1700 with a relatively low dielectric loss tangent of approximately 2.83 at 1 MHz. Moreover, a significant magnetodielectric coefficient of approximately 2.3% is observed under a relatively small magnetic field of only 0.1 kOe, highlighting strong magnetoelectric coupling. These results establish GaFeO3 thin films as a promising platform for room-temperature multifunctional devices, mainly low-field magnetoelectric memory and spintronic architectures.

### Paper [115]
Title: Phonon Fluctuations Enhanced Superconductivity in a Three-Band Cuprate Model
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39710
Authors: Jiarui Liu, Yao Wang
Abstract: The microscopic origin of high-$T_c$ superconductivity in cuprates remains an outstanding puzzle due to the interplay of intertwined orders. Experimental observations and theoretical studies have suggested crucial roles of phonons, with the $B_{1g}$ oxygen buckling mode emerging as a promising candidate. Here, we investigate the microscopic influence of this phonon in a three-band cuprate model. We find that the $B_{1g}$ phonon plays opposite roles in superconductivity depending on whether it lies in the adiabatic or antiadiabatic regime at a given temperature. Thermally populated adiabatic phonons suppress $d$-wave pairing through the loss of quasiparticle coherence, whereas antiadiabatic phonons simultaneously enhance coherence and the attractive $d$-wave pairing vertex, strengthening superconductivity across multiple diagnostics. This enhancement also exhibits pronounced electron-hole asymmetry arising from the different orbital characters of doped carriers. Our results therefore establish a microscopic mechanism through which $B_{1g}$ phonons can cooperate with electronic correlations to substantially enhance $d$-wave superconducting pairing in cuprates.

### Paper [116]
Title: I$_4$ complex acting as near-infrared emitter with a quantum memory in silicon
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39713
Authors: Miaomiao Han, Song Li, Gerg\H{o} Thiering, Thomas Frauenheim, Peter De\'ak, Bing Huang, Adam Gali
Abstract: Silicon color centers emitting in the near infrared offer a route to spin--photon interfaces compatible with mature complementary metal--oxide--semiconductor fabrication. A key unresolved candidate is the X-line at 1039.7 meV in the photoluminescence (PL) spectrum, long attributed to a complex of four self-interstitials (I$_4$), yet whose microscopic structure and spin properties have remained controversial and uncharacterized at a predictive level. Using hybrid-functional (HSE06) density functional theory, we unambiguously establish the bare I$_4$ complex as the origin of the X-line: our calculations quantitatively reproduce the zero-phonon line, the complete phonon sideband structure---identifying the prominent secondary peaks as one- and two-phonon replicas of strongly coupled quasi-local modes---and the EPR hyperfine constants. The charge transition level is found at VBM$+$0.08~eV, conclusively ruling out the previously proposed association with the B3 EPR center and its correlated deep donor level. Most significantly, we identify a metastable spin-1 triplet excited state separated from the singlet by $\sim$1.6~meV, consistent with the NL51 EPR signal. The $D_{2d}$ symmetry of the defect suppresses intersystem crossing and phosphorescence by first-order spin--orbit coupling, implying an exceptionally long spin--lattice relaxation time. Combined with a spectrally resolvable zero-field splitting and near-infrared emission, the triplet state of the I$_4$ complex constitutes a viable spin quantum memory intrinsic to silicon.

### Paper [117]
Title: Influence of hydrophobic polyelectrolyte composition on pH-dependent solubilization of lipid bilayer membranes
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39736
Authors: Martijn Koorengevel, Michelle N. M. de Dood, Wouter T. E. Bosker, James L. Martin Robinson, Cornelis A. van Walree, Bas G. P. van Ravensteijn, Willem K. Kegel
Abstract: Lipid bilayer membranes can be solubilized in the form of disks of approximately 10 nm in diameter by several types of hydrophobic polyelectrolytes (HPE). The disks are stabilized by wrapped HPE along the rims of the disks. We compare the solubilization behavior of anionic HPE with chain lengths in between 20 - 100 monomers, and with varying molecular composition. We find that (1) HPE as short as 20 monomers can readily solubilize lipid bilayer membranes; (2) spatial intermolecular arrangement of ionic and hydrophobic groups in HPE determine if solubilization occurs; (3) anionic HPEs may exhibit two distinct pH-dependent solubilization transitions: the first is encountered in the lower-pH regime upon increasing the pH, whereas the second emerges at higher pH when the pH is decreased; (4) a binomial composition distribution of hydrophobic and ionizable groups over the chains, compared to a fixed composition, does not lead to a measurably wider pH range where solubilization occurs for the transition at low pH. We link the solubilization behavior of one of the HPE's to pH-dependent partitioning between macroscopic oil and water phases. Finally, (5) we find that linear composition superposition of the hydrophobic groups in HPE quantitatively accounts for the composition dependence of the pH's where solubilization occurs. This result holds within a range of compositions and breaks down below a critical fraction of hydrophobic residues in a HPE chain. Throughout, we compare the observations to a recently developed model for ligand-mediated cooperative transitions. Our findings have potential impact on anti-cancer medicine and drug delivery applications.

### Paper [118]
Title: Moir\'e-enabled chiral plasmons in twisted bilayer graphene nanoislands
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39737
Authors: Magnus Husted, Viktor Kanstrup, Eduardo J. C. Dias, Joel D. Cox
Abstract: Twisting two stacked graphene layers superimposes a moir\'e superlattice that reshapes their electronic structure, yet how it shapes the collective optical response of finite, nanoscale bilayers remains largely unexplored. Using an atomistic tight-binding and self-consistent density-matrix approach, we compute the twist-dependent plasmonic response of twisted bilayer graphene nanoislands comprising thousands of atoms. The spectra are strongly tunable and split into two regimes at a critical angle where a single moir\'e wavelength spans the island; beyond it, an ordered ladder of resonances emerges whenever an integer number of moir\'e periods fits within the overlapping bilayer. In doped islands, the interlayer coupling also produces a moir\'e-enabled Born--Kuhn chiral plasmon: opposite transverse dipoles in the two layers form an electric quadrupole that hybridizes with the bright dipole, opening an anticrossing near the critical angle, with a handedness that reverses with the twist direction. Triangular islands confirm that the moir\'e lattice, not the flake boundary, governs both effects, establishing twist as a mechanical handle on chiral graphene plasmons.

### Paper [119]
Title: Direct Observation of Electron Trapping States in SiO$_2$ by Constant-Final-State Photoelectron Yield Spectroscopy: Insights into the Electron-Transfer Model of Contact Electrification
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39748
Authors: Renjiro Shibuya, Manato Tateno, Runa Hoshikawa, Tomohiro Ishibashi, Hirohiko Fukagawa, Hisao Ishii
Abstract: Understanding electrostatic charging of silicon dioxide (SiO$_2$) is important for electrostatic control in a wide range of applications. Contact electrification (CE) is a major source of such charging, and electron transfer is considered one of its leading mechanisms. Several electron-transfer models have been proposed to explain CE; however, the density of states (DOS) involved in electron transfer has not been experimentally characterized. Determining this DOS is therefore essential for understanding the charging mechanism. However, measuring the DOS of insulating materials such as SiO$_2$ remains challenging: photoemission spectroscopy, the standard technique for probing the DOS of materials, is unsuitable for highly insulating materials because sample charging during measurement can cause spectral distortion or even complete signal loss. Here, we directly and quantitatively determined the in-gap DOS of amorphous SiO$_2$ using constant-final-state photoelectron yield spectroscopy (CFS-YS). Weak occupied states were observed within the band gap, and their marked increase after electron injection revealed that electron-trapping states are distributed over a broad energy range. Vacuum annealing markedly altered the in-gap DOS and electron-trapping behavior. The observed changes suggest a role for surface adsorbates, including water, in the charging behavior of SiO$_2$. Charging simulations based on the experimentally measured in-gap DOS approximately reproduced the previously reported charging behavior and showed that the accumulated charge was concentrated near the surface. These findings indicate that in-gap states distributed over a broad energy range mediate electron transfer during CE and highlight the importance of considering experimentally determined DOS when evaluating proposed electron-transfer models.

### Paper [120]
Title: Sign reversal of ST-FMR signals in CoFeB/Pt explained by contributions beyond spin-orbit torque
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39749
Authors: Christian Berg, Ao Du, Durgesh Kumar, Sinan Shu, Sourabh Manna, Sachin Krishnia, Mathias Kl\"aui
Abstract: Spin-orbit torques (SOT) provide an efficient route for electrical control of magnetization and is of central interest for low-power magnetic memory applications. Spin-torque ferromagnetic resonance (ST-FMR) is a widely used technique to quantify SOT efficiencies. However, the rectified voltage measured in ST-FMR can contain contributions that are not directly related to SOTs, which affects the accuracy of the extracted torque efficiencies. This may contribute to the large variation of reported SOT efficiencies in literature. In this study, we investigate the damping-like torque efficiencies in CoFeB/Pt heterostructures as a function of Pt thickness. Conventional ST-FMR analysis yields an apparent sign reversal of the extracted efficiency at a Pt thickness of approximately 10 nm. By combining complementary measurement geometries and analysis approaches, we separate the torque-induced signal from additional voltage contributions that share the same angular dependence. Our results show that non-torque voltage contributions can produce an apparent sign reversal in conventional ST-FMR analysis, highlighting the need to distinguish SOT-induced signals from parasitic contributions and we show how to reliably determine the SOT efficiencies.

### Paper [121]
Title: Growth and morphology of core-shell nanowires incorporating dissimilar hexagonal semiconductors: the case of InAs-ZnTe
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39770
Authors: Danylo Mosiiets (NEEL - NPSC), Susheng Tan (PITT), Sergey Frolov (PITT), Mo\"ira Hocevar (NEEL - NPSC), Joel Cibert (NEEL - NPSC), Edith Bellet-Amalric (PHELIQS)
Abstract: Core-shell nanowires incorporating semiconductors from two groups, III-V for the core and II-VI for the shell, have been grown by molecular beam epitaxy in two dedicated chambers connected under ultra-high vacuum, and studied by electron microscopy. During the growth of the shell, the wurtzite structure of the core is replicated in the shell, without any creation of defects. This is true for a wide range of shell thickness, from about one monolayer, up to 25 nm thanks to the good lattice match between the InAs material of the core and the ZnTe material of the shell. While (1 100) m-facets and (11 20) a-facets coexist on the sidewalls of the core, during the growth of the ZnTe shell the size of the m-facets progressively increases at the expense of the a-facets which eventually disappear. A fast growth of the ZnTe shell is observed for the first few monolayers, with a high sticking; this is followed by a slower growth, of the same order of magnitude as observed on a ZnTe core, with a dependence on the nanowire radius which points to the occurrence of mononuclear nucleation

### Paper [122]
Title: Two-dimensional binary Lennard-Jones mixtures with ECMC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39793
Authors: Kush Patel, Gabriele Tartero, Werner Krauth
Abstract: We discuss reversible and non-reversible local Monte Carlo algorithms for long-range two-dimensional binary Lennard-Jones mixtures. We illustrate how the factorized Metropolis filter efficiently handles long-range interactions even for binary mixtures, using an adapted cell-veto strategy. We then benchmark the non-reversible event-chain Monte Carlo (ECMC) algorithm against the factorized Metropolis algorithm. For simple test cases, we demonstrate speedups of several orders of magnitude in the equilibrium and the out-of-equilibrium dynamics. Using the ECMC algorithm, we provide an overview of the physics of binary Lennard-Jones mixtures, from the case without depletion (no interaction between solute and solvent) all the way to the depletion limit (with a diverging number of solvent particles). We analyze the algorithms in different thermodynamic phases and in the corresponding limits.

### Paper [123]
Title: High-temperature expansion of the inverse-square Ising chain in a magnetic field: exact coefficients and finite-size reconstruction
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39815
Authors: M. A. Rajabpour
Abstract: The one-dimensional Ising chain with inverse-square interactions is a paradigmatic long-range system: it exhibits the unusual Thouless transition, with a discontinuity in the spontaneous magnetization, yet its full field-dependent thermodynamics remains unknown in closed form. We develop the high-temperature expansion of its free energy while retaining the dependence on a uniform magnetic field at every order. We prove that, after a natural rescaling of the coupling, each coefficient is a rational polynomial in the squared hyperbolic tangent of the reduced field. Remarkably, at each order $n$ the field-dependent free-energy coefficient of a finite, periodically repeated ring is an exact polynomial of degree at most $n$ in $L^{-2}$. The infinite-chain coefficient is its constant term and can therefore be reconstructed from $n+1$ finite rings without numerical extrapolation. Using this reconstruction, we determine the full field-dependent expansion through order 26: 26 polynomials containing 377 rational coefficient entries. We further derive exact formulas, valid at every order, for the coefficients of the two highest powers of the field variable using matching determinants and a cycle-Laplacian reduction. These results reveal an unexpected algebraic structure in the microscopic thermodynamics of the inverse-square chain and provide high-order information about its magnetic response.

### Paper [124]
Title: Steering interfacial molecular structures by substrate-molecule charge transfer
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39822
Authors: Benjamin Achatz, Matthias Zeilerbauer, Marco Thaler, Milan On\v{c}\'ak, Shadi Fatayer, Laerte L. Patera
Abstract: Intermixed self-assembled monolayers (SAMs) provide a powerful approach for tuning inorganic-organic interfaces, but their local structure and energetics often remain elusive. Here, an intermixed stripe phase of fullerene $C_{60}$ and Zn(II)-5,10,15,20-tetrakis(4-aminophenyl)porphyrin (Zn-TAPP) on a Ag(111) surface is identified, establishing a direct link between molecular assembly and charge transfer. Scanning tunnelling spectroscopy (STS) and Kelvin probe force spectroscopy (KPFS) have been used to map electronic states and local contact potential differences (LCPD). Compared to close-packed $C_{60}$ islands, the stripe phase exhibits a larger interfacial dipole and increased occupation of previously unoccupied $C_{60}$ states, consistent with enhanced charge transfer from Ag(111) to $C_{60}$. Analysis indicates that larger $C_{60}$ spacing in the stripe geometry reduces Coulomb repulsion, stabilizing the intermixed phase and enhancing charge transfer with respect to close-packed domains. These results provide direct evidence of charged $C_{60}$ molecules in stripe structures and guidelines for engineering intermixed SAMs with tailored interfacial electronic properties.

### Paper [125]
Title: Epitaxial Magnon Junctions with Y3Fe5O12/Gd3Ga5O12/Pt-nanoparticles/Y3Fe5O12
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39841
Authors: Jianing Lin, Haoyu Zhuang, Tianyi Zhang, Jiyang Ou, Jihao Xia, Hongyu Wang, Jiang Niu, Qinglong Yu, Caihua Wan, Guoqiang Yu, Richeng Yu, Xiufeng Han
Abstract: The excitation, propagation, manipulation, and detection of magnons lie at the core of magnonics. Among various device architectures, high-quality magnon junctions with a ferromagnetic insulator (FI)/antiferromagnetic insulator (AFI)/ferromagnetic insulator (FI) configuration are predicted to exhibit a near-100% magnon junction ratio (MJR), making them promising candidates for high-performance non-volatile memory applications. Yttrium iron garnet (Y3Fe5O12, YIG) is widely employed as the FI material in such structures for its exceptionally low magnetic damping. However, in previously reported magnon junctions, the epitaxial relationship between the two YIG layers is often disrupted by lattice-mismatched AFI spacers. Although this leads to distinct coercivities, it simultaneously degrades the crystalline quality of the top YIG layer, thereby limiting the magnontransmission efficiency. Here, an epitaxial magnon junction is demonstrated incorporating a hybrid Gd3Ga5O12 (GGG)/Pt-nanoparticles spacer that maintains coherent epitaxy while enabling an antiparallel magnetic configuration. Compared with nickel oxide-based magnon junctions, the coercivity-tuned top YIG layer in our structure exhibits superior crystalline quality and much lower magnetic damping. A clear magnon junction effect is observed with a 43% MJR achieved. The interlayer coupling between the two ferrimagnetic insulators is found to enhance the MJR. These results offer valuable insights for the development of advanced magnon-based spintronic devices.

### Paper [126]
Title: Quantum Hamiltonian time crystals from covariant quantum Landau--Lifshitz--Gilbert relaxation
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39886
Authors: Antti J. Niemi
Abstract: Classical Hamiltonian time crystals are constrained energy minima that move along symmetry orbits at frequencies set by Lagrange multipliers. We construct quantum counterparts by minimizing the energy over density matrices with fixed spectrum and charge expectations. A symmetry-covariant quantum Landau--Lifshitz--Gilbert equation selects them as periodic attractors, preserving the spectrum and fixing the multipliers dynamically. A chiral spin trimer motivated by Cu$_3$ molecular magnets realizes this motion at a prepared magnetization, without a Zeeman term.

### Paper [127]
Title: Ammonium thiocyanate enables in-situ structural templating of MAPbI3 via quasi-2D perovskite scaffold
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39887
Authors: Sophie Tucker, Isaac Metcalf, Rachel C. Kilbride, Elena J. Cassella, Jessica H. Persaud, Faiz Mandani, Niranjana Raj, Max Allen, Charlie H. G. Nicholls, David G. Lidzey, Alice L. B. Pyne, Robert D. J. Oliver, Silvia Motti, Jacky Even, Aditya D. Mohite, Alexandra J. Ramadan
Abstract: Ammonium thiocyanate (NH4SCN) has been used extensively to improve the current extraction and device performance of quasi-two-dimensional perovskite solar cells. Whilst this additive has been shown to influence quasi-2D perovskite crystallisation, a detailed understanding of its mechanism has not yet been established. Through spectroscopic and structural studies, we demonstrate that NH4SCN enables quasi-2D perovskite phases to structurally template the dominant 3D phase. Contrary to previous reports, we suggest that the benefit of this additive comes from an improvement to the 3D perovskite structure, not from charges transferring through vertically oriented 2D perovskite layers. We show that this additive provides kinetic control over the quasi-2D phase formation, suppressing the unfavourable n = 1 perovskite phase and promoting the growth of high-n 2D scaffold phases by extending the nucleation window. This promotes the in-situ templated growth of MAPbI3. We provide robust structural analysis demonstrating highly (110)-ordered 3D perovskite, with the mechanism confirmed by close lattice matching between this phase and quasi-2D phases. Furthermore, we show that this structural control is cation dependent, requiring both close lattice matching between quasi-2D and 3D perovskite phases, and successful kinetic control of the relevant quasi-2D phases.

### Paper [128]
Title: Excitons in solid molecular hydrogen from quantum Monte Carlo
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39894
Authors: Edgar Josu\'e Landinez Borda, Ravindra Shinde, Claudia Filippi, Markus Holzmann, Saverio Moroni
Abstract: We use quantum Monte Carlo (QMC) methods to investigate the lowest neutral excitation of solid molecular hydrogen in phase-I under increasing compression, with emphasis on structural and response properties. We employ QMC wave functions for the excited state that feature only a single orbital substitution, or a multideterminant expansion including every single particle-hole transition, and compare the results with the Bethe-Salpeter equation (BSE) and time-dependent density-functional theory (TDDFT). We examine a number of nuclear configurations representing thermal and quantum fluctuations. The effect of the multideterminant expansion is marginal on the QMC excitation energies but crucial on the exciton localization and related quantities, irrespective of full optimization and imaginary time projection. In the strong insulating regime at low density, multideterminant QMC results match and in some cases surpass the degree of localization afforded by BSE. A similar trend is observed for transition dipole moments and excitation-induced forces, with generally good agreement between QMC and BSE but marked discrepancies for nuclear configurations where localization differs significantly. TDDFT generally gives a more extended exciton with correspondingly weaker transition dipole moments. These differences decrease upon compression, as the system approaches a semiconducting regime where multiple particle-hole transitions are less important.

### Paper [129]
Title: Kelvin-Wave Multiplication by Sound Emission
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39909
Authors: Weican Yang
Abstract: A subsonic Kelvin quantum can emit a phonon and split into two softer quanta, increasing Kelvin population while losing energy. In the soft-phonon limit, rotational invariance relates the leading acoustic shear amplitude, odd under reversal of phonon axial momentum, to the energy released. Microscopic calculations support this dispersion dependence. In a reflecting cavity with coherent sound and Kelvin seeds, Gross--Pitaevskii evolution shows Kelvin gain, larger mean-square displacement and smaller mean-square slope; a $\pi$ shift of the initial sound phase reverses these changes.

### Paper [130]
Title: Ensemble-dependent effect of disorder on multicritical points in a system with long-range interactions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39929
Authors: Nir Schreiber, Reuven Cohen
Abstract: We introduce two variants of the Blume--Emery--Griffiths model with long-range, mean-field-like interactions and quenched disorder. Although the two variants are described by different Hamiltonians, they yield the same free energy. Moreover, both Hamiltonians lead to the same average energy and hence to the same microcanonical entropy. The canonical and microcanonical phase boundaries differ markedly, and so does the multicritical behaviour of the two ensembles. In the microcanonical ensemble, tricritical points appear for any fraction of active spins, whereas in the canonical ensemble they emerge only when this fraction is sufficiently large. At lower fractions, canonical critical endpoints arise instead. We also report the formation of gaps in the entropy as a function of magnetization, another manifestation of ensemble inequivalence.

### Paper [131]
Title: Majorana Zero Modes in Fractional Vortices
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39942
Authors: Lecheng Fang, Haotian Li, Haijiao Ji, Quanxin Hu, Hong Ding, Noah F. Q. Yuan
Abstract: Majorana zero modes (MZMs) are promising for topological quantum computation, with vortices in topological superconductors serving as a leading platform. Recent experiments show that integer Abrikosov vortices in iron-based superconductors can split into fractional vortices (FVs) carrying fractional flux quanta. We show that due to topological protection, MZMs hosted by FVs are spatially unfolded over the FV cores, while fermionic bound states remain localized at individual FV cores. This yields zero-bias conductance peaks in both local tunneling and nonlocal crossed Andreev reflection, offering an alternative MZM signature.

### Paper [132]
Title: Monte Carlo simulation of mixed spin-orbit ordering
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39943
Authors: Yu. D. Panov, V. A. Ulitko
Abstract: The effect of a magnetic field on the thermodynamic properties of the Kugel-Khomskii model with maximal entanglement using the quasi-classical Monte Carlo method with the heat bath algorithm. The temperature dependencies of single-site correlators show that the application of a magnetic field results in non-zero magnetization and the simultaneous appearance of an orbital ordering at the same point in the Brillouin zone where a non-zero spin-orbit correlator exists.

### Paper [133]
Title: Microscopic Mechanism and Control of Reentrant Localization
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39961
Authors: Pei-Jie Chang, Quanfeng Lu, Wentao Yang, Dong Ruan, Gui-Lu Long
Abstract: Increasing quasiperiodic modulation can restore extended states after localization, raising the question of how the same modulation drives both localization and recovery. We identify a microscopic mechanism in quasiperiodic dimer chains by resolving scattering between Fourier channels. Specifically, scattering into neighboring channels and back shifts the spectral edge relative to a localization boundary determined by a neutral auxiliary resonance. Their successive crossings drive the same spectral packet with fixed integrated-density-of-states (IDS) labels into localization and back into an extended regime. This recovery persists across finite parameter ranges in both gap-opening families, including relative-phase detuning. Furthermore, an exact sublattice mapping enables compensated control of the recovery window and predicts recovery thresholds for individual IDS labels.

### Paper [134]
Title: Selective effects of selenium substitution on superconductivity and magnetic memory in 4Hb-TaS2
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39969
Authors: Shalev Gur, Sajilesh K. P., Shubam Purwar, Irena Feldman, Ilay Mangel, Nitzan Ragoler, Amit Kanigel, Beena Kalisky
Abstract: Disorder plays a central role in correlated quantum materials, but its effects can be highly selective when several electronic orders coexist. The van der Waals heterostructure 4Hb-TaS2 provides such a case, combining superconductivity, charge-order tendencies, strong scattering, and magnetic memory manifested through spontaneous vortices. Here we use Se substitution as a controlled local perturbation of the disorder landscape in 4Hb-TaS2. We find that superconductivity remains remarkably robust while the magnetic memory is strongly suppressed at higher Se concentrations. These results show that Se substitution does not create the hidden magnetic memory, but instead selectively weakens its macroscopic manifestation. More broadly, they reveal a sharp separation between robust superconducting and hidden-memory phases, and a highly disorder-sensitive spontaneous-vortex response in 4Hb-TaS2.

### Paper [135]
Title: Nonequilibrium statistical thermodynamics of a small quantum system coupled to quantum reservoirs
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39973
Authors: Jeremy Schofield, Raymond Kapral
Abstract: A statistical mechanical formulation where nonequilibrium averages of selected quantum observables are given exactly as averages over local density operators is used to describe the dynamics and thermodynamics of a small quantum system coupled to reservoirs. The local density operators are functionals of constraint variables that play the role of thermodynamic forces that satisfy a set of equations of motion. A focus of the work is on the evolution of the average internal energy of the quantum subsystem that may be subject to external autonomous driving that does work on the system, and on entropy production in the system. The nonequilibrium statistical thermodynamics the quantum subsystem coupled to reservoirs, as well as that for the isolated subsystem and reservoirs, are discussed. When the subsystem is coupled to large quantum reservoirs, the effects of the quantum subsystem on the reservoirs can be captured through boundary conditions on the reservoir equations, and these boundary conditions are derived. The formulation is illustrated by considering the heat transfer through a system comprising a quantum system coupled to two heat reservoirs with different temperatures.

### Paper [136]
Title: Confinement Controlled Phase-Pure 2D Layered Perovskites for Band-Edge Engineering
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39974
Authors: Danila A. Tatarinov, Alexander Schleusener, Roman Krahne
Abstract: Controlling the number n of connected octahedra layers in two-dimensional metal halide perovskites enables fine control of the semiconductor band gap that is highly advantageous for optoelectronic and light emission applications. Phase purity in terms of n is crucial towards narrow emission bands and the design of band structure alignment for charge carrier funneling. We present a simple and scalable synthesis method using cesium and butylammonium as A-cations to fabricate microcrystal powders of 2D lead bromide perovskites that results in samples with very high n-phase purity. The obtained microcrystals show a sharp onset in absorbance and single narrow emission peak in UV-blue spectral range, and we confirm their structure and composition with X-ray diffraction and energy dispersive spectroscopy. We show that we can tailor the spectral absorption of microcrystals samples by controlled mixing of the n-phase pure powders, and we demonstrate this capability with spectral photocurrent measurements using pellets pressed from pure and mixed powders. Both the spectral and dynamic response of the photocurrent of the pellets depend strongly on the composition of the powders, highlighting the design potential of our approach towards band-edge engineering.

### Paper [137]
Title: Electronic liquid crystals and superconductivity from doping a moir\'e Mott insulator
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39978
Authors: Yichi Zhang, Yiyu Xia, Zhongdong Han, Jekwan Lee, Brandon Bauer, Kenji Watanabe, Takashi Taniguchi, Wenjin Zhao, Kin Fai Mak, Jie Shan
Abstract: Electronic liquid crystals, such as stripe phases and nematics, are self-organized states of matter with spontaneously broken rotational symmetry. They are expected to emerge in a doped Mott insulator as a compromise between short- and long-range Coulomb interactions, and are believed to be intimately connected to the emergence of high temperature superconductivity either as a competing order or an intertwined order. Moir\'e semiconductors provide a controllable platform to explore the relationship between stripe phases and superconductivity near a Mott insulator. Here we report the generic appearance of stripe phases in a doped moir\'e Mott insulator and the observation of a stripe to-superconductor quantum phase transition in twisted bilayer WSe2. The stripe phases were revealed by electrical transport studies as a thermodynamic phase transition from a high temperature isotropic phase to a low-temperature anisotropic phase accompanied by a Fermi surface reconstruction. Near the stripe-to-superconductor transition, we also observed an additional thermodynamic phase transition from metallic stripes to pinned insulating stripes. The stripe-to-superconductor transition further exhibits an intriguing self-similarity that highlights the intimate relationship between the two orders. Our results pave the path for studying intertwined orders in moir\'e materials.

### Paper [138]
Title: Evidence for an effective gauge field in flakes of superconducting 4Hb-TaS2
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39979
Authors: Shalev Gur, T. R. Devidas, Eylon Persky, Roni Anna Gofman, Irena Feldman, Avior Almoalem, Sudip Kumar Saha, Mark H. Fischer, Avraham Klein, Jonathan Ruhman, Amit Kanigel, Beena Kalisky
Abstract: Understanding the normal state of unconventional superconductors is central to uncovering the origin of superconductivity itself. Unconventional superconductivity often emerges from correlated electronic states characterized by intertwined symmetry-breaking orders. A striking example is 4Hb-TaS2, whose superconducting state exhibits a remarkable range of phenomena, including an unusual magnetic memory effect. Previous experiments showed that magnetic memory can be encoded above Tc and later revealed through spontaneous vortices below Tc, suggesting time-reversal symmetry breaking. However, since memory and vorticity are intertwined, this prevents establishing whether the effect results from trapped flux, weak conventional magnetism, or a true hidden electronic order. Here, we show that magnetic memory persists in the vortex-free Meissner regime of exfoliated 4Hb-TaS2 flakes, where it appears as a stable remanent modification of the superconducting screening response. Our results rule out explanations based on trapped vortices. Furthermore, the spatial profile of the flux generated by trained samples at zero field is inconsistent with conventional weak magnetism, suggesting a hidden electronic phase that imprints an effective flux on the superconducting condensate.

### Paper [139]
Title: Thermodynamic Crossovers in Supercritical Matter: One, No One, and One Hundred Thousand
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39986
Authors: Yang Xu, Matteo Baggioli
Abstract: Beyond a critical point, two thermodynamic phases become continuously connected, without being separated by any thermodynamic singularity. The paradigmatic example is the liquid-gas transition, where several criteria have been proposed to distinguish liquid-like and gas-like regimes within the supercritical region. Recently, a pair of thermodynamic crossover lines, $L^\pm$, was introduced to address a proposed incompatibility of previous definitions with the universal critical behavior of the Ising model. These lines divide the supercritical region into three regimes, meet only at the critical point, and obey the expected critical scaling laws. Here, we demonstrate that these requirements do not uniquely determine the crossover boundaries. Instead, infinitely many pairs of lines satisfy the same conditions, with different choices of thermodynamic paths selecting different crossover boundaries. We further show that the Lee-Yang formalism, despite providing an intrinsic characterization of critical singularities, does not resolve this ambiguity in defining real-axis crossover boundaries. Restricting to regular probing paths that respect the $Z_2$ symmetry of the critical point recovers the Widom and $L^\pm$ prescriptions asymptotically. This restriction, however, is a choice of protocol rather than a property of the system, and even then two inequivalent prescriptions survive. Our results demonstrate that universal critical behavior alone does not select privileged supercritical thermodynamic crossover boundaries. While such lines may identify physically meaningful changes in thermodynamic, structural, or transport properties, their precise locations and the widths of the regions they delimit remain dependent on the operational criteria used to define them.

### Paper [140]
Title: Non-Bloch Dynamical Quantum Phase Transitions with Memory
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39994
Authors: Zhesen Yang, Wei-Wei Yang, Henning Schomerus, Jiangping Hu
Abstract: Dynamical quantum phase transitions (DQPTs) of periodic chains are determined by real Bloch momenta. We identify non-Bloch DQPTs, which occur only in open chains and are determined by complex momenta instead. They arise in a Hermitian chain coupled to a simple substrate, whose memory is treated exactly, and originate from the point-gap winding number $W$ of the projected propagator: for $W\neq0$, open boundaries change both the dynamical free energy and the critical times, even in the thermodynamic limit. We find that both follow from an analytic continuation to complex momenta, and show that $W$ can be measured by particle counting. This reveals a dynamical bulk-boundary correspondence, in which non-Hermitian topology emerges from Hermitian dynamics with memory.

### Paper [141]
Title: Influence of pairing and correlations on transport properties in three-terminal superconducting nanostructure
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40059
Authors: G. G\'orski, V. Pokorn\'y, T. Doma\'nski
Abstract: We study the local and nonlocal charge transport properties of three-terminal nanostructure, consisting of the correlated quantum dot embedded between two metallic electrodes and superconductor. Using the linear response theory, we analyze interplay of the induced on-dot pairing with the correlation effects, inspecting their influence on the conductance and thermopower. Our calculations predict negative values of the nonlocal conductance and reversal of the Seebeck coefficient when the quantum dot ground state is represented by the BCS-type singlet configuration. These phenomena are realized when the subgap charge transport is predominantly contributed by the crossed Andreev scattering. In contrast, on the doublet side, we obtain signatures for the Kondo effect manifested by the low-temperature enhancement of the charge conductance and clearly evident in the thermopower. Such temperature-dependent local and nonlocal transport properties can unambiguously identify the specific configuration of the correlated quantum dot and reveal the rich physics emerging in the doublet-to-singlet crossover region.

### Paper [142]
Title: Dynamical mean-field theory for associative memory with block-correlated patterns
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40060
Authors: Andrea Callegaris, Yoshiyuki Kabashima
Abstract: In associative memories storing correlated patterns, retrieval of individual memories can compete with symmetric mixed states. A dynamical description is needed to determine how the initial state selects between these outcomes. We extend the generating-functional dynamical mean-field theory (DMFT) for independent patterns to independent blocks of correlated binary patterns of fixed finite size. The resulting effective single-neuron process has a self-consistent cross-talk noise covariance and self-feedback coefficients that depend on the block-covariance eigenvalues. For hierarchically correlated patterns with nonmonotonic transfer, increasing the loading can permit retrieval from an imperfect initial state before further loading prevents it. DMFT reproduces the retrieval trajectories and basin geometry found in direct simulations. Near the lower boundary of this re-entrant region, direct networks reveal stationary saddles separating coexisting mixed and target-retrieval states. Parameter sensitivities of DMFT trajectories predict nearby shifts of this boundary. At the tested upper boundary, retrieval remains accessible from initial states with larger target overlap, indicating a change in basin access. Under fixed-point convergence assumptions, the theory recovers the equations of generalized self-consistent signal-to-noise analysis.

### Paper [143]
Title: Quantum Metric Scaling and Its Exceptions in Classical Flat Bands
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40066
Authors: Kin Hung Fung, K. T. Law, C. T. Chan
Abstract: The quantum metric length has been proposed as the localization length scale in one-dimensional flat bands. Here, we study this quantum metric scaling and the exceptions to it in a classical flat band formed by a locally resonant mass--spring system. We find that the localization length follows the quantum metric length near the flat band but departs from this proportionality at the exact flat-band frequency. Selective mass disorder also produces divergent localization lengths or arbitrarily short tails near the flat-band frequency without changing the clean metric. These exceptions show that disorder can select localization scales beyond those predicted by the quantum metric alone.

### Paper [144]
Title: Magnetotransport experiments on side-contacted topological insulator three-terminal Josephson junctions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40083
Authors: Christoph Wassmer, Ella Nikodem, Oliver Breunig, Yongjian Wang, Yoichi Ando, Jakob Schluck
Abstract: Multiterminal Josephson junctions have recently attracted substantial attention. The ability to study Andreev bound states in a multi-phase parameter space opens up exciting avenues for generating topologically non-trivial phases in superconducting devices. A particularly interesting case is the trijunction based on a topological insulator (TI), which is predicted to host Majorana zero modes for suitable phase configurations (Fu and Kane, PRL 2008). Here we turn to the platform of the side-contacted TI trijunction and present experimental evidence for a multiterminal Josephson effect. We study the transport properties and find typical features of a two-dimensional critical current contour (CCC) as well as bias regimes in which only a single junction remains dissipationless. We pay particular attention to these features and study the effects of magnetic fields on single junctions within the trijunction, where, depending on the field direction, the supercurrents show Fraunhofer patterns, SQUID oscillations, or a monotonic decay. We find good agreement with semiclassical simulations for a single TI junction that take into account the top and bottom surface states. We observe pronounced non-convex shape deformations of the CCC under perpendicular magnetic fields that reflect the flux buildup in the loop formed by our trijunctions and provide a simple model sufficient to explain the observed effect. Our results provide the foundations for future studies of Majorana zero modes on this platform.

### Paper [145]
Title: Static Response of the Strongly Coupled Electron Gas from Diffusion Monte Carlo
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40093
Authors: Muhammed H. G\"une\c{s}, Yubo Yang, Vitaly Gorelov, Miguel A. Morales, Matteo Gatti, Lucia Reining, Shiwei Zhang
Abstract: The local field factor $G(q)$ of the HEG describes how exchange and correlation modify the response to a static perturbation, and enters dielectric screening, effective electron--electron interactions, and the xc kernel of TDDFT. Accurate zero-temperature data for $G(q)$, however, remain limited to metallic densities, $r_s\leq10$. In this work, we present diffusion Monte Carlo calculations of the static density response function $\chi(q)$ and of $G(q)$ for $2\leq r_s\leq100$, and describe and discuss the methodological steps that enabled these calculations. We show how optimization of the perturbing potential in the trial wave function has an important effect, as a poor choice can reverse the sign of the response. A closed-form expression is provided for its optimal value, together with the density dependence of the linear-response window. We further show that backflow correlations leave $\chi(q)$ unchanged and that $G(q)$ depends only weakly on system size even at very low density, so that Slater--Jastrow wave functions and modest system sizes suffice. Together, these results provide a practical guide for future QMC calculations of response properties of the electron gas and real materials. Our results agree with previous DMC data at metallic densities for $q>k_F$, and extend them below $k_F$. They also agree with diagrammatic QMC at $r_s=2$. At lower densities, our results show that $G(q)$ develops a nonmonotonic structure at intermediate-$q$, where the response approaches that of the noninteracting gas. Quantitative agreement is seen with finite-temperature path integral Monte Carlo, suggesting that the previously reported finite-temperature structure is inherited from the ground state. Using a parametrization of $G(q)$ that reproduces our data throughout the liquid phase, we relate this structure to short-range order in the pair correlation function and the static structure factor.

### Paper [146]
Title: Single-atom billiards in an ultracold bath with energy-tunable collisions and threshold-sensitive detection
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40098
Authors: Silvia Hiebel, Sabrina Burgardt, Julian Fe{\ss}, Artur Widera
Abstract: Transport processes at the microscopic scale can differ strongly from their macroscopic counterparts, particularly when the environment is not a well-defined surface but an unordered thermal medium. Ultracold quantum gases offer a high degree of controllability and directly observable dynamics, thereby providing a versatile platform to study such transport processes under precisely controllable conditions. Here, we realize an energy-tunable single-atom collider by transporting individual cesium atoms at controlled velocity through an ultracold rubidium bath. The constant lattice velocity sets the directed contribution to the collision-energy distribution, while the lattice depth provides a tunable energy threshold for collision-induced transport disruption. By independently varying these parameters together with bath density and temperature, we separate the number of collision events from their probability to disrupt transport. Across a regime with only a few expected collisions per passage, the inferred single-collision ejection probability rises from small values to near unity as the collision energy exceeds the lattice confinement scale. The experiment thus converts microscopic collisional energy transfer into a binary single-atom observable and provides a controlled link between individual scattering events and emergent dissipative transport.

### Paper [147]
Title: Continuous tuning of quantum critical behavior in hybrid Josephson junction arrays
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40104
Authors: E. Fugl, L. L. Olsen, S. Sasmal, M. Efthymiou-Tsironi, G. Nagda, F. Krizek, L. Casparis, S. Vaitiek\.enas
Abstract: We report finite-temperature scaling of voltage-driven quantum phase transitions in hybrid Josephson junction arrays. A dual-gate geometry separately tunes interisland coupling and proximity-induced superconductivity, providing access to superconducting, insulating, and metallic transport regimes. The critical resistivity and scaling exponent evolve continuously with electrostatic tuning. Where the three regimes converge, the critical resistivity approaches $h/4e^2$, consistent with the self-dual limit, while the scaling exponent approaches the quantum-percolation estimate. Tuning toward stronger insulating behavior raises the critical resistivity and shifts the scaling exponent toward the classical-percolation value, while both parameters decrease toward the metallic regime. These results connect scaling regimes previously reported in different two-dimensional superconducting systems through continuous electrostatic control.

### Paper [148]
Title: Non-Gaussian transport in tempered fractional Laplace motion
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40113
Authors: Yiming Zhao, Wei Wang, Ralf Metzler
Abstract: We study a minimal stochastic model for active transport featuring a non-Gaussian displacement probability density function and transient superdiffusion. Our approach is based on a combination of an exponential tempering in the parental fractional Brownian noise correlations, augmented by a gamma-distribution of the diffusivity across the ensemble. For the resulting tempered fractional Laplace motion we derive analytical expressions for the mean-squared displacement, the kurtosis, the non-Gaussianity parameter and the displacement probability density function. We also show via stochastic simulations that the model quantitatively reproduces the main experimental observations in a recent study of active cargo particle transport by a carpet of amoeba cells.

### Paper [149]
Title: Adiabatic Variational Principle: Born-Oppenheimer Theory for Emergent Electronic-Order Dynamics
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40117
Authors: Yang Yang, Ali Rayat, Ho Jang, Yunhao Fan, Sankha Subhra Bakshi, Gia-Wei Chern
Abstract: Recent advances in microscopic many-body methods have enabled increasingly accurate descriptions of equilibrium quantum states, yet large-scale simulations of emergent-order dynamics typically rely on phenomenological equations with prescribed free-energy functionals. We introduce the Adiabatic Variational Principle (AVP), a framework for deriving order-parameter dynamics directly from an interacting many-body Hamiltonian. When microscopic relaxation is fast compared with order-parameter evolution, the many-body state follows the collective configuration adiabatically, in analogy with the Born--Oppenheimer separation of electronic and nuclear motion. This assumption makes explicit the adiabatic closure implicit in conventional phase-field descriptions. Each instantaneous order-parameter configuration then defines a constrained quasi-stationary many-body state, and the Lagrange multipliers enforcing these constraints directly provide the thermodynamic forces driving the collective evolution. Nonlinear Mori--Zwanzig projection theory further connects the reversible dynamics to the microscopic algebra of the collective variables and relates dissipation and fluctuations to the eliminated degrees of freedom. Applications to charge- and spin-density-wave dynamics recover Allen--Cahn domain growth, resolve evolving domain networks together with their microscopic electronic structure, and yield generalized Landau--Lifshitz dynamics for itinerant magnetism. The AVP thus provides a solver-independent microscopic foundation for phase-field-like simulations of the nonequilibrium dynamics of emergent electronic order.

### Paper [150]
Title: Investigation of perovskite related oxides: structural and magnetic properties
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40139
Authors: Antara Sarkar
Abstract: Perovskites exist in varied structures and show structure dependent magnetic properties which is of great interest in the field of multiferroics, magnetocaloric and spintronic devices. Here we intend to study the various rare-earth(R) and transition metal (TM) ion interactions depending on their structures. Different Curie temperature, spinglass transition and spin reorientation transitions were observed depending on the degree of interactions and structural properties. It is interesting to study the role of orbital interactions, disorder in magnetic transitions leading to change in superexchange and double exchange interaction. On substitution at A-site or B-site results in change in magnetic interactions, as well as creates structural distortions. We also obtained results where the spin interaction of the transition metal and rare-earth interaction, magnetic anisotropy, orientations of orbitals, disorder induced interactions were observed.

### Paper [151]
Title: Spin-Orbit Coupling Response Theory within the Oriented Spin Space Group Framework
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40149
Authors: Yuntian Liu, Xiaobing Chen, Yutong Yu, Jes\'us Etxebarria, J. Manuel Perez-Mato, Qihang Liu
Abstract: Spin-orbit coupling (SOC) plays a pivotal role in a broad range of phenomena in condensed matter physics. The recently developed spin space group theory offers a new perspective for characterizing the fundamental symmetry-breaking features of SOC-related phenomena, yet its full potential remains to be explored. Here, we provide a general framework that relates the SOC-induced symmetry breaking to modifications of physical properties and emergence of new physical effects. This approach incorporates the recently developed oriented spin space group (OSSG) formalism, which can explicitly describe the symmetry reduction induced by SOC. By parameterizing the SOC Hamiltonian using a SOC tensor that connects real and spin spaces, an expansion in terms of the SOC components can be constructed for the response tensor describing any physical quantity. This framework determines not only whether a SOC-induced response is symmetry allowed, but also the lowest order in SOC at which it emerges and its dependence on magnetic anisotropy. As representative applications, we analyze the magnetoelectric coupling in Type-II multiferroics and show that magnetically induced electric polarization in different systems can be the result of SOC effects of different orders, and can exhibit varying characteristics of magnetic anisotropy. We further apply this theory to the anomalous Hall effect and spin magnetization in antiferromagnets, demonstrating that their distinct transformation properties under the OSSG allow a large anomalous Hall response to coexist with weak spin magnetization. A high-throughput analysis of materials in the MAGNDATA database identifies several symmetry-based material classes favorable for this coexistence. Overall, the proposed framework provides a unified symmetry description of SOC-induced phenomena and offers a systematic way for the identification of SOC-driven functional materials.

### Paper [152]
Title: Cubic-Zn(1-x)Mn(x)Te optical modes across the phonon and phonon-polariton regimes
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40157
Authors: M. Mina, A. Elmahjoubi, T. Ngulezhu, S. Thiam, A. V. Postnikov, T. Tite, P. Bourges, F. Bourdarot, N. Biniskos, A. Polian, Y. Le Godec, N. Guignot, J. -P. Itie, S. Diliberto, S. Michel, A. Maillard, A. En Naciri, L. Broch, P. Franchetti, K. Strzalkowski, O. Pages
Abstract: Alloying offers a convenient route to tune the physical properties of semiconductors, at the price, however, of local lattice/bond distortions needed to accommodate the lattice mismatch. Being nearly lattice-matched and moreover highly resistant to lattice and bond distortions thanks to a largely covalent bonding, cubic Zn(1-x)Mn(x)Te sets a benchmark to test the sensitivity of lattice vibrations to minimum lattice and bond distortions induced by alloying. We explore experimentally the two transverse optical regimes of cubic Zn(1-x)Mn(x)Te (x<0.7), with ab initio calculations in support: (1) the phonon-polariton regime at quasi-infinite (photon-like) wavelengths averaging over the alloy disorder, probed by forward-Raman scattering, and (2) the purely-mechanical phonon regime down to minimum wavelengths nearly matching the lattice constant zooming into the alloy disorder, achieved by inelastic neutron scattering. The short Zn-Te bond is found to exhibit a compact optical duo distinguishing homo from hetero local bond environments (1 bond: 2 mode), as explained by the percolation model. This deviates from the historical Zn(1-x)Mn(x)Te MREI (modified-random-element-isodisplacement) scheme inspired from classical Raman and infrared studies, in which the optical modes are blind to the bond environment (1-bond: 1-mode). So, the test is positive, reinforcing the status of the percolation scheme as a robust decryptor of (even small) lattice distortions induced by alloying through the lattice dynamics.

### Paper [153]
Title: Coexistence of Superconductivity and Chern insulators with |C| $\geq 1$ in twisted rhombohedral bilayer-trilayer graphene
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40170
Authors: Qiu Yang, Zihao Huo, Le Liu, Tianqi Song, Fankun Wang, Jingwei Dong, Kenji Watanabe, Takashi Taniguchi, Weibo Gao, Xiaobo Lu, Guangyu Zhang, Wei Yang
Abstract: Rhombohedral multilayer graphene (RMG) has been demonstrated as a promising platform for investigating strongly correlated phases of matter. Here, we observe a coexistence of superconductivity and Chern insulators in a twisted rhombohedral bilayer-trilayer graphene moir\'e superlattice. Superconductivity, evidenced by a sharp transition with a critical temperature of ~180 mK, nonlinear I-V with a BKT temperature of 135 mK, and a signature of phase-sensitive interference patterns, emerges from a symmetry-breaking Fermi surface near v = -2 and D = 0, and it survives at a large in-plane magnetic field up to 2 T, strongly deviating from the Pauli limit. Moreover, we observe a strong modulation of the Ginzburg-Landau coherence length from 467 nm to 145 nm as the in-plane magnetic field increases from 0 to 2 T, whereas the critical current Ic barely changes. Unlike previous studies in RMG, we observe a competition between superconductivity and a correlated insulator at v = -2, monitored by displacement fields and twist angles. Note that we also observe integer Chern insulators with C = 1, 3, and -3 at v = 1/2, 1, and 3, respectively, for large D > 0 in the same superconducting device. The observations establish twisted rhombohedral graphene multilayers as a fascinating system to investigate unconventional superconductivity, high Chern number topology, and their interplay.

### Paper [154]
Title: Cage-related crystal-field effects in the paramagnetic range of PrB$_6$
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40176
Authors: Mehdi Amara, Christine Opagiste, Natalya Yu. Shitsevalova
Abstract: New insights into the paramagnetic range of PrB$_6$ below 50 K are revealed through specific-heat, thermal-expansion and magnetic measurements. The latent heats of the two successive magnetic transitions, together with an improved description of the lattice contribution, enable a detailed determination of the thermal variation of the magnetic entropy. Immediately above the first-order magnetic ordering temperature ($T_N = 6.7$ K), the magnetic entropy per Pr$^{3+}$ is close to that of a doublet, instead of the triplet crystal-field ground state identified by inelastic neutron scattering. Temperatures in excess of 30 K are required to reach the entropy of the threefold-degenerate ground state. This is reminiscent of the CeB$_6$ case and, similarly, strongly suggests a dynamical splitting of the crystal-field ground state within the boron cage.
