Further evidence of this splitting is provided by the analysis of the low-temperature paramagnetic susceptibility, which resembles that of a non-magnetic ground state. In addition, a large thermal-expansion anomaly is observed below 20 K, consistent with the expected centrifugal Jahn-Teller effect.

### Paper [155]
Title: N\'eel vector switching by magnetization-dependent layer-resolved spin-orbit torque
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40215
Authors: Sheng Pan, Weiwei Wang, Jie-Xiang Yu
Abstract: Layer-resolved spin-orbit torques offer a route to controlling antiferromagnetic order through local spin-charge conversion. First-principles calculations show that the layer-resolved spin Hall conductivity of the antiferromagnetic topological insulator $\mathrm{Mn{Bi}_{2}{Te}_{4}}$ consists of a magnetization-independent spin Hall term plus two spin anomalous Hall terms that are even in the N\'eel vector yet evolve with the local magnetic order, while local inversion asymmetry differentiates surface and inner layers. This response enables reversible, field-free switching of the N\'eel vector in finite-septuple-layer films. Although the magnetization-independent spin Hall term alone can reverse the order, the spin anomalous Hall terms provide magnetization-dependent feedback that redistributes the layer-resolved torques and reshapes the switching trajectory, offering a route to tailoring current-driven switching through layer and interface engineering.

### Paper [156]
Title: Stabilizing fluctuating Cooper pairs with a terahertz resonator
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40222
Authors: Frieder Lindel, Jonathan B. Curtis, J\'er\^ome Faist, Eugene Demler
Abstract: Coupling quantum materials to electromagnetic cavities or resonators without external driving has gained prominence as a new control mechanism. Here we develop an equilibrium theory of a mesoscopic, disordered two-dimensional superconductor coupled to a subwavelength terahertz resonator, focusing on the interplay between superconducting phase fluctuations in the sample and electromagnetic fluctuations of the resonator. Modeling the resonator as a lumped-element circuit, we show that the coupling enhances, rather than suppresses, the superconducting transition temperature $T_c$. When the sample is smaller than the Pearl length, phase fluctuations near the Berezinskii--Kosterlitz--Thouless (BKT) transition strongly amplify this enhancement. Crucially, the resistive quasiparticle response of the sample must be included in the analysis: at low frequencies the superfluid shunts the normal-fluid channel, reducing the total sample impedance. For representative parameters, a $1 \,\mu\mathrm{m} \times 1 \,\mu\mathrm{m}$, $8 $nm-thick disordered film, we find a relative $T_c$ enhancement of about $1\%$, an order of magnitude larger than the $0.1\% $ obtained when phase fluctuations are neglected. These results identify superconductors with soft phase fluctuations as promising platforms for equilibrium control with terahertz resonators.

### Paper [157]
Title: Chiral superconductivity on a closed Klein bottle: a chirality wall and its Majorana seam spectrum
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40251
Authors: Junhao (Eigen),  Wang
Abstract: We study a spinless $\pi$-flux lattice superconductor whose normal-state momentum-space fundamental domain is a Klein bottle. Within this model, a glide lift and a hidden antiunitary symmetry independently force the Bogoliubov--de Gennes (BdG) Chern number to vanish. A diagonal pairing breaks the antiunitary obstruction exactly when its real part is nonzero; this is necessary, not sufficient, for a chiral phase. We derive explicit Pfaffians determining Chern parity and identify three analytic families of gap closings in a real-pairing section. For a chiral state on a closed real-space Klein bottle, the prescribed pairing and gluing produce a $\Delta C=2$ chirality wall. A translation-invariant unfolding resolves two co-propagating Majorana branches. Their finite-size levels depend on circumference and holonomy; the near-zero pair is lifted at first order by allowed local seam perturbations. A cut-cylinder control has the same low-energy channel count, so gluing reconnects existing gapless content. We also distinguish literal gluing from deck projection: particle--hole symmetry exchanges the deck eigenblocks, precluding their interpretation as independent BdG quotient Hamiltonians. The contribution is a model-specific account of pairing compatibility and closed-surface seam spectra. The energy cost is evaluated for a fixed pairing background; neither a protected zero mode nor a self-consistent wall solution is claimed.

### Paper [158]
Title: Large Magnetocaloric Effect and Adiabatic Temperature Change in the Tellurate: Gd2TeO6
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40296
Authors: Abhijit Nayak (Department of Physics, National Institute of Technology, Andhra Pradesh, Tadepalligudem, India), Gurunathan Angusamy (National Institute of Chemical Physics and Biophysics, Tallinn, Estonia), Kusampal Yadav (School of Physical Sciences, Indian Association for the Cultivation of Science, Kolkata, West Bengal, India), S. K. Panda (Department of Physics, Bennet University, Greater Noida, Uttar Pradesh, India), Devajyoti Mukherjee (School of Physical Sciences, Indian Association for the Cultivation of Science, Kolkata, West Bengal, India), Raivo Stern (National Institute of Chemical Physics and Biophysics, Tallinn, Estonia), Tanmoy Chakrabarty (Division of Sciences, Krea University, Sri City, Andhra Pradesh, India), Krishnamurthy Jyothinagaram (Department of Physics, National Institute of Technology, Andhra Pradesh, Tadepalligudem, India)
Abstract: We investigate the structural, magnetic, thermodynamic, and magnetocaloric properties of polycrystalline Gd2TeO6, a rare-earth tellurate unexplored for cryogenic magnetic refrigeration. Powder X-ray diffraction confirms single-phase orthorhombic symmetry (P212121), featuring two crystallographically distinct seven-coordinate Gd sites. Magnetization measurements reveal no conventional long-range magnetic ordering down to 2 K, although low-field magnetic irreversibility emerges below approximately 4 K. Curie-Weiss analysis yields a Weiss temperature of -1.25 K, indicating weak predominantly antiferromagnetic interactions. The magnetization reaches approximately 97% of the theoretical saturation moment at 7 T with negligible hysteresis. Heat-capacity measurements reveal no sharp zero-field anomaly above 2 K, while a broad field-induced maximum indicates redistribution of low-energy magnetic entropy. Debye-Einstein analysis separates the lattice and magnetic contributions. At 2.5 K, the maximum magnetic entropy changes (-DeltaSM) reach 21.40, 48.51, and 54.81 J kg-1 K-1 for field changes of 0-2, 0-5, and 0-7 T, respectively. The corresponding volumetric entropy change reaches 400.50 mJ cm-3 K-1. Measurements up to 14 T yield (-DeltaSM) = 63.51 J kg-1 K-1, approaching the theoretical limit of 64.25 J kg-1 K-1. The estimated adiabatic temperature change is approximately 22.7 K for 0-7 T. First-principles calculations with GGA+U approximation reveal competing antiferromagnetic states, highlighting Gd2TeO6 as a promising candidate for cryogenic magnetocaloric applications.

### Paper [159]
Title: Topological Quantum Spin Liquids in a Ladder with Majorana fermions and p-wave Superconductivity
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40297
Authors: Magali Korolev, Fan Yang, Erik S. S{\o}rensen, Karyn Le Hur
Abstract: In this article, we address the possibility of topological quantum spin liquids with only short-range (spin) correlations in the whole phase diagram in a ladder system, through an exactly solvable model which represents a generalization of the two-dimensional Kitaev honeycomb spin model to a ribbon geometry. This system gives rise to $\mathbb{Z}_2$ gauge fields emerging in a static way in the ground state through Majorana fermions which then produce either $0$ or $\pi$ flux. Through a quantum field theory perspective, mathematical and Density Matrix Renormalization Group results, we present our progress on the geometrical, topological and physical classification of this model through observables navigating on the whole phase diagram presenting three spin-liquid phases with a spin gap and a metallic phase in the sense of Majorana fermions. We propose a bulk-edge correspondence revealing the presence of protected zero-energy Majorana fermions at the edge in the phases with a gap and revealing also the presence of itinerant Majorana fermions in the bulk in the topological liquid phase. The proposed approach allows us to distinguish between properties of the brickwall ladder giving rise to a special line and those of the square ladder allowing for the gapless Majorana liquid phase. This model gives some hope on finding solutions for topological quantum spin models, e.g. with $\pi$ flux, on a square lattice geometry. This approach also allows for a correspondence onto models of topological superconductivity.

### Paper [160]
Title: Structural signatures of primary and secondary relaxation during physical aging of a metallic glass
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40298
Authors: Beatrice Baraldi, Stefano Marchesin, Davide Marchesi, Lara Piemontese, Francesco Dallari, S{\o}ren S. S{\o}rensen, Eloi Pineda, Marco Di Michiel, Giulio Monaco, Peihao Sun
Abstract: Physical aging drives glasses toward equilibrium and changes their thermodynamic and mechanical properties, but the accompanying microscopic structural evolution remains poorly understood. Here, using our recently developed low-background setup combining fast scanning calorimetry with \emph{in-situ} X-ray total scattering, we investigate microscopic structural changes during physical aging in Pd$_{42.5}$Cu$_{30}$Ni$_{7.5}$P$_{20}$ metallic glass. The measurements span an unprecedented six orders of magnitude in time and a temperature range extending from above the glass transition temperature to more than 150\,K below it. We observe dominant structural changes near the first and second peaks of the structure factor. Near and above the glass transition temperature, changes in the first sharp diffraction peak closely follow enthalpy relaxation and are associated predominantly with increasing degrees of medium-range ordering. At lower temperatures, the enthalpy evolution can no longer be described by this contribution alone, revealing an additional, faster relaxation process that correlates with changes near the second diffraction peak and thus short-range structural rearrangements, including increased Pd--Pd correlations. These results establish a direct connection between thermodynamic relaxation and structural evolution, providing strong evidence that primary and secondary aging processes are associated with distinct structural length scales.

### Paper [161]
Title: Transport in spinful non-Hermitian gauge lattices
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40317
Authors: Hamed Ghaemi-Dizicheh, Shahram Dehdashti, Janis N\"otzel
Abstract: We extend the generalized transfer-matrix method introduced in [H. Ghaemi-Dizicheh, Phys.\ Rev.\ B \textbf{107}, 125155 (2023)] to one-dimensional spinful non-Hermitian tight-binding lattices with matrix-valued Abelian and non-Abelian gauge couplings. We derive a minimal channel-resolved transfer matrix and identify its eigenvalues with the generalized Bloch factors. The same transfer matrix determines the periodic- and open-boundary spectra, generalized Brillouin zone, skin localization, and finite-sample scattering conditions. In the Abelian limit, the model separates into fixed spin sectors, yielding exact spectral results and closed finite-length transport expressions in terms of Chebyshev polynomials. These results reveal bipolar spin-dependent skin localization and distinguish the reciprocal hopping scale, which governs poles, zeros, and reflectionlessness, from the directed-amplification factor controlling transmission asymmetry, transparency, and invisibility. For non-Abelian gauge fields, noncommuting link matrices coherently mix the spin sectors, such that four transfer eigenmodes jointly determine the non-Bloch spectrum and transport eigenchannels. We show that this mixing produces spin-hybridized lasing and coherent-perfect-absorption channels, Zeeman-tunable lasing thresholds, and direction-dependent reflectionless transport accompanied by pronounced spin conversion. The resulting boundary-condition criteria describe lasing, coherent perfect absorption, reflectionlessness, transparency, and invisibility, while identifying spin polarization and conversion as experimentally accessible signatures that distinguish Abelian from non-Abelian non-Hermitian transport.

### Paper [162]
Title: Multidimensional revival dynamics in a bosonic many-body system
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40353
Authors: Denis Ullmo (LPTMS, Universit\'e Paris-Saclay / CNRS, Orsay), Steven Tomsovic (LPTMS, Universit\'e Paris-Saclay / CNRS, Orsay, Department of Physics and Astronomy, Washington State University, Pullman), Gabriel M. Lando (LPTMS, Universit\'e Paris-Saclay / CNRS, Orsay), Peter Schlagheck (CESAM research unit, University of Liege, Liege)
Abstract: The collapse and revival of localized states in many-body systems is a quintessential example of a quantum interference phenomenon that cannot be explained purely within mean-field theories. Historically, the majority of previous work on quantum revivals has been in a Schr\"odinger mechanics context, typically for effectively 1D systems with a focus on the autocorrelation function as the key detection observable. The general revival subject acquires a far greater complexity in the bosonic many-body context that offers a larger variety of observables and intrinsically implies high dimensionality. Here, the focus is on the simplest nontrivial (i.e., not 1D) bosonic many-body system, namely a symmetric two-site Bose-Hubbard model. The non-equilibrium situation of a coherent initial state prepared on one of the two sites of this integrable 2D system in the strongly self-trapping regime is considered. The revival behaviors emerging from the resulting dynamics within expectation values, coherences, and the autocorrelation function are theoretically derived, and the entanglement behavior is illustrated. Several new features, unknown so far in the Schr\"odinger context and identified here in terms of five ``puzzles'', are encountered and discussed, among them, a synchronized resurgence behavior, differing revivals times depending on the specific observable, and a manifestation of a precession that appears in the underlying mean field dynamics. These new phenomena can be entirely understood through a comprehensive semiclassical theory, building upon the underlying classical (mean-field) phase-space structure. A quantitative comparison of the latter with semiclassical predictions and quantum perturbative calculations yields an overall excellent agreement and confirms that a full semiclassical theory is pivotal for the understanding of these genuinely quantum many-body interference phenomena.

### Paper [163]
Title: Soliton-Lattice Origin of Incommensurability in High-Pressure Antimony
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40399
Authors: M. Smirman, K. Sun, H. Sheng, I. I. Mazin
Abstract: The microscopic origin of incommensurability in host-guest crystals remains unresolved. Here we show that the high-pressure antimony host-guest phase Sb-II is a soliton lattice, where localized guest-host misregistries decorate a
  commensurate 4/3 reference structure. First-principles calculations reveal a corrugated and hysteretic registry landscape, motivating a Frenkel-Kontorova description that accounts for soliton-lattice approximants of the form
  $(4n+1)/(3n+1)$. Machine-learning-potential calculations of these approximants show an energetically preferred soliton spacing, and their localized misregistry cores are also verified by first-principles relaxations. At finite
  temperature, the thermally broadened soliton lattice appears as an atomic density wave along the guest chains, consistent with experimental diffraction. This soliton-registry picture provides a microscopic mechanism for host-guest
  incommensurability in Sb-II.

### Paper [164]
Title: Light-induced chiral altermagnets from conventional antiferromagnets
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40410
Authors: Jorge Cardenas-Gamboa, Devika Jayadeep, Aritz Leonardo, Gregory A. Fiete, Jeroen van den Brink, Maia G. Vergniory
Abstract: Altermagnets combine compensated collinear magnetic order with momentum-dependent nonrelativistic spin splitting, offering new opportunities for spintronic applications. Here, we propose a route to optically induce altermagnetism through coherent lattice control. Using symmetry analysis, we identify phonon distortions that lift the spin degeneracy of conventional antiferromagnets while preserving the rotational symmetries connecting opposite-spin sublattices. Among these, geometric chiral phonons provide a particularly appealing pathway, linking the emergence of altermagnetism to structural chirality. Taking Fe$_2$TeO$_6$ as a prototype, we demonstrate that a linearly polarized mid-infrared pulse can drive a structural instability through negative biquadratic coupling between an infrared-active phonon and an optically silent, symmetry-breaking mode. First-principles-based lattice dynamics reveal the emergence of a transient chiral distortion, while electronic-structure calculations establish that the light-induced structure exhibits nonrelativistic spin splitting characteristic of altermagnetism. Our results establish a symmetry-guided pathway toward the ultrafast optical control of altermagnetic states through nonlinear phononics.

### Paper [165]
Title: Micromagnetic Lagrangian and Hamiltonian Normal-Mode Formulation of Cavity Magnon-Photon Interactions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40416
Authors: Claudio Serpico, Marco Lembo, Giovanni Miano
Abstract: We investigate magnon-photon interactions in a resonant cavity starting from the coupled Maxwell and Landau-Lifshitz equations linearized about an arbitrary micromagnetic ground state. By introducing an electric vector potential and a suitable interaction Lagrangian, the formulation avoids the cancellation of large magnetostatic contributions that arises in the conventional magnetic-vector-potential description and becomes inaccurate under modal truncation. The magnetization and cavity fields are expanded in the normal modes of the uncoupled magnetic and electromagnetic subsystems, with coupling coefficients given by overlap integrals of the corresponding eigenfunctions. The resulting multimode Lagrangian has a noncanonical structure due to the gyrotropic terms, and the corresponding Hamiltonian formulation is obtained using the Faddeev-Jackiw approach. The radiative magnon-photon interaction is encoded in the resulting Poisson-bracket structure, while the cavity-induced modification of the magnetostatic field enters explicitly in the Hamiltonian. The coupled system is diagonalized through a generalized Bogoliubov transformation, yielding canonical polariton variables that provide a natural basis for quantization. Application to a perpendicularly magnetized YIG disk in a rectangular microwave cavity shows hybridization between the cavity mode and several magnetostatic modes, together with appreciable cavity-wall-induced frequency shifts when the sample is placed close to the conducting walls.

### Paper [166]
Title: Seeding and Amplification of Chaos in Long-Range Interacting Spin Chains
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40417
Authors: Sareen Sabra, Jeffrey G. Rau
Abstract: Truncating weak interactions is a natural simplification of a many-body Hamiltonian, but the validity of this approximation need not be uniform in distance and time. In a chaotic system, a weak long-range coupling can seed a small disturbance far from its source, after which the local dynamics can amplify that disturbance exponentially. We study this mechanism in an infinite-temperature classical Heisenberg spin chain with a dominant nearest-neighbor exchange and a weak interaction tail. Using a classical decorrelator, we compare exponentially and power-law decaying tails over several decades in interaction strength. For exponentially decaying interactions, the long-distance front remains ballistic when the tail is sufficiently shallow, with a velocity controlled by the competition between the spatial decay of the seed and the local chaotic growth. For sufficiently rapid exponential decay, the nearest-neighbor front, which is defined by an intrinsic butterfly velocity $v_b$, is recovered within the accessible space-time window. For power-law interactions, the arrival time is logarithmic in distance for all exponents studied, corresponding to an exponentially expanding spatial front; the power-law exponent controls the coefficient of this logarithm. We interpret both behaviors using a seeding-and-amplification picture in which long-range couplings generate weak disturbances throughout the chain whose growth is governed primarily by the local nearest-neighbor dynamics. In this picture, the overall strength of the tail primarily controls when the long-range front becomes visible, while its spatial decay controls the propagation law. Thus a weak interaction tail can leave conventional spin-dynamical observables essentially unchanged while qualitatively altering the long-distance propagation of decorrelation.

### Paper [167]
Title: Electric field control of anisotropic spin traffic between spin-filtering and magnon-scattering in a layered antiferromagnet
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40423
Authors: Mohamed Soliman, Jaime Garrido Aldea, Davy Borowski, Dorye L. Esteras, Neeraj K. Rajak, Niels Chapuis, Abdelkarim Ouerghi, Arnaud Gloppe, J\'er\^ome Robert, Bernard Doudin, Takashi Taniguchi, Kenji Watanabe, Jos\'e H. Garc\'ia, Stephan Roche, Jean-Fran\c{c}ois Dayen
Abstract: Layered A-type antiferromagnetic (AFM) semiconductors offer a unique platform for spintronic devices by combining robust magnetism with gate-tunable semiconducting properties. We present a dual-gate field-effect transistor based on CrSBr, a layered A-type AFM with pronounced transport anisotropy and complex spin dynamics. Our device provides independent control over carrier density and displacement field. Systematic transport measurements reveal that the displacement field, rather than doping, predominantly governs the sign and magnitude of magnetoresistance ratio (MRR). We identify the origins of both negative and positive MRR components and connect them to interlayer and intralayer spin-dependent transport mechanisms. We interpret these findings with ab initio calculations and an effective spin-circuit model that reproduces the anisotropic magnetotransport. Our results identify the displacement field as an efficient electrical control parameter of spin transport in CrSBr and suggest that multi-gate architectures could provide similar control in other layered antiferromagnetic semiconductors.

### Paper [168]
Title: Teleported free energy simulations
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40428
Authors: Davide Carbone (Centre d'\'Etudes et de Recherche en Informatique et Communications)
Abstract: Escorted free energy simulations reduce dissipation with a flow field that follows the equilibrium distribution. A continuous flow, however, cannot transfer probability between metastable basins. We show that any escort then faces a variance floor set by the change in basin weights, and lift it by adding teleportation: jumps at a rate proportional to the driving rate. We derive the corresponding nonequilibrium identity, the zero-dissipation condition, and the minimal number of jumps it requires, and illustrate the gain on an exactly solvable model and a tilted double well.

### Paper [169]
Title: Time Reflection-Refraction Crossover at Topological Kramers Edge States
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40442
Authors: Junpeng Hou, Ya-Jie Wu
Abstract: Time-reversal symmetry protects helical edge states against static nonmagnetic backscattering. We show that spatially uniform temporal modulation produces a time reflection-refraction crossover within the same quantum spin Hall phase while preserving time-reversal symmetry of the instantaneous Hamiltonian. A finite gap to the bulk and an edge splitting that vanishes at the Kramers crossing allow the modulation to suppress bulk excitation while remaining nonadiabatic between the edge channels. Virtual transitions through bulk states enable coherent conversion between counter-propagating edge channels at fixed crystal momentum. Near the crossing, $qT$ governs the leading crossover dynamics for a fixed ramp shape, where $q$ is the momentum offset and $T$ the ramp duration. Coherent interference can suppress and restore reflection as the ramp slows. In the adiabatic limit, two initially time-reversed wave packets follow their respective propagation branches and acquire equal geometric displacements. Their group-velocity contributions cancel, isolating the contribution from the Berry curvature to be directly observable. Temporal modulation thus controls protected states and quantum geometry along a topological edge.

### Paper [170]
Title: Static dipolar impurity in a Fermi gas
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40460
Authors: Eugen Dizer, Xin Chen, Richard Schmidt, Arthur Christianen
Abstract: We consider a static dipolar impurity immersed in a Fermi gas and show how dipolar interactions shape both the few-body scattering properties and the resulting polaron physics. Using Fumi's theorem, we connect the anisotropic phase shifts directly to the ground-state energy, and compute the exact impurity spectrum via the functional determinant approach. The resulting spectrum is a convolution of independent even- and odd-wave contributions, giving rise to a ground state with simultaneously occupied even- and odd-wave bound states whose separation is tunable via the dipole length. A variational ansatz limited to one particle-hole excitation fails to capture this doubly occupied ground state. We further find that long-range dipolar interactions weaken Anderson's orthogonality catastrophe, reducing the power-law decay of the quasiparticle weight. These effects can be tested with ultracold polar molecules, where microwave shielding and independent tunability of interactions enable experimental access to the predicted regimes.

### Paper [171]
Title: Chiral $p$-Wave Superconductivity Proximate to a Fractional Chern Insulator and a Topological Charge Density Wave in a Moir\'e Chern Band
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40469
Authors: Yunze Ouyang, Fengcheng Wu
Abstract: We study a spin-polarized moir\'e Chern band of twisted MoTe$_2$ with short-range repulsion, finding superconductivity proximate to a fractional Chern insulator at filling $\nu=2/3$ and a topological charge density wave at $\nu=3/4$. Exact diagonalization reveals pair binding over $0.7\lesssim\nu\lesssim0.77$, consistent with experiment, along with finite superfluid stiffness, chiral $p$-wave pairing correlations, and adiabatic connectivity to an attractive model. At weak repulsion, Kohn-Luttinger theory supports a Fermi-surface instability with the same pairing symmetry. The superconducting state has a Bogoliubov-de Gennes Chern number $C_{\mathrm{BdG}}=-1$, opposite to the normal-state Chern number at $\nu=1$. Our results reveal interplay among superconductivity, fractional topology, and charge order.

### Paper [172]
Title: High-throughput quantification of interfacial protein load in monodisperse oil-in-water emulsions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40506
Authors: Varun Potdar (1Department of Chemical Engineering, Texas Tech University, Lubbock, TX, USA), Alexander Favela (1Department of Chemical Engineering, Texas Tech University, Lubbock, TX, USA), Michael Boehm (Motif FoodWorks, Inc., Boston, MA, USA), Stefan Baier (School of Chemical Engineering, The University of Queensland, Brisbane, QLD 4072, Australia), Siva Vanapalli (1Department of Chemical Engineering, Texas Tech University, Lubbock, TX, USA)
Abstract: Food proteins play a critical role as emulsifiers, where adsorption at the oil and water interface governs emulsion formation and stability. However, quantitative characterization of interfacial protein load remains challenging due to polydispersity in conventional emulsification methods. We combine microfluidic generation of monodisperse emulsions with colorimetric protein quantification to measure the post-collection initial interfacial load ($\Gamma_0$) and subsequently monitored over 48 h to obtain the saturation load ($\Gamma_\infty$). Interfacial load exhibited a strong bulk concentration-dependent trend and was well described by a pseudo-first-order model. Both $\Gamma_0$ and $\Gamma_\infty$ followed sublinear scaling with concentration ($\Gamma_0$ ~ C^{0.21}$, $\Gamma_\infty$ ~ C^{0.12}$). Interfacial loads were largely independent of droplet diameter, with modest reductions in initial loading for the smallest droplets at low protein concentration. Measured loads exceeded idealized monolayer predictions and fell between bilayer and trilayer bounds, consistent with accumulation beyond a simplified monolayer model. Additionally, high-throughput mapping was performed across three proteins, seven concentrations, three pH values, and two ionic strengths, revealing that $\alpha$-Casein exhibited higher interfacial loads than $\beta$-Casein and Whey protein Isolate, with maximum adsorption near the isoelectric point and at elevated ionic strength. In summary, this study establishes a quantitative experimental framework for linking protein properties, formulation conditions, and interfacial behavior using monodisperse microfluidic emulsions, while requiring only milligram-scale protein quantities.

### Paper [173]
Title: Quasi-Phases of Quantum Matter
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40526
Authors: Nick G. Jones, Paul Fendley, Nathanan Tantivasadakarn, Ruben Verresen
Abstract: Within quantum many-body phase diagrams, continuous transition lines can appear and then terminate. However, it has remained unclear whether the neighboring regimes can be meaningfully described as distinct phases, akin to water and vapour, since any sharp characterization would have to go beyond the usual notion of phases of matter. We answer this in the affirmative by introducing quasi-phases: gapped regimes near a critical point that are connected in the broader phase diagram, but not by any gapped path accessible from small perturbations of the critical theory. The physical consequences include interface modes between regimes in the same phase of matter. More striking still, describing both the extended critical line and the gapped path connecting the regimes requires a bigger multicritical parent theory. In spin-chain examples with continuous terminating lines with central charge $c=1$, we find multicritical $c=3/2$ and $c=9/5$ orbifolds describing so-called 'quotient symmetry protected' and 'representation symmetry protected' topological phenomena. While one-dimensional symmetry-protected topological phases are labeled by projective representation classes, these quasi-phases can have interface modes carrying higher-dimensional linear representations. We turn this into a constructive principle through a weakening of the Lieb-Schultz-Mattis anomaly. Supporting distinct quasi-phases makes an unnecessary critical point infrared-necessary, even if it is unnecessary at the ultraviolet scale. This condition is more restrictive than simply having a codimension-one critical line, revealing richer structure within phases of matter than their conventional classification suggests.

### Paper [174]
Title: Odd Elasticity in Strain-Gradient Continua: Anisotropy Classification and Non-Hermitian Elastodynamics
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40581
Authors: Payam Poorsolhjouy
Abstract: Odd elasticity extends continuum mechanics to active nonconservative systems containing internal energy sources, such as robotic active metamaterials with nonreciprocal feedback. Modeling these systems as effective continua requires relaxing Maxwell-Betti reciprocity, which results in stiffness tensors lacking major symmetry. In this contribution, we extend this framework to 3D strain-gradient odd elasticity to incorporate the length scales inherent in these heterogeneous systems. By applying the full hierarchy of 3D point-group symmetries along chiral and achiral branches, we derive the exhaustive set of fourth-, fifth-, and sixth-order odd elastic stiffness tensors. Further, utilizing the dynamic principle of virtual work, we formulate the equilibrium equations, higher-order boundary conditions, and the generalized acoustic tensor for elastodynamic analysis. The results demonstrate that nonreciprocal moduli survive even under full isotropy in the sixth-order stiffness, under hemitropy in the fifth-order stiffness, and under cubic symmetry in fourth-order stiffness. In contrast, the elastodynamic analysis reveals that under isotropic and hemitropic limits, despite the underlying odd stiffness moduli, the acoustic tensor remains unconditionally Hermitian. Non-Hermitian elastodynamics is shown to require anisotropy. By numerically analyzing a transversely hemitropic odd elastic continuum, we demonstrate that this symmetry reduction results in non-Hermitian elastodynamic phenomena including exceptional points in the dispersion spectra and active energy exchange through simultaneous amplification and attenuation of the elastic wave components. These results provide the necessary theoretical foundation for future multiscale homogenization frameworks for anisotropic strain-gradient active microstructures, and for the design of nonreciprocal strain-gradient metamaterials.

### Paper [175]
Title: Algebraic theory of abelian fractons and beyond
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40596
Authors: Wilbur Shirley, Evan Wickenden, Bowen Yang, Agn\`es Beaudry, Michael Hermele
Abstract: We develop an algebraic theory of fusion, mobility, and statistics in abelian gapped fracton orders and topological phases. The framework generalizes the algebraic description of abelian anyons to systems with restricted-mobility and spatially extended excitations. We consider translation-invariant bosonic systems whose pointlike and extended excitations have finite fusion order. The basic objects are braided fusion complexes (BFCs): chain complexes encoding fusion and mobility, equipped with a braiding chain map encoding statistics. Their equivalence classes define braided fusion theories (BFTs). Our framework encompasses arbitrary translation-invariant Pauli codes, as well as all abelian anyon theories in two spatial dimensions, including chiral theories. We give evidence for the conjecture that every nondegenerate BFT can be realized as the excitation theory of a gapped phase and that the BFT describing such a phase is a phase invariant. We derive constraints on pointlike fusion and mobility, showing, for example, that neither the Pauli X nor Z sectors of the X-cube and anisotropic models can occur alone in a nondegenerate BFT. For nondegenerate theories with only pointlike excitations, we extract a simpler theory of mutual statistics, consisting of a fusion module and a map to its statistical dual. This theory of mutual statistics is an invariant of the BFT and, at odd fusion order, gives an equivalent description of the full braided fusion theory. We interpret remote detection as the generalization of mutual statistics to fracton orders. In the BFC setting, we give a precise formulation of the excitation-detector principle: every nontrivial excitation can be detected by an operator supported at spatial infinity, and every nontrivial detector detects some excitation. Assuming a unique infinite-space ground state, we prove that this principle is equivalent to nondegeneracy.

### Paper [176]
Title: Light-Cone Friedel Oscillations: a Window into van Hove Superconductivity
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40648
Authors: Archisman Panigrahi, Vladislav Poliakov, Leonid Levitov
Abstract: Van Hove (vH) singularities can enhance superconductivity by generating a large pairing susceptibility. Yet, it remains challenging to experimentally establish whether pairing really occurs near these points. We show that the saddle-point dispersion near a vH point gives rise to a `light-cone' vH-selective behavior of Friedel oscillations, which is drastically different from the familiar concentric circles. Each vH point maps onto its own real-space light-cone sector, allowing STM around a single impurity to measure whether---and how strongly---that saddle-point quasiparticle sector is gapped. A superconducting gap near vH points suppresses the oscillations in the directions corresponding to peaks in the gap function angular dependence, a behavior that provides a signature of vH-related pairing at STM-accessible length scales. This STM protocol, targeting vH superconductivity, is capable of determining whether superconducting pairing emerges near the vH points or elsewhere on the Fermi surface.

### Paper [177]
Title: Renormalization for Robust Memory without Synchronization
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40651
Authors: Shashwat Chakraborty, Jong Yeon Lee
Abstract: Local error correction can preserve classical information against noise, but protection under synchronized updates need not survive when sites evolve at independent random times. Establishing stability is particularly challenging when protection relies on the collective action of competing local rules. Here we develop a spacetime renormalization framework that reveals how independently acting local updates can collectively stabilize memory. We identify sufficient conditions under which the geometry of stable interfaces and suppression of defect propagation produce reliable erosion at large scales. Applying this framework, we prove stability of the asynchronous \textsf{R} squeezing code and introduce robust processes in two and three dimensions. In these systems, both encoded phases persist under sufficiently weak noise, even when the noise favors one phase. Our results provide a criterion for designing autonomous memories sustained by competing local stochastic dynamics.

### Paper [178]
Title: Variational wavefunctions for ideal anyon gas: superfluid and skyrmion Hall crystal
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40657
Authors: Ya-Hui Zhang
Abstract: The ground state of an ideal gas of anyons with statistical angle $\theta\in(0,\pi)$ is a long-standing open problem, with renewed interest from the recent observations of fractional Chern insulators. In this work we construct variational wavefunctions that build in the exact anyon statistics and combine theory-guided composite-boson, composite-fermion and parton wavefunctions with neural-network correlations on the sphere. For spinless and SU(2) semions we confirm the paired anyon superfluid, well below the best crystal states. At $\theta=\pi/3$ liquids and crystals are closer in energy for the spinless case. For $\theta=\pi/3$ anyons with SU(3) flavor, we find two candidates. One is a skyrmion Hall crystal, in which the anyons form an integer quantum Hall state in the effective magnetic field from the spin texture. The other is a chiral color superconductor of six-anyon clusters, the color-flavor-locked pairing descendant of a holon metal with SU(3) color gauge symmetry and SU(3) flavor symmetry; its wavefunction is a hyperdeterminant rather than a product of parton determinants. Within our variational ansatz the color superconductor has better energy than both crystals and the color-flavor locked phases with U(1)$^2$ and Z$_3$ gauge groups. For twisted MoTe$_2$ this suggests that the reentrant integer quantum anomalous Hall phase is the skyrmion Hall crystal of the doped anyons, and that the observed superconductivity may be the color superconductor, which can undergo a transition to an $f+if$ BCS superconductor with a jump of the chiral central charge by 3.

### Paper [179]
Title: Operator quantum singular value transformation: Spectral mapping, design and topological invariants
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2401.09007
Authors: Chusei Kiumi, Yohei Tanaka, Akito Suzuki
Abstract: Quantum singular value transformation (QSVT) provides a unifying framework for quantum algorithms by applying the polynomials of quantum signal processing (QSP) to block-encoded matrices. We connect this framework with spectral analysis, design and topological protection in condensed matter physics. Extending QSVT beyond matrices to possibly infinite-dimensional operators (operator QSVT), we establish the following. First, it transforms every block of the operator by the QSP polynomials; no singular value decomposition is needed. Second, a spectral mapping theorem determines the full spectrum of the QSVT operator, including continuous spectra, from the embedded operator through the QSP polynomial, allowing spectral analysis without translation invariance. Third, the spectral mapping turns QSP into spectral design: the QSP phases can stretch, fold and separate the bands and place the eigenvalues. Finally, the kernel sectors that conventional QSVT usually sets aside carry a topological index, invariant under the choice of QSP phases, local perturbations and gap-preserving deformations, which guarantees topologically protected states. These results unify two quantum walks: Szegedy's walk from quantum algorithms and the split-step walk from condensed matter physics. Their kernel sectors encode the graph's Euler characteristic and the difference of bulk winding numbers, respectively. For the split-step walk, QSP breaks chiral symmetry but not the topological protection, and can move the protected state into a continuous band to form a bound state in the continuum (BIC).

### Paper [180]
Title: Universal Critical Jump for Inverse-Square Ising Ferromagnets
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36131
Authors: Dyuman Bhattacharya
Abstract: For every translation-invariant ferromagnetic Ising chain with $n^2J_n\to B>0$, we prove $2\beta_cBm_c^2=1$, without restrictions on the finite core or the tail convergence rate. The proof bounds the energy of macroscopic interfaces and rules them out if the critical product exceeds one. Despite the nonzero jump, connected susceptibility is infinite both at criticality and in a neighboring ordered interval. At the critical coupling, the excess magnetization decays more slowly than any positive power of the field as the field tends to zero. We also prove the jump relation in continuous time, for a positive-Stieltjes class of Ohmic spin-boson models.

### Paper [181]
Title: Defining 2-group symmetries on lattices and extracting their anomalies
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37745
Authors: Yitao Feng, Yu-An Chen
Abstract: Ordinary $0$-form symmetries $G$ can mix with $1$-form symmetries $\Pi_2$ to form a $2$-group $\mathcal{G}$. We give a concrete lattice definition for a class of finite $2$-group symmetries in terms of local relations among symmetry operators. These relations identify the Postnikov class $[\rho]\in H^3(BG,\Pi_2)$ that characterizes the mixing. We develop a method to extract anomalies of these symmetries in one spatial dimension by restricting symmetry operations to an interval and comparing their composition near its endpoints. We also construct explicit qubit symmetry actions in $d=1,2,3$ spatial dimensions for a $2$-group with $G=\Pi_2=\mathbb{Z}_2$. These actions share the same nontrivial Postnikov class but include both anomaly-free and anomalous realizations. Their cohomological anomalies are classified by $H^{d+2}(B\mathcal G,U(1))$, which is $\mathbb{Z}_2$ for $d=1,2$ and $\mathbb{Z}_2\times\mathbb{Z}_2$ for $d=3$, and our constructions realize every class in these dimensions. Our results connect higher-group symmetry and its anomalies to concrete lattice operations, providing a microscopic framework for studying anomalous symmetries.

### Paper [182]
Title: Interpretable neural equation learning for high-dimensional materials data
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37880
Authors: Philip Jacobson, Prasad Iyer, Remi Dingreville, Saaketh Desai
Abstract: Equation learning seeks to discover compact, interpretable equations from data using various machine-learning techniques. However, few workflows discover and interpret learned equations in sparse, high-dimensional materials datasets. We present nn-EQL, an equation-learning framework that generates candidate equations through iterative training, pruning, and retraining of a neural network with customizable, user-specified neuron-level activation functions. Candidate equations are ranked using a minimum description length (MDL)-inspired score, balancing error and sparsity, and diagnosed to assess term-wise contributions, input-scaled sensitivities, and uncertainties. We demonstrate nn-EQL on standard AI-Feynman benchmarks as well as multiscale high-dimensional materials datasets such as finite-element simulations of heterogeneous materials, and high-throughput experimental thin-film deposition data. nn-EQL integrates with tabular data and high-dimensional data via standard encoder models, learning equations and providing insight into learned terms. Looking forward, integrating physically interpretable representations, multimodal and multi-fidelity data, and adaptive data acquisition could enable equation learning to formulate and test mechanistic hypotheses.

### Paper [183]
Title: Quantum gravity phenomenology in quadrupolar Bose-Einstein condensates: Finite-temperature thermodynamics and projected bounds
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37930
Authors: Asma Tahar Taiba, Abdelaali Boudjemaa
Abstract: We study the thermodynamics of quadrupolar Bose-Einstein condensates in the realm of the generalized uncertainty principle (GUP) for the quest of understanding and testing quantum gravity (QG). Using both quadratic GUP (QGUP) and linear-quadratic GUP (LQGUP) which incorporate modified density of states, we calculate corrections to the condensed fraction, critical temperature, equation of state and other thermodynamic quantities. We find that the intriguing interplay of quadrupole-quadrupole interactions (QQIs) and the GUP may strongly modify the finite-temperature properties of the condensate, with the LQGUP generally predicting larger corrections than the QGUP, although their relative magnitudes depend on the chosen parameters and the validity of the perturbative expansion. We further estimate the sensitivity of the critical temperature to GUP-induced effects by imposing an assumed fractional temperature resolution which provides projected upper bounds on the GUP deformation parameters. Our findings demonstrate that quadrupole-quadrupole interacting Bose gases provide a promising platform for testing GUP-induced deviations from standard quantum mechanics and for probing QG phenomenology through measurements of finite-temperature many-body observables. They also motivate future studies using realistic system-specific parameters.

### Paper [184]
Title: Symmetry-resolved periods in a cyclic-dominance automaton: separating intrinsic from lattice-induced periodicity
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37990
Authors: Francisco J. Mu\~noz, Juan Carlos Nu\~no
Abstract: Periodic attractors of spatially extended systems possess not one period but a hierarchy of periods, one for each level of observation and symmetry quotient, and this hierarchy separates how a pattern evolves from how it fits the finite domain. For a synchronous threshold model of cyclic dominance on an $L\times L$ torus, equivalent to the three-color cyclic cellular automaton of Fisch, Gravner and Griffeath, we measure for every attractor a \emph{tuple} of periods: that of the populations ($\Tpop$), of the full configuration ($\Tst$), and of the configuration up to color rotations ($\Tcol$), lattice symmetries ($\Tgeo$) and the full space--color group ($\TG$). Equivariance implies exact divisibility relations and the factorization $\Tst=\TG\,m$, where $m$ counts the applications of the symmetry $h$ that maps the configuration onto itself after $\TG$ steps: $\TG$ and the type of $h$ belong to the pattern, $m$ to the lattice, and the attractors are relative periodic orbits whose full period emerges from the interaction between the intrinsic dynamics and the lattice symmetries. A family of attractors built around a pair of counter-rotating spirals, with $\Tpop=24$ and $\TG=8$, persists from $L=9$ to $L=40$ while $\Tst$ takes values from 24 to 960, given in every case by the order of $h$; on a rectangular $9\times12$ torus it takes the value 48, absent from all square tori. With more than $4.5\times10^5$ runs at each of $L=8$ and $L=9$ we estimate the basins of attraction: fixed points take 95--97\% of the configuration space, and no single period is enough to tell the oscillating attractors apart. The same analysis locates local diversity: it is balanced whenever the cycle has a color symmetry and, on large lattices, it is concentrated at the spiral cores.

### Paper [185]
Title: Reducing the cost of ancilla purification for Majorana quantum computation
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37991
Authors: Piotr {\'C}wikli{\'n}ski, {\L}ukasz Fojcik, Micha{\l} Studzi{\'n}ski
Abstract: Braiding Majorana zero modes does not by itself provide a universal set of quantum gates. Additional operations can be supplied through ancillary states, whose preparation need not have the same protection as braiding. Here, we consider purification of the non-Gaussian ancilla used for an entangling gate in Bravyi's scheme. We keep his elementary braid-and-fusion comparison and change how its outputs are combined. An additional layer compares the product of the three stabilizer signs. The first accepted errors giving an incorrect output then require three faulty input blocks rather than two. As a result, the preparation error decreases cubically instead of quadratically, and the asymptotic raw-copy overhead is reduced. We also construct a preliminary sequence in which a processed block is paired with a fresh copy from the same noisy source. For independent inputs supported in the even-parity sector and ideal purification operations, this sequence establishes a sufficient distillability fidelity of 59.95 percent after free Gaussian twirling. At a common source fidelity, it reduces the expected cost of preparing an input for the recurrence by more than seven orders of magnitude relative to the balanced construction studied here. Finally, we examine imperfect purification operations in an effective syndrome-noise model. The additional layer leaves the leading operation-error coefficient unchanged, although a nonzero error floor remains.

### Paper [186]
Title: Sample Complexity for Generative Learning of Quantum Measurement Data
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38028
Authors: Shuhan Zhang, Yi-Zhuang You
Abstract: Generative learning of a quantum measurement distribution from finite data requires choosing a model capacity that balances representation accuracy against estimation cost. We develop a bias-variance framework that decomposes the expected population Kullback-Leibler error at model capacity $s$ and sample size $N$ as $\mathcal E(s,N)=B(s)+V(s,N)$, with representation bias $B$ and a variance term $V$ capturing the mean estimation error. In the well-sampled regime, $V(s,N)\simeq[NC(s)]^{-1}$, and exponential or algebraic laws for $B$ and $C$ yield four scaling templates for the optimal error, from inverse powers of $N$ to inverse powers of $\ln N$. For local autoregressive models, the context radius sets the model capacity, and $B$ is the summed conditional mutual information lost by truncating the context. For a smoothed histogram learner, we derive an exact finite-sample estimation cost that accounts for rarely observed contexts and recovers the parameter-count law in the well-sampled regime. For transverse-field Ising Born distributions, the independently evaluated bias and estimation cost predict both the optimal context radius and the optimal error. In the well-sampled regime, the estimation cost grows exponentially with context radius. Its competition with approximately exponential bias decay in the gapped regimes predicts power-law error decay in $N$, while algebraic bias decay at criticality predicts asymptotic inverse-power decay in $\ln N$. Bulk-site results are consistent with the corresponding finite-sample predictions. These results connect the structure of quantum measurement distributions to the model capacity justified by finite data.

### Paper [187]
Title: Nonequilibrium Thermodynamics of Semilinear Stochastic Partial Differential Equations
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38166
Authors: Zelin Liu, Hao Ge
Abstract: We develop a rigorous mathematical framework for the nonequilibrium thermodynamics of semilinear stochastic partial differential equations. The invariant Gaussian measure of the associated Ornstein-Uhlenbeck process, which need not be time-reversible, serves as the reference measure. We define thermodynamic quantities in terms of probability densities and derive the entropy balance equation. We obtain explicit expressions for the entropy production rate and prove that its vanishing in the stationary state is equivalent to time reversibility. Sample-path entropy production is identified with the logarithm of the Radon-Nikodym derivative of the forward path measure with respect to the time-reversed path measure and is shown to satisfy fluctuation theorems. For nonstationary systems, sample-path entropy production decomposes into housekeeping heat and free-energy dissipation, each satisfying an integral fluctuation theorem. Both have nonnegative expectations, and the mean free-energy dissipation equals the decrease in relative entropy with respect to the stationary distribution. The theory is applied to semilinear stochastic heat equations with spatially correlated noise, a random polymer model, a nonlinear model with a divergence-free drift, and two coupled stochastic heat equations in contact with heat baths at different temperatures.

### Paper [188]
Title: Drift-induced nonreciprocity in image-polariton-mediated dipole emission
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38348
Authors: Christian Nicolaisen Hansen, Eduardo J. C. Dias, Christos Tserkezis, Joel D. Cox
Abstract: Electrical current bias offers a magnet-free route to nonreciprocal light propagation at the nanoscale. Here, we theoretically explore drift-induced nonreciprocity in graphene heterostructures containing hexagonal boron nitride (hBN), where graphene plasmons interact with hBN phonon polaritons in the infrared. Both the random-phase approximation and the Boltzmann transport equation are used to characterize the nonlocal optical response of drift-biased graphene. We compare graphene on hBN with an image-polaritonic structure comprising graphene, a thin hBN spacer, and a gold substrate. Carrier drift produces pronounced directional shifts of the polariton dispersion, while coupling to hBN phonon polaritons strongly modifies the nonreciprocal response. In the image-polaritonic structure, the graphene polaritons become up to twice as confined while retaining a comparable Fizeau shift. The nonreciprocal polariton dispersion produces directional near-field emission and nonreciprocal dipole-dipole interactions, the latter quantified through the cross density of optical states, reaching a left-right dissymmetry of up to ~40%. Our results identify image polaritons in drift-biased graphene as a compact, electrically tunable platform for directional coupling between quantum emitters at the nanoscale.

### Paper [189]
Title: Capacity of the Hopfield-Hebbian dynamics with correlated patterns
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38437
Authors: Mihailo Stojnic
Abstract: Allowing for small fraction of errors in retrieved stored binary patterns, Hopfield-Hebbian neural network (NN) associative memory dynamics [14] achieves linearly scaled capacity. The famous proportionality constant $\alpha_c\approx 0.14$ is obtained in a large dimensional scenario as the ratio of the number of reliably stored patterns $m$ and the patterns' length $n$ under the assumption that uniformly randomly chosen binary patterns are \emph{independent} among themselves.
  While such an idealized scenario is now well understood, more practical scenarios with correlated/noisy datasets remaining challenging even for classical statistical mechanics replica approaches. We consider precisely such practical settings where each \emph{archetype} pattern is replaced with $M$ correlated \emph{examples} with parameter $r$ governing the quality of correlation. We develop a powerful Random Duality Theory (RDT) based framework to analyze correlated data and already on the first level of RDT lifting obtain capacity characterizations that remarkably well match behavior exhibited through simulated dynamics. Moreover, characterizations explicitly feature correlation parameters $M$ and $r$, which allows to precisely determine their effects on phase-transitioning behavior of the underlying dynamics.
  Our results are also a well-fitting complement to the existing literature. Namely, to circumvent challenging statistical mechanics analyses, an alternative combination of spectral and replica methods is considered in [4]. We confirm that [4]'s elegant spectral characterizations are excellent approximations in the low correlated regime. At the same time, we also close the gap in the regimes with higher correlation levels where spectral methods deviate from the practical dynamics performance.

### Paper [190]
Title: Exploring Fractional Quantum Hall States with Deep Learning: From Abelian to Non-Abelian Phases
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38533
Authors: Xiangyu Ma, Kehuang Chen, Yuntian Gu, Weiluo Ren, Ji Chen, Di Luo, Fengcheng Wu, Xiang Li
Abstract: Fractional quantum Hall systems host strongly correlated topological phases with potential applications in topological quantum computation, yet identifying their microscopic ground states remains challenging because competing phases are often nearly degenerate and Landau-level mixing is difficult to treat. Here, we introduce a symmetry-adapted neural-network wavefunction that describes Abelian and non-Abelian states while incorporating all Landau levels. The method accurately captures composite-fermion liquids and the emergence of Wigner crystallization at low filling. When the first Landau level is partially filled, finite-size overlaps and entanglement spectra provide evidence consistent with $\bar{2}^{k}1^{k+1}$-type parton phases at $\nu=4/3$ and $7/5$, while the $\nu=3/2$ state exhibits anti-Pfaffian-like entanglement structure. These results demonstrate that neural-network wavefunctions offer a flexible framework for studying competing topological orders in fractional quantum Hall systems.

### Paper [191]
Title: Nonlinear response and retarded three-point correlators of charge transport
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38571
Authors: Jin Hu
Abstract: We study nonlinear response of charge transport in relativistic kinetic theory within the relaxation time approximation. For a classical gas of massless charged particles coupled to an external $U(1)$ gauge field, we organize the Boltzmann equation perturbatively in the source and derive a recursive solution for the distribution function to arbitrary response order. At second order we analytically obtain fully retarded three-point correlators at generic frequencies and momenta, verify the Ward identities implied by charge conservation. We also discuss the general properties of these correlators, including their analytic structure, consistency limits, and relation to Schwinger--Keldysh effective theories of diffusive fluctuations.

### Paper [192]
Title: Conformal Spectrum of (Super) Chiral Ising and XY Models
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38614
Authors: Oleg Antipin, Jahmall Bersini, Jacob Hafjall, Giulia Muco, Francesco Sannino
Abstract: We determine the spectrum of scaling dimensions of composite operators in the critical Gross-Neveu-Yukawa and Nambu-Jona-Lasinio-Yukawa models through a next-to-leading order semiclassical computation. Their infrared fixed points belong to the chiral Ising and chiral XY universality classes, respectively. At next-to-leading order, we resolve the Lorentz structure of the operator spectrum. For the parameter values at which supersymmetry is expected to emerge in the infrared, we obtain a closed-form expression encoding the full spectrum within the semiclassical methodology. The resulting organization of operators into superconformal multiplets provides further evidence for emergent superconformal dynamics.

### Paper [193]
Title: Quantum Gate Engineering in Superconducting NISQ Platforms: Experimental Methods and Emerging Directions
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38635
Authors: Alessandro Sarno (Dipartimento di Fisica 'Ettore Pancini', Universit\`a degli Studi di Napoli 'Federico II', Via Cinthia, 80126, Napoli, IT), Viviana Stasino (Dipartimento di Fisica 'Ettore Pancini', Universit\`a degli Studi di Napoli 'Federico II', Via Cinthia, 80126, Napoli, IT), Antonio Pio Italiano (Dipartimento di Fisica 'Ettore Pancini', Universit\`a degli Studi di Napoli 'Federico II', Via Cinthia, 80126, Napoli, IT), Alessandra Pizza (Dipartimento di Fisica 'Ettore Pancini', Universit\`a degli Studi di Napoli 'Federico II', Via Cinthia, 80126, Napoli, IT), Carlo Cosenza (Dipartimento di Fisica 'Ettore Pancini', Universit\`a degli Studi di Napoli 'Federico II', Via Cinthia, 80126, Napoli, IT), Wladislav Guarino (Dipartimento di Fisica 'Ettore Pancini', Universit\`a degli Studi di Napoli 'Federico II', Via Cinthia, 80126, Napoli, IT), Domenico Montemurro (Dipartimento di Fisica 'Ettore Pancini', Universit\`a degli Studi di Napoli 'Federico II', Via Cinthia, 80126, Napoli, IT), Francesco Tafuri (Dipartimento di Fisica 'Ettore Pancini', Universit\`a degli Studi di Napoli 'Federico II', Via Cinthia, 80126, Napoli, IT), Davide Massarotti (Dipartimento di Ingegneria Elettrica e delle Tecnologie dell'Informazione, Universit\`a degli Studi di Napoli Federico II, Via Claudio 21, 80125, Napoli, IT), Halima Giovanna Ahmad (Dipartimento di Fisica 'Ettore Pancini', Universit\`a degli Studi di Napoli 'Federico II', Via Cinthia, 80126, Napoli, IT)
Abstract: This review aims to provide researchers, experimentalists, and quantum engineers with an updated and technically grounded perspective on the implementation of quantum gates in superconducting NISQ devices. The goal is to establish a comprehensive bridge between theoretical quantum circuit synthesis and its practical, experimental implementation, focusing on superconducting quantum technologies. We systematically examine the diverse landscape of superconducting qubits and processor architectures, ranging from conventional transmons to alternative and unconventional designs, evaluating their unique design trade-offs, connectivity constraints, device physics, and materials impact. Moving from theory to actual implementation, we detail how single- and two-qubit operations are synthesized via microwave-driven and flux-activated pulses. We here present our pulse engineering, wave shaping, calibration workflows, demonstrating how to bridge the gap between abstract gate models and real-world hardware limitations in the direction of a hardware-software co-design framework. To validate these operations, we choose benchmarking methodologies with the purpose to get the more efficient metric for the specific evaluation aspect, including truth tables and quantum process tomography. Therefore, we both discuss the theoretical concepts behind the gate synthesis and show experimental implementation of most fundamental gates by proposing a practical workflow.

### Paper [194]
Title: Bootstrapping Wilson lines in Critical Bosonic QED$_3$ Part 1: Large-$N$ endpoint correlators and CFT data
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38663
Authors: Jun Maeda, Toshiki Onagi, Soichiro Shimamori
Abstract: We study the conformal Wilson line with general charge $q$ in critical bosonic QED$_3$ with $N$ complex scalar flavors by combining diagrammatic four-point calculations with endpoint crossing. We formulate the endpoint crossing equations with the full $\mathrm{SU}(N)$ flavor structure, keeping positivity manifest. For arbitrary positive integer charge $q$ held fixed at large $N$, we obtain closed-form expressions for four-endpoint correlators and mixed correlators involving two bulk Hubbard--Stratonovich fields through first subleading order in $1/N$. These results determine the endpoint dimension, the defect $g$-function, and the dimensions and endpoint OPE coefficients of the lowest non-singlet Wilson-line primaries. Moreover, combining the diagrammatic correlator with endpoint crossing, somewhat surprisingly, we find agreement between the bulk and Wilson-line spectra in every non-singlet flavor channel through order $1/N$. At degenerate levels, this agreement concerns the OPE-weighted averages of anomalous dimensions probed by the endpoint correlator. For unit charge, we independently reproduce these averages from the known bulk higher-spin spectrum.

### Paper [195]
Title: The Effective Field Theory Origin of the Fuzzy Sphere Method
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38702
Authors: Mykola Dedushenko
Abstract: Fuzzy sphere Hamiltonians for (2+1)D conformal field theories are usually written directly on the lowest Landau level. We introduce a framework organizing them as descendants of local ``mother theories'' on $S^2\times\mathbb R$. We start with the example of Ising mother theory built from two charged Dirac fermions coupled to a real mediator $\phi$. Integrating out $\phi$ and the higher Landau levels produces the fuzzy Hamiltonian, while integrating out the fermions gives a conventional local $\phi$ effective field theory flowing to the Ising point. Mild modifications of the same theory reach the Gaussian point (tricritical route) and the Lee-Yang continuation. Exploring the space of mother theories, we next promote $\phi$ to a complex field $\Phi$ and construct a new example, whose descendant is an easy-plane ferromagnet on the fuzzy sphere. Its spontaneous symmetry breaking phase gives a pseudo-Goldstone relativistic scalar, similar to the model introduced by Y.-C. He, while its critical point is non-relativistic, providing the first example of $z=2$ scaling on the fuzzy sphere. Remarkably, it admits exact one-magnon solutions, and the $z=2$ critical point can be found analytically. We then outline a rich landscape of further applications of our approach.

### Paper [196]
Title: An Eigenvector-Continued Green's Function Method
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38780
Authors: Stefano Brolli, Carlo Barbieri
Abstract: Emulating the Green's function using reduced-order equations is challenging because of the amount of information it contains. This information includes the one-particle spectroscopy over the entire energy spectrum, the one-body density matrix, and the ground-state energy. Nevertheless, the computational speedup offered by emulators is essential for applications requiring repeated model evaluations, which are ubiquitous in many-body physics. These include parameter calibration, uncertainty quantification, phase-diagram mapping, and geometry optimization. In this Letter, we introduce a systematically improvable emulator based on eigenvector continuation and moment-matching. The resulting approximation, which we refer to as an ``eigenvector-continued Green's function,'' can be obtained by solving linear equations, enabling an efficient separation of the computational workload into offline and online stages.

### Paper [197]
Title: Generating Multipartite Entanglement via Structured-Bath Engineering
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38807
Authors: Baptiste Debecker, Philipp Westhoff, Visal So, Sebastian Paeckel, Guido Pagano, John Martin, Mattia Moroder, Fran\c{c}ois Damanet
Abstract: We present how to dissipatively prepare genuinely multipartite-entangled steady states with mutually independent local reservoirs by harnessing an Abelian strong symmetry and temporal bath correlations. In interacting Ising chains, we demonstrate that, for a broad class of system-environment couplings, flat baths exponentially erase Ising-axis correlations. Structured baths overcome this obstruction by converting temporal bath correlations into classical spatial correlations. Strong symmetry then promotes these correlations to the quantum domain, enabling global multipartite entanglement. Within weak-coupling theory, we obtain exact steady states despite the generator's nonquadratic nature: strong symmetry reduces their construction to a classical problem for arbitrary Ising couplings. This reduction reveals an exact connection between classical distinguishability and entanglement. We then show how to engineer the bath spectrum to increase the correlation length and enable the preparation of GHZ-like states from separable initial conditions. We test the mechanism beyond the analytical regime using exact Markovian embeddings and tensor-network simulations. Our approach is particularly suited to superconducting and trapped-ion platforms, and we propose a concrete implementation for the latter using sympathetically cooled motional modes.

### Paper [198]
Title: From Entanglement Bootstrap to DHR Theory
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38821
Authors: Kohtaro Kato, Yoshiko Ogata
Abstract: We establish a direct connection between the entanglement bootstrap and the Doplicher--Haag--Roberts theory of superselection sectors for two-dimensional quantum spin systems. Under the entanglement-bootstrap assumptions, we prove Haag duality and establish a one-to-one correspondence between simple entanglement-bootstrap anyon types and irreducible superselection sectors. For each fusion channel, we further construct an affine bijection from density matrices on the entanglement-bootstrap fusion space to normalized positive intertwiners in the corresponding operator-algebraic fusion channel. These results connect the entropic and operator-algebraic descriptions of anyons directly, without passing through exactly solvable lattice models.

### Paper [199]
Title: Universality in randomly connected neural networks: old and new on a conjecture of Geman and Hwang
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38893
Authors: Jonathan Touboul
Abstract: Randomly connected networks are ubiquitous in computer science, physics or biology. A central question is whether, and how, their collective dynamics depend on the choice of the distribution of the random couplings. Forty years ago, Geman and Hwang formulated a precise version of this \emph{universality} question for randomly connected neural networks. We come back to these conjectures, review what was proven since, provide a few new extensions and propose new conjectures in this paper. For $n$ neuron networks with couplings with a mean scaling as $1/n$ and light enough tails, two classes of limits are expected. For \emph{weakly heterogeneous} couplings, with variance $o(1/n)$, the network is predicted to converge to a McKean--Vlasov equation, and a broad literature has established this under general assumptions. For \emph{strongly heterogeneous} couplings, with variance of order $1/n$, the limit is a non-Markovian equation driven by a self-consistent Gaussian process. After landmark results for Gaussian couplings, universality was only recently established for linear interactions, while the continuous-time nonlinear case relevant to neuroscience remained open. Here, in the weakly heterogeneous regime, we prove quenched convergence on every time interval under a fourth-moment assumption only, and convergence in probability under an assumption slightly stronger than a second moment. In the strongly heterogeneous regime, we prove convergence for nonlinear interactions in continuous time, averaged over sub-Gaussian couplings and on every time interval, by a relative-entropy comparison with Gaussian couplings. We then extend the conjecture to heavy-tailed couplings, and propose a limit driven by a self-consistent $\alpha$-stable process, for which we prove existence for every $\alpha\in(0,2)$ and uniqueness for $\alpha\in[1,2)$, and provide extensive numerical evidence in all three cases.

### Paper [200]
Title: Generalized Symmetries of QED$_4$ on Non-Spin Manifolds
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38907
Authors: Michele Del Zotto, Vivek Saxena
Abstract: Generalized symmetries are sensitive to the tangential structure of spacetime. We study this interplay for massless QED on oriented non-spin four-manifolds, where the photon becomes a spin$^c$ connection and the path integral sums over spin$^c$ structures. The shifted flux quantization extends the magnetic 1-form symmetry into a $w_2$-twisted 1-form symmetry: its $2\pi$ rotation acts as a gravitational sign, fixed by the normal bundle of the surface the operator wraps. The obstruction to treating it as an ordinary magnetic 1-form symmetry is an order-two mixed magnetic-gravitational anomaly, which is classified by reduced oriented bordism and detected on the Wu manifold. In a fixed spin$^c$ sector, this anomaly is removable by a counterterm, so it is a property of the sum over spin$^c$ structures. We discuss its consequences for gapped, Coulomb, and Higgs phases. The chiral anomaly is governed by the index of the spin$^c$ Dirac operator, so rational chiral defects carry gravitational as well as electromagnetic responses. A spin$^c$ Hall topological field theory cancels the electromagnetic part but leaves a framing anomaly. At the level of anomaly inflow, this is canceled for every rational angle by stacking a neutral, generally noninvertible Witten-Reshetikhin-Turaev layer. The same framing subtlety affects the chiral defects of QED on curved spin manifolds. The complex projective plane illustrates each of these features explicitly.

### Paper [201]
Title: A First-Order Phase Transition in Spin-Imbalanced BCS Theory of Superconductivity
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.38961
Authors: Marius Lemm, Barbara Roos
Abstract: We study a translation-invariant BCS functional of superconductivity for a system of spin-$1/2$ fermions with spin-dependent chemical potentials, $\mu_+\neq\mu_-$. Numerical work of Caldas and Mota and analytical work of Freiji, Hainzl, and Seiringer has suggested an unusual first-order phase transition in spin-imbalanced BCS models, in which the normal state becomes globally unstable while remaining locally stable. Here, we prove that a first-order phase transition occurs in such spin-imbalanced BCS functionals. For each sufficiently small fixed spin imbalance and sufficiently low temperature, there is a coexistence coupling $\lambda_{\mathrm{fo}}$ strictly below the linear-stability threshold $\lambda_{\mathrm{lin}}$ of the normal state at which the Cooper pair density exhibits a nonzero jump and the minimized energy has a kink. The methodological insight is a coercivity estimate obtained by decomposing the BCS energy minimization into two parts, where the ``inner'' problem minimizes over the one-particle density matrices at fixed pairing and can be exactly solved by a Legendre--Fenchel transform.

### Paper [202]
Title: Fate of Quantum Magic
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39059
Authors: Liuke Lyu, Erik S. S{\o}rensen, William Witczak-Krempa
Abstract: Magic, a quantum resource distinct from entanglement, measures departure from states stabilized by products of Pauli operators. Such stabilizer states can be maximally entangled yet admit efficient classical description: quantum advantage requires magic. We develop a powerful framework to evaluate the degree of magic in generic many-body states. We use it to study the fate of magic under thermal and dynamical evolution. In the transverse field Ising model, we find a thermal sudden death of magic that saturates with system size, and occurs after the death of entanglement. We reach similar conclusions in generic spin chains, and in the Kitaev quantum spin liquid on the honeycomb lattice, where the threshold shows an intricate dependence on subregion shape. We then study how magic equilibrates after a quantum quench. In quenches of generic spin chains, the magic of a subregion typically dies before entanglement, contrary to thermal trajectories. An explanation is given in terms of the geometry of non-magical states. As a third type of trajectory, we examine the fate of magic with separation with an appropriate notion of non-local magic and establish a sudden death. Finally, we show how to experimentally witness a volume law of frame-robust magic in quantum materials by using neutron scattering.

### Paper [203]
Title: virasoro_blocks: Software for the 2d bootstrap
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39106
Authors: David Poland
Abstract: We introduce virasoro_blocks, software that computes Virasoro conformal blocks at any central charge in a form suitable for the numerical bootstrap. At each finite order in the elliptic nome, Zamolodchikov's recursion gives the block as a positive prefactor times a rational function of the exchanged weight. However, its many Kac poles make it difficult to use in semidefinite programming approaches to the bootstrap. In this software we use optimal interpolation to reduce the number of poles while keeping high accuracy. The software works in arbitrary precision and handles singular central charges, including the minimal models and $c=1$. It also computes global blocks, $N=1$ superconformal blocks, torus characters, and torus one-point blocks. We illustrate it with two applications. Assuming only scalar Virasoro primaries, we compute bounds on the scalar gap that approach the window of Liouville solutions between $(c-1)/16$ and $(c-1)/12$. We also bound the average exchanged dimension in four-point functions of identical scalars at several central charges. These bounds have a rich structure, and many known 2d CFTs lie on or near their boundary.

### Paper [204]
Title: Resonance and Transition-Channel Competition in Cavity-Assisted Floquet-State Stabilization
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39169
Authors: Zhuocheng Ma, Hongzheng Zhao, Liang-You Peng
Abstract: Static coupling to a dissipative cavity has been proposed as a route to autonomously stabilize Floquet eigenstates. The cavity provides an energy-selective relaxation channel: when its photon energy matches a quasienergy difference modulo drive quanta, it mediates transitions between Floquet states, followed by irreversible photon loss. Here, we show that at intermediate driving frequencies, this resonance condition alone is not sufficient for stabilization. Instead, the steady state is controlled by the interplay between resonance selection and the cavity-induced transition strengths between Floquet states, which can either cooperate or compete. This interplay leads to successful stabilization, mixed steady states despite resonance, and complete breakdown near Floquet-zone hybridization. We further show that the same mechanism extends to multilevel spin-S systems, providing a general framework for understanding when static dissipation can reliably stabilize driven quantum states.

### Paper [205]
Title: Position- and Temperature-Dependent Responsivity in a KID-Based Phonon-Mediated Detector
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.39338
Authors: Z. J. Smith, O. Wen, S. Q. Dang, E. Panner, J. Anczarski, T. Aralis, T. Dyson, S. W. Henderson, H. Magoon, A. Simchony, C. Stone-Whitehead, N. Tabassum, B. A. Young, M. Munaf, D. J.
